## Delivery sequence

| Phase | Deliverable | Exit gate |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1. Reliable foundations | Repeatable PostgreSQL setup, trustworthy tests, explicit data ownership | Core schema and isolation checks pass without seeded personal data |
| 2. VAP visual system ("Plano") | Plano shell, themes, navigation, reusable components and templates | User visual approval and passing suites |
| 3. Core laboratory journey | Shared customer → private proposal → sample → analysis → report | Complete two-laboratory workflow passes end to end |
| 4. Network stock visibility | Searchable network materials and approved indicators | Useful overview with no private-data leakage or implicit write access |
| 5. Supporting modules and simplification | Remaining quality, equipment, commercial, and administration flows | Each retained module has tested workflows and the agreed UI |
| 6. Release readiness | Regression, security, performance, recovery, and handover | Full verification and explicit deployment approval |

Phases are historical acceptance milestones, not permission to continue automatically. The frozen eight-gate contract governs Phase 1; after verified publication Codex stops. Claude Code handles Phase 2 outside Codex. Phase 3 needs a later explicit user request.

## Phase 1 — Reliable foundations

Current phase. Finish the bounded foundation work before expanding features.

- Separate test failures into fixture dependencies, missing schema, PostgreSQL incompatibilities, and real business defects. Replace implicit fixture assumptions with isolated factories or explicit test setup.
- Verify the intended PostgreSQL schema with fresh migrations on a disposable test database. Test migrations that affect actual current records and preserve their values; test rollback where meaningful. Do not retain unused schema or upgrade paths solely for hypothetical legacy deployments. Prepare any migration consolidation or schema baseline for review before removing applied history or changing existing databases.
- Audit laboratory ownership across routes, validation, relationships, lookups, exports, queued work, and cache keys. Cover direct identifier access, bulk actions, laboratory switching, deleted records, and unauthorized membership.
- Verify sequence concurrency, rollback behavior, and laboratory/year boundaries. Keep database constraints and transactions aligned with application rules.

Exit: the core customer/proposal/catalog/sample path works on PostgreSQL, isolation regressions pass, and every remaining suite failure has a known category. Unresolved failures remain in the backlog and block final release.

### Current foundation evidence

- The specimen register and code lookup now require fresh active/verified direct laboratory membership and `view_samples`. Network summary access, a forged active-lab selection, archived/unassigned lineage, and inconsistent customer/site/subject ownership do not expose peer specimens. Root, specimen, and accession-code searches remain grouped inside the owned query; PostgreSQL accession-code sorting and exact date filtering no longer use nonexistent aliases or string functions on timestamps. Bounded flat positive parameter lists replace silent flattening/truncation, and parameter filters use the issued intake snapshot for the specimen's own profile/code, not today's catalogue. Six unused specimen mutation routes and their controller redirects are removed; resource links point to canonical intake. Soft archiving a specimen no longer deletes its analyses or permanently erases result evidence.
- Issued analytical scope now drives queued insert/verify/approve jobs, their request validation, entry and review forms, analysis-workflow counts/audit, and pending-work exports. The intake snapshot includes formula controls, qualitative status, reference labels, and limits; later catalogue additions, detachment, or renaming do not silently change issued work. Incomplete issued definitions fail closed, client-supplied limits cannot replace stored reference evidence, and results from another analysis on the same specimen do not enter the local workflow summary. The result-mutation file passes **118 tests with 725 assertions**; analysis reads pass **24 tests with 352 assertions**; export checks pass **8 tests with 167 assertions**. The unused MySQL connection definition was removed from Laravel configuration; this does not delete a MySQL server or database.
- Integration connectors now have one required owning laboratory, assigned from the active direct membership and immutable through the ordinary form. The hub, direct management/review/retry URLs, inbound sample matching, and outbound result publishing are restricted to that laboratory. Issued parameter codes and scope govern matching and publication; late catalogue additions do not enter delivered results. Imports use the canonical result action, preserving its permission, qualification, stage, equipment, and lineage checks. Reject/import decisions lock the transmission; the delivery worker rechecks source ownership before sending. Manual delivery retry now locks and claims only failed/retrying records, queues after commit, and leaves pending/delivered records unchanged; stale jobs do not reopen delivered or terminal-failed deliveries. A dedicated four-process PostgreSQL retry test proves one claim and one queued job. The Integration Hub file passes **23 tests with 164 assertions**; the concurrency file passes **one test with 11 assertions**. The current six-file affected regression passes **37 tests with 244 assertions**. Development and test databases received the additive connector-owner migration without resets. Concurrent outbound HTTP execution, remote idempotency, and broader route/notification audits remain Phase 1 gates.
- This specimen slice passes **62 focused cases**. The latest related regression passes **407 tests with 3,818 assertions**, covering canonical intake entrypoints, laboratory sample access, PostgreSQL foundations, write smoke, lifecycle, analysis reads, result workers, counter-analysis access, collection corrections, and programmed placement. Pint and diff checks pass. No frontend layout or assets changed in this slice; these are programmatic HTTP/data checks, not browser, whole-suite, or Phase 2 visual acceptance.
- Worksheet ownership now has explicit laboratory and unique analysis foreign keys. Draft generation, manual creation, cell/name corrections, archive/restore, list/lookup/direct reads, analysis brief links, and related audit reads/downloads use fresh direct membership and permissions. Canonical analytical lineage must agree with stored JSON; missing, archived, ambiguous, or foreign lineage fails closed. Names/cells remain editable, while laboratory, original author, analytical identifiers, and server-issued scope are immutable through ordinary model updates. Archive/restore use atomic POST actions; retries preserve content/timestamps and do not repeat audit entries. Five permissions are registered idempotently without implicitly granting them to users or roles.
- Worksheet drafts use the issued intake parameter snapshot, not today's profile catalogue. New intake snapshots also record calculation mode and profile-specific unit/reference values. Catalogue edits cannot substitute an issued parameter, label, code, or recorded setting; unrecorded older settings remain blank rather than being inferred from today's catalogue. Missing/invalid issued scope blocks generation without altering retained data. Foreign-lineage and outside-scope results cannot populate the draft or inflate its completion count, and zero values remain intact. These checks do not change the pending scientific insertion/verification/approval rules; other catalogue-dependent result/controller/export consumers still need the same issued-scope audit.
- A shared workbook validation contract serves both Form Requests and internal save actions. It accepts only editable names/sheets/cells, enforces dense bounded grids, and rejects oversized sheets/rows/columns or more than 50,000 cells before wildcard validation expands the input. Cancelled model writes roll back entire batches and their audit records. A stale request captured by the shared active-laboratory resolver was removed; switching laboratories now resolves each current request separately.
- The worksheet slice passes **105 checks with 870 assertions**: 92 ownership/action/read/audit/model/validation cases, four migration/permission cases, and nine real four-process generation/archive/restore and membership/permission-revocation cases. The concurrency tests commit to the dedicated test database, preserve sample/accession/analysis attributes and sequence counters, and remove only their own temporary fixtures. Test-harness cleanup issues found during the first run were repaired, and its identified failed fixture was removed only from `lims_unleashed_test`; no development records were reset. The latest combined affected-core run passes **334 tests with 2,681 assertions**, including this slice, issued intake corrections, shared creation boundaries, analysis reads, and write smoke. **19 focused frontend checks pass**, including native Inertia draft isolation and real compiled read-only/pending/error/empty/recovery states. The production build passes with the existing large-bundle warning; Pint and diff checks pass. This is not a refreshed whole-suite or visual acceptance result.
- Fresh disposable PostgreSQL replay now passes **137 migrations**, including worksheet ownership, with empty-database rollback/reapply of the latest migration. Retained worksheets cause rollback to refuse ownership removal, and unassigned records cause the forward migration to refuse guessed ownership. The verified development worksheets table was empty when the additive migration was applied. The disposable database was removed after replay; MySQL and backups remain untouched.
- The current clean PostgreSQL replay passes **138 migrations** and creates **173 tables**. On its isolated empty database, the connector-owner migration rolled back and reapplied; its required `lab_id` and foreign key were verified. The disposable database was removed. This is an empty-schema replay, not a retained-data upgrade rehearsal or project-wide test result.
- An earlier isolated PostgreSQL replay passed **139 migrations** and created **173 tables**. The procurement-pricing migration's seven columns and generated line total were verified; empty-database rollback removed the seven columns, and reapply restored them. The disposable database was removed. Current supplier/order/detail tables were checked empty before removing the legacy receipt inference. This does not establish a retained-data upgrade or make the missing QMS schema valid.
- Purchase-order creation and update now reject supplier blocks before opening a transaction, limit editable lines to the routed order, reject duplicate, malformed, or soft-deleted line IDs, validate unit prices to the database's four-decimal scale, and derive header totals from persisted generated line totals. Eight focused regression tests pass with 44 assertions; two procurement schema/rollback tests pass with eight assertions. The full-suite snapshot above remains red for the classified fixture and QMS gaps.
- After the original whole-suite snapshot, the five procurement-need workflow tests stopped relying on seeded administrators, catalogue items, suppliers, departments, warehouses, or labs. They now verify the active operational-notification contract and all five pass with 42 assertions. Combined with the purchase-order integrity file, 13 tests pass with 86 assertions. The refreshed whole-suite result appears above.
- Supplier-assessment notifications no longer throw when a clean database has not seeded `view_isuppliers`/`view_iorders`. Active verified administrators and users with direct or role-granted permissions remain eligible; the sender and inactive users are excluded. Three isolated notification tests pass with six assertions. The refreshed whole-suite result above includes this repair.
- Latest focused foundation regression: 160 tests passed, 1,554 assertions (proposal workflow/isolation, lab-code access, sample access/numbering/concurrency, PostgreSQL repairs, profile configuration, export ownership, laboratory notifications and their administration). Subsequent retention-job regression: two tests passed, four assertions. Additional intake regression: eight tests passed, 107 assertions. These are targeted runs, not a new whole-suite result. Pint and `git diff --check` pass.
- Latest canonical collection regression: 169 tests passed, 1,673 assertions across collection corrections/archive/read/export scope, programmed placement, lifecycle integrity, canonical intake entrypoints, write smoke, laboratory notifications, and sample access. Fixtures are local and tests cover retries, stale authorization, mixed-laboratory batches, rollback, preserved analytical evidence, and stored signature files. Ten frontend mutation/compilation tests and two collection design-contract tests passed at this checkpoint. Production build, Pint, and `git diff --check` pass. No development records or databases were reset. This initial browser run reached only the login screen; subsequent authenticated functional checks are recorded below. Screenshot matching remains a separate Phase 2 gate. Independent concurrency/commit checks remain separate gates.
- Authorized local demo access: `app:demo-laboratory` creates a verified non-administrator reviewer, two isolated demo laboratories, two shared customers/sites, two laboratory-private draft proposals, and four canonical direct/programmed intakes with eight pending analyses. Existing administrator credentials, VAP Lab, settings, databases, and backups remain untouched. Passwords are generated, displayed only on first creation, and never reset on reruns. Non-local environments, occupied identifiers, and revoked demo access fail closed; transactional failure rolls back the entire dataset. The new fixture suite passes 16 tests with 117 assertions; combined with collection correction and canonical entrypoint tests, 54 tests pass with 775 assertions. No notifications, mail, or jobs are dispatched by fixture creation.
- Latest canonical intake hardening regression: **180 tests passed, 1,583 assertions** across intake corrections, synchronization and independent synchronization concurrency, numbering, entrypoints, collection corrections/archive, demo data, sample access, and customer-note schema. **25 focused frontend tests pass**, including executed watchers, draft isolation, offset/second preservation, numeric quantity serialization, actual shared-input error rendering, and Vue compilation. Production build, Pint, and `git diff --check` pass. This is a targeted result, not a new whole-suite snapshot.
- Subsequent individual affected-file runs pass **209 tests with 1,135 assertions**: shared creation boundaries (88), issued intake corrections (67), programmed placement (49), and commercial sample workflow (5). These runs cover single/manual/spreadsheet limits, malformed identifiers, duplicate explicit codes, trusted attribution, source aliases, portal-row projection and replay, immutable issued source evidence, and first-link graph integrity. The internal raw-material QC flow adds two passing tests with 92 assertions after replacing seeded customer/catalog/lab/admin assumptions with local fixtures. Its notification delivery is faked; separate real Reverb delivery remains unverified. The 25 focused frontend tests still pass.
- `SampleEntryValidation` now supplies one rule/attribute/compatibility contract for ordinary intake, manual batches, spreadsheet rows, and server-derived portal values. Only validated data enters the creation payload. Batch/import actors and times are server-stamped afterward. Invalid PostgreSQL identifier shapes return validation errors rather than reaching bigint queries, and duplicate explicit batch codes fail before writes. Fresh transactional access checks and sequential source-row replay rejection are covered; independent revocation and simultaneous HTTP creation races remain unproven.
- A portal row selector must identify an active request belonging to the selected customer/site, and both request aliases must agree. Original row evidence comes from the stored request, not submitted JSON. Nonempty selected-row catalogue/lot/quantity fields override header fields; empty row fields fall back to header values and then operator-confirmed intake values. Numeric quantities, including zero, become valid text. The effective catalogue and metadata are validated again before scope projection and numbering. Creation locks the source request and rejects a row already registered, including a duplicate later in the same manual batch; the transaction rolls back prior rows and number reservations. Later corrections preserve the issued source row even if the request is edited, while allowing an explicit operational lot correction. The lock/replay behavior still needs an independent-process concurrency test.
- All three creation entrypoints now recheck fresh active/verified laboratory membership and `add_samples` permission inside the write transaction. The source request is then loaded with a row lock and current customer/site/status checks. This narrows stale-request races but does not by itself prove a revocation-at-boundary or simultaneous-creation worker race; those remain verification gates.
- Issued analytical links cannot be replaced. The model permits only their first initialization against a complete live graph with unambiguous root/code ownership, matching laboratory and parent customer/site, original product/matrix/type continuity, distinct samples, and one live analysis/profile per sample. Archived or cross-code analyses, extra or missing links, duplicate IDs/profiles/codes, changed categories, and changed canonical product/matrix fail closed. This repairs initial programmed placement without adding an event bypass. Existing scope-selection fixtures now select before issuance; the commercial workflow fixture uses a site actually owned by its customer. No assertions were removed or weakened.
- `UpdateSampleEntry` now owns transactional corrections and rechecks fresh active/verified membership, permission, qualification, laboratory, root, site, and private proposal context. Form Request and model boundaries reject issued identity rewrites. Catalog changes do not recalculate an issued profile/parameter snapshot. Equivalent numeric-string IDs preserve the canonical stored snapshot instead of causing a server error. Ordinary corrections cannot forge quality-release decisions, archive evidence, batch attribution, or analytical links.
- Shared payload preparation preserves omitted nested metadata, issued links, custom retention/discard dates when their inputs are unchanged, and discarded retention state. Offset-bearing timestamps normalize to the existing application's storage timezone before persistence; unchanged UTC responses round-trip without an hour shift or loss of stored seconds. This is not a project-wide conversion to UTC storage. Explicit receipt/collection metadata corrections are mirrored between intake, accession, and the appropriate collection subject; collection dates also reach canonical analyses. Original submitted evidence is retained. Scientific stage rules are unchanged.
- Intake synchronization locks a fresh laboratory/root inside its own transaction, derives allowed profiles from the selected product/matrix/department rather than trusting client-resolved IDs, and validates typed ownership, unique root ownership, customer/site lineage, and live codes before replay. Invalid scope fails before writes; a failure halfway through graph creation rolls back records, activity, links, and sequence reservations. Four independent PHP workers holding the same stale intake commit one graph and one set of numbers, for both direct and programmed flows. This does not prove HTTP creation idempotency, every physical graph invariant, or all ownership/catalog-revocation races.
- Customer/custody notes now use a nullable text column consistent with the 2,000-character validation limit. The additive migration is applied to development and test databases without resets. Unicode values, repeated migration execution, safe short-value rollback, and refusal to truncate retained longer notes are tested. Both ordinary intake and accession corrections accept the same limit and reject longer values before writes.
- Counter-analysis foundation regression: 83 tests passed, 601 assertions across the new ownership suite and related notification/inbox/admin checks. Four focused frontend mutation tests pass; production build and Pint pass. The design-contract subset still has four existing failures (121 passed), consistent with the previously reported whole-frontend visual backlog. Browser interaction and screenshot matching were not verified in this slice.
- Result-mutation foundation regression: 183 tests passed, 1,313 assertions across result jobs/schema, counter-analysis ownership, notification ownership, and the repaired lifecycle approval test. Pint and `git diff --check` pass. This slice changes backend behavior only; it is not a new whole-suite or visual acceptance result.
- Lifecycle-integrity regression: all six tests now pass with 21 assertions after replacing seeded role, result, and collection prerequisites with local fixtures. Combined with the result-mutation suite, 109 tests pass with 686 assertions. The write-smoke file now passes all 49 tests with 550 assertions on local fixtures; it previously had 23 observed fixture-prerequisite failures. Four related inventory report/ledger files add seven passing tests and 108 assertions, for 56 passing tests and 658 assertions in this focused slice.
- Fresh PostgreSQL replay: all current **136 migrations** succeeded on a newly created disposable database with **173 public tables**; it was then removed. The newest customs-reference migration rolled back and reapplied on that empty database; the earlier collection-owner/catalog migrations also have a separate empty-database rollback/reapply check. Development and test databases were migrated additively, never reset. The canonical collection-owner constraint is deferrable but immediate by default, and permits at most one non-null owner across live and archived intakes. Two constraint tests and the eight affected ownership suites pass. Malformed duplicate-owner fixtures defer that named constraint only inside their rollback-only test transactions.
- Active catalog schema now includes equipment categories, phytosanitary products, and paid services, plus category links for inventory statuses and equipment items. Actual lookup records, inventory equipment relation, safe rollback refusal with retained data, and the paid-service create/update/validation workflow pass. The 78-test combobox file passes; its private proposal-agreement lookup now proves 403 without direct lab membership and 200 with it. This schema alignment does not imply the wider equipment, certificate, or quality modules are accepted.
- Public `/components` is intentionally absent, and its smoke assertion now verifies 404. Settings tests use a local verified admin and pass four tests with 28 assertions. Activity statistics group by PostgreSQL hour extraction; the focused endpoint passes 10 assertions. The split admin-page smoke identifies 11 passing cases and two failures in QMS/non-conformity pages because `v_non_conformities` and its action table are absent. Adding those tables without the laboratory-ownership design would expose a broader quality module prematurely, so this remains explicit later-phase work rather than a hidden fixture failure.
- Portal authentication now keeps the `portal` guard separate from staff laboratory context. Portal request trend uses PostgreSQL month grouping and seven distinct months. Portal-account login/activity timestamps are represented in the schema and model; a missing activity column previously left wrapped PostgreSQL requests in an aborted transaction after a superficially successful response. All 11 portal service tests pass with 95 assertions. The portal smoke file now passes eight tests with 56 assertions: six real PDF endpoints prove access to the account's own site and rejection of guests, another site of the same shared customer, an unrelated customer's site, and archived documents. These are retrieval fixtures for already-issued documents, created without financial issuance callbacks; financial signing is not accepted by this test. Contract-guide PDF generation also has its missing number-to-words import repaired. A fresh database replay includes both new portal timestamp columns. Global administrator notification routing for portal requests remains outside the approved laboratory-ownership contract and needs review before acceptance.
- Contract-guide creation now runs on its own administrator/customer/site/product fixtures. A sequence trait's post-insert identity synchronization permits an ordinary `created` callback to save the generated document number without allowing issued sequence/scope replacement. The allocator unit file passes 21 tests with 32 assertions. A nullable customs reference now matches the contract-guide item payload; repeated migration execution preserves it, and rollback refuses retained references. The contract-guide workflow file passes three tests with 15 assertions. These checks do not authorize changes to financial or scientific signing rules.
- Actual analytical sample and lab-code models no longer increment the allocator's assigned number a second time. New numbers match their reservation; explicit numbers are preserved; the next number follows retained archived evidence without rewriting it. Existing numbering scopes and display formats are unchanged. The PostgreSQL foundation file passes 11 tests with 89 assertions, including six model-level numbering cases that exposed the previous `2, 4` instead of `1, 2` defect.
- Portal intake concurrency now has 22 passing cases with 360 assertions. Four independent HTTP workers reach the post-validation access boundary together for both direct and programmed intake. One source row issues one graph and one set of numbers; duplicate submissions receive 422. Removed membership/permission, inactive or unverified operators, archived laboratories, revoked qualifications, and changed/archived/completed source records issue no identifiers. Qualification revocation preserves 403 across single, manual-batch, and spreadsheet intake; the creation-boundary file passes 91 tests with 438 assertions. Notification delivery is faked, so this does not prove external delivery or every mutation-window ownership race.
- Counter registration concurrency now has 13 passing cases with 197 assertions. Four independent PHP workers deserialize captured jobs and handle them against committed PostgreSQL fixtures. Replays from the same operator or four different operators reuse one counter, sample, and counter lab-code reservation. Revocation of membership, permission, active/verified status, laboratory availability, or live intake/accession/code/sample/analysis/result lineage at the access boundary produces no counter or sequence reservation. Test-owned records and activity are removed after each case, including failure cleanup. This verifies database replay and boundary rechecks, not the queue broker's uniqueness lock or real notification delivery.
- Post-numbering foundation checks pass **397 unique targeted tests with 2,491 assertions**, across actual model numbering, sequence allocator, canonical intake synchronization/creation, portal concurrency, counter concurrency/access, programmed placement, and result mutations. These are affected-file runs, not a refreshed whole-suite snapshot.
- Analysis reads now require fresh active/verified direct lab membership and the relevant permission. Lists, archive filters, search, lookups, workflow details, and worksheet summaries exclude peer operational records and archived or conflicting lineage. Main-lab network visibility does not grant operational access; explicit membership/switching selects one private list. PostgreSQL search uses actual identifiers/catalog names and a bound text date match; department/profile ordering uses correlated subqueries instead of missing table references. Reading approved results no longer writes completion dates. The new read file passes **22 tests with 321 assertions**.
- Unused manual analysis creation/identity-update routes, their request class, four unreachable ordinary/counter form pages, and the unregistered result-create method are removed. New analyses come only from canonical intake/placement; ordinary identity metadata corrections remain in intake. Analysis archive/restore now uses POST and a transactional action with current operator/lineage locks. Mixed-lab batches and malformed IDs fail before writes; retrying preserves dates and audit counts; exceptions or observer cancellations roll back the entire batch and activity. Issued identities, child records, and result evidence remain unchanged. The new mutation file passes **29 tests with 110 assertions**. Independent archive-window concurrency is still unverified.
- Analysis-specific backend checks plus write smoke/lifecycle regressions pass **106 tests with 1,012 assertions**; the related result-worker/counter regression also passes. **11 focused frontend tests pass**, covering POST payloads, repeat suppression, actual disabled/busy bulk rendering, error retention, completed/unknown-stage guards, and changed Vue compilation. Production build, Pint, and diff checks pass. The build still warns about existing large chunks. No authenticated Playwright session remains, so this slice has no new browser interaction or visual acceptance result.
- Report Studio and Label Studio now create their own administrator fixtures. Label Studio also creates its own equipment fixture and passes all 16 tests with 145 assertions. Report Studio's commercial rendering tests use explicit unsaved document payloads instead of arbitrary database rows; 77 tests pass, two optional-driver tests skip, and its executive export still fails at the absent QMS/non-conformity schema. These supporting modules are not Phase 1 acceptance claims.
- Added missing role/permission labels, collection-product location, analysis product ownership, and result product snapshots. Additive migrations are applied to development and the dedicated test database. Legacy-aware snapshot migrations intentionally do not drop operational values on rollback.
- Added missing result notes, verification status, calculation inputs/metadata, and insertion-method/time fields in one idempotent additive migration. Development and test databases are updated. Model/schema alignment, exact calculation literals, mixed legacy-shaped columns, repeated migration execution, rollback retention, and a fresh replay of the current chain are tested.
- Product-linked intake now creates collection, sample, and analysis records using valid enum values. Technician notification context includes sample identity, conditioning, parameter count, and workflow links. Direct recipients and permission-selected employee audiences for laboratory alerts now require active, verified membership of the source laboratory. Client-site notices additionally verify that the recipient still owns the sample's site/customer relationship.
- Sample, result, counter-analysis, retention, and ordinary laboratory event notifications resolve canonical source ownership rather than trusting legacy result collection identifiers or a global permission audience. Deleted sources/laboratories and archived foreign or unassigned ownership conflicts fail closed. Delivery rechecks membership and source ownership, including in the final separately queued broadcast job. Empty audiences or disabled templates do not consume notification deduplication windows.
- Stored laboratory alerts remain visible only to current direct laboratory members. This also applies to shared unread props, admin lists/details/searches, analytics, and CSV exports; a forged active-laboratory session does not grant membership. Issued notice ownership remains available for authorized historical review after source archiving. Legacy private notices without trustworthy laboratory context remain persisted but hidden. Notification-center JSON queries now cast the existing text payloads safely for PostgreSQL rather than calling MySQL JSON functions; no stored data was rewritten.
- Notification preference, template, inbox, and admin-flow fixtures no longer depend on seeded personal users or roles. Reminder jobs tolerate missing admin roles and process samples in bounded batches. Deleting an unaccessioned collection product no longer crashes on absent parent/code relationships.
- Counter-analysis registration now uses a Form Request and transactional action, capturing an explicit laboratory ID in the job and rechecking active/verified direct membership and permission at execution. Canonical sample/code/product ownership replaces stale result pointers. Live lineage, deleted sources, archived foreign/unassigned conflicts, missing legacy job context, and malformed same-laboratory links are covered. Record creation, request flags, and sequence reservations roll back together; notification dispatch is deferred until commit. Sequential retries preserve existing counters, including archived/completed records; orphan request flags can recover through normal dispatch. Independent-process registration and access-boundary revocation are now covered by the concurrency slice above. Real after-commit notification delivery remains unverified.
- Counter lists, search/lookups, direct edit/update, bulk delete/restore, and counter-result default data are laboratory-scoped. Result-request validation now requires a canonical local analysis or counter-analysis, not only a globally valid sample. Metadata edits cannot reassign issued analytical lineage or change a completed counter; reading completed results no longer finalizes the record. Delete/restore routes use POST, and their bulk/single-row clients expose busy/disabled states and suppress repeated clicks without changing the existing layout or motion. Other legacy GET mutation routes still require audit.
- All nine batch/individual result insert, verify, and approve jobs now capture laboratory/user IDs and delegate to a transactional action. Workers recheck fresh active/verified direct membership, permission, qualification, live canonical source lineage, assigned parameter coverage, and both submitted/persisted equipment readiness. Membership, operator, laboratory, root, sample/code/product, and existing result rows are locked. These tests cover stale state before execution and rollback, not every concurrent permission, qualification, or ownership-change race.
- Complete batch identifier/lineage checks precede writes. Results are matched by sample/parameter, duplicate parameters and malformed result identifiers fail closed, and stage whitelists prevent forged actors or changes to earlier-stage evidence. Actor IDs/names/times are server-stamped. Exact retries preserve dates, result/media counts, and activity entries, including operator renaming and a changed personal signature. Specification checks are recomputed from canonical stored units and limits; unrelated provenance survives review. The insertion parent/product identifier mix-up is removed, and counter completion clears only its requested source result flag. Individual approval waits for all assigned parameters and all associated analyses before completing the product/sample.
- Signature content, image MIME, size, and structure are validated before mutation. New files are staged without invoking destructive single-file replacement; old files remain until commit. Failed second-row writes and later outer transaction rollbacks retain old signatures and clean staged files. Temporary source files are removed even on failure. After-commit behavior is exercised through Laravel's transaction-wrapped test manager; independent worker/real database commit and concurrent replay checks remain required.
- Result-request normalization is consolidated instead of three duplicated selector maps. Scalar and selector IDs both work, approval notes and equipment references survive normalization, and the legacy individual endpoint validates exactly one row through the same ownership boundary. Ordinary and counter-analysis default-result data reject peer samples for every stage. The complete lifecycle-integrity file now uses self-contained local fixtures: six tests pass with 21 assertions. This removes its four observed seeded-role/result prerequisites and exposes its underlying assertions; other legacy fixture-dependent files remain.
- Laboratory/year numbering preserves existing codes and numbers, rejects laboratory reassignment, handles rollback without orphan counters, and issues distinct identifiers under parallel intake. Migration rollback restores the former constraint only when issued numbers do not overlap; otherwise it refuses without renumbering records.
- Analysis export lists, summaries, searches, and workbook downloads use the active laboratory and fail closed for ambiguous collection ownership, including archived foreign links. Network visibility and a forged lab switch do not expand operational access. Canonical sample-to-code ownership drives audit exports; department filters use their correct query aliases.
- Direct/programmed collection creation routes, pages, and duplicate processing jobs are removed outright. Canonical sample intake is the only creation path; obsolete receipt/contract/quote helper methods with no routes or callers are removed. Existing collection views and corrections remain, without compatibility redirects or replacement creation stubs.
- Collection reads, searches, statistics, corrections, labels, parameter sheets, and workbook downloads require local canonical intake ownership, the correct live collection subject, and matching customer/site lineage. Archived foreign/unassigned conflicts fail closed; mixed-laboratory exports reject the entire batch. Search OR conditions cannot escape laboratory scope, and completion statistics use the correct total denominator.
- A shared Form Request, transactional action, and flat resource replace the duplicated collection correction processes. Issued intake identity and commercial links cannot be reassigned through a correction. Only active, verified local operators are offered or accepted. Quantity, packaging, and collection-outcome metadata remain optional instead of imposing requirements absent from intake; scientific stage rules are not changed. Omitted fields retain their values and submitted selectors report field-level validation errors.
- Collection archive/restore uses POST and a single transactional action that archives or restores the collection-product and its canonical intake root together. It preserves parent collections, sample/code/analysis/result identifiers, analytical evidence, certificates, and stored signatures; it does not invoke the destructive legacy sample/result cascade. Mixed-laboratory batches fail before writes, partial failures roll back, and retries do not duplicate activity. Repeated intake synchronization does not recreate an archived graph or issue replacement identifiers. Other mutation routes and deletion hooks remain in the wider audit.
- Programmed placement now captures laboratory/operator IDs and delegates to a transactional action. It rechecks active verified membership, permission, typed collection ownership, live parent/subject/code, and requested profile/department compatibility at execution. Sequential retries preserve existing and archived samples. Partial writes roll back analyses, activity, and sequence reservations. The focused worker file passes 24 tests with 103 assertions; independent concurrent workers and real commit delivery remain unverified.
- Collection correction UI is edit-only and single-record, with read-only intake identity, local owner selection, explicit archive consequences, and disabled/busy safeguards. Better-ui and emil-design-eng guided clear task hierarchy and immediate high-frequency controls without new ornamental motion. Ten executable frontend mutation/compilation tests and two collection design-contract tests pass; production build passes. This is not browser or pixel-perfect acceptance.
- Inventory stock positions now derive item name/category from the item rather than writing two nonexistent duplicate columns. A new additive migration supplies the traceability fields used by stock transactions and reagent consumption. Consumption creates its ledger type and linked transaction even without seed data. Inventory lookup, cost-based value report, analytics, stock adjustment, low-stock alert, reagent expiry, transfer, and ledger tests pass on local fixtures. The previous hardcoded per-unit valuation and PostgreSQL-incompatible month/date functions were removed in the touched report paths; other legacy inventory report calculations and network ownership remain to audit.
- Commercial-document create, sharing/delivery, and invoice-payment tests now create their own verified users and invoices; they no longer alter every invoice to establish a filter fixture. Along with the procurement-order read check, four affected files pass 11 tests with 139 assertions. The document-generation smoke file now exposes three passing missing-document/collection checks and one explicit absent-QMS-schema failure instead of four seeded-admin prerequisites; it still skips most document downloads when no record exists, so this is not PDF coverage. The broad LIMS smoke fixture was subsequently made transactional and local, as recorded below.
- Procurement order register/detail pages now tolerate the absent non-conformity table without reporting a false zero: the index shows an unavailable indicator, the detail chart labels that signal unavailable, and the optional receiving NC control is disabled. A forced NC submission receives a field error before stock writes. This is a temporary integration boundary, not QMS acceptance; the later non-conformity schema still needs laboratory ownership and the procurement lookup must be scoped before that table is introduced. Better-ui and emil-design-eng guided the state distinction without a new animation or layout direction. The affected PHP test passes three cases with 45 assertions and the production frontend build passes; browser/state and pixel comparison are not verified.
- Controlled-document upload exposed a PostgreSQL schema mismatch: `v_files.content` and `v_file_versions.content` held filesystem paths in binary columns, returning streams that broke JSON responses and downloads. A guarded migration converts both to text while preserving UTF-8 paths. Documents and their tags now have required laboratory owners; empty local development/test tables received both additive owner migrations, while retained unassigned rows fail closed instead of receiving a guessed owner. File lists/direct reads, mutations, shares, tags, workflow tasks, and text-version comparison are restricted to the active direct-member laboratory. Metadata resources no longer reveal storage paths. Text comparison reads only two authorized revisions of one file, checks type/size/encoding, and does not cache the response. Parent validation rejects files as folders and folder cycles. The persistent browser store clears on user/lab switches, filters stale responses by the active lab, and keys remembered folders by user and lab. The affected five PHP files pass **16 tests with 146 assertions**; four focused frontend comparison/cache checks pass. This is bounded file-manager coverage, not the whole route/media audit or visual acceptance.
- The broad LIMS smoke file no longer depends on a seeded personal administrator. It creates a local admin/laboratory membership and explicit customer/sample records inside a database transaction. Its theme test relies on rollback instead of writing an absent original theme back into a non-null PostgreSQL column. Seven tests now pass with 51 assertions; four routes reach the known absent QMS/non-conformity schema and still fail. This focused run occurred after the refreshed whole-suite snapshot above, so it is not folded into that snapshot.
- The shared customer dossier now requires direct laboratory membership and shows only accepted proposals and sample counts/recent entries owned by the active laboratory. Customer identity and sites remain shared. Financial, portal, and document summaries without laboratory ownership are withheld rather than shown as misleading zeroes. The unused global executive dashboard export route, controller, and page are removed; the current workbench remains the dashboard. The customer access file passes two tests with 44 assertions, the retired-export test passes, two focused frontend contracts pass, and the production build passes. The existing ISO smoke test still stops at the absent `v_non_conformities` table before its route checks; this is not a new QMS acceptance result or Phase 2 visual review.
- The shared site dossier had the same cross-lab aggregate leak: finance, portal, collection, certificate, and document counts/charts/recent activity were calculated without laboratory ownership. Its detail response now requires direct active-lab membership and presents only canonical sample counts and recent entries for that lab, site, and customer lineage. Unowned aggregates and misleading zero-valued financial cards are removed. Portal credential set/reset routes additionally require edit permission and direct membership before validation; the password form's enabled state now matches the server's mixed-case/number/symbol rule. Malformed site identifiers return 404 without aborting PostgreSQL transactions. Two-lab switching, inconsistent lineage, allowed password change, revoked membership, and forbidden credential writes are covered by **4 tests with 71 assertions**. The site design contract and production build pass; this is functional privacy work, not Phase 2 pixel approval. Broader portal credential ownership remains a separate audit decision.
- Legacy controller and manual PDF smoke fixtures no longer assume seeded users or inventory: they create transactional local admin/lab/item/site prerequisites. Their focused files pass **4 tests with 11 assertions**. The media-library reference guided the attachment-route fixture; no media collection or conversion behavior changed.
- MFA and passkey registration-option tests now create their own verified internal user and portal customer/site rather than requiring seeded accounts. The two focused files pass **6 tests with 31 assertions**. This repairs test prerequisites only; it does not claim browser-verified 2FA or passkey ceremonies.
- Latest combined affected-file run: **40 PHP tests passed, 396 assertions** across shared customer/site dossiers, Label Studio and its migration/logo resolver, legacy controller, manual PDF, MFA, and passkey options. Two focused frontend contracts, production build, Pint, and `git diff --check` pass. This is not a refreshed whole-suite result or Phase 2 visual acceptance.
- Label Studio labels now require immutable active-laboratory ownership; custom templates belong to one laboratory, while read-only system presets are visible to all laboratories. Source lookup, label/template lists, direct URLs, PDFs, mutation routes, and template application enforce that boundary. Inventory sources require a local stocked position; sample and collection sources require local intake ownership. Template input and application allow only presentation fields, preventing nested ownership or attribution changes. The create screens show the active laboratory as fixed rather than offering a misleading cross-lab selector, with distinct loading, empty, and retry states. PDF logo rendering now embeds only bounded public PNG/JPEG files or raster data; remote URLs, private paths, and SVG are ignored. This project's ignored `public/storage` symlink had pointed at another app's storage; it was corrected to this app's public disk without changing storage contents. Development and test databases received an additive migration after confirming the relevant tables were empty; retained unassigned rows fail closed, and empty rollback/reapply is tested. A refreshed disposable PostgreSQL replay passes **143 migrations and 173 tables**, with non-null label ownership and the custom-template/system-preset constraint; the disposable database was removed. The two affected PHP files pass **22 tests with 225 assertions**; the logo resolver passes two unit tests with 14 assertions; the focused frontend contract and production build pass. Built-in presets are supported but none were seeded, so this does not claim a populated shared preset library or Phase 2 visual acceptance.
- Inventory warehouses now require an immutable laboratory owner. Transfers require their own owner and database-enforced same-lab source/destination, distinct warehouses, and positive quantity; active item/warehouse stock positions are unique and nonnegative. The canonical transfer action locks the route's warehouse/stock/transfer rows, reserves stock, records the ledger, makes bulk creation all-or-nothing, and permits one receive or cancel with rollback on failure. Warehouse and legacy stock lists, lookups, direct reads, form validation, edits, and bulk actions use the active direct-member laboratory; referenced warehouses and stock positions with balances or movement history cannot be archived. The old direct transfer form/write path and its unused page/request/resource are retired; legacy list/create/edit routes present the canonical workflow while legacy mutations return 410. Development and test databases were empty for these tables when the additive migration was applied. A new disposable PostgreSQL replay passes **144 migrations and 173 tables**; empty rollback/reapply of the latest migration succeeds, owner and composite/check constraints were verified, and the disposable database was removed. Focused transfer/network/Label Studio regression passes **37 tests with 411 assertions**; the 25 affected inventory/procurement/report fixture tests pass with 273 assertions, combobox smoke passes 78/298, and seven inventory write-smoke cases pass with 63 assertions. Production build, Pint, and diff checks pass. The broader inventory-item/order/analytics/report, transaction, batch, import, and background paths are not yet uniformly scoped; real concurrent transfer replay remains unverified. This does not claim Phase 4 network-stock acceptance or Phase 2 visual review.
- The inventory item pages now sum and show only the active laboratory's stock positions and associated movement rows; their warehouse choices are local. Item creation rejects peer warehouses, ignores forged creator IDs, and writes an opening-stock ledger entry. Catalog metadata edits no longer replace or delete balances; the edit form shows local positions read-only and directs users to the audited adjustment path. Adjustment locks the warehouse and stock row, checks batch ownership, rejects negative outcomes, permits a zero count with before/after audit detail, and rolls back fully. The older stock register now routes opening balances and quick +/- through that same ledger action; its ordinary edit changes thresholds only, and a generic decrement no longer mislabels a reagent correction as consumption. The edit upload path uses multipart method spoofing and file validation without sending saved document metadata again. The focused PHP files pass **7 tests with 126 assertions**, the inventory write smoke passes **six tests with 56 assertions**, transfer/ledger regression passes **13 tests with 115 assertions**, the frontend form contract passes **three tests**, and the production build passes. The user has now selected **lab-owned item/catalog records with read-only network search**, replacing the earlier shared-catalog working assumption. Both current PostgreSQL databases contain zero item and stock rows, so a fail-closed ownership migration is possible without reassigning retained records; item ownership, catalog CRUD/lookup/export, procurement/maintenance/import references, and cross-lab relational constraints remain the next Phase 1 inventory slice. This stock-only slice does not claim item-catalog isolation or Phase 4 network search.
- The later inventory slice makes catalog items laboratory-owned and network stock search read-only; same-lab database constraints protect stock, transfers, batches, ledger entries, and reagent consumption. Direct ledger/reagent/mobile mutation paths that bypassed stock control return 410. The user approved four-decimal quantities with an item-defined unit: stock, thresholds, transfers, consumption, batch balances, ledger, procurement needs, purchase orders, and deliveries now use `NUMERIC(18,4)` where quantity is stored. Canonical item creation requires a unit; issued stock prevents changing it. Both consumption entrypoints use one locking action; selected batches must belong to the exact stock position, and reversal restores both stock and batch. Batch-tagged adjustments move warehouse and batch balances together; untagged adjustment, consumption, and transfer cannot spend batch-reserved stock. Procurement approval, conversion, and receipt retain four decimals; approval and receipt cannot exceed their source balances. The inventory-value UI/CSV and procurement dashboards/PDFs use per-item units or line counts instead of adding unlike units. An isolated PostgreSQL replay passes **151 migrations and 173 tables**; fractional inventory, ledger, and procurement migrations have empty rollback/reapply and retained-record refusal coverage. The latest focused procurement/report files pass **24 tests with 216 assertions**; the subsequent need/order PDF files pass **10 tests with 115 assertions**. Three procurement/report frontend checks and the production build pass. No Phase 2 visual match is claimed.
- Frontend numbering no longer generates random identifiers before submission. Production build passes. Refreshed frontend suite: **228 passed, five failed** (233 tests). The five failures remain shared-control/table design contracts, shell/detail reference drift, and Portuguese-copy coverage; the controlled-file comparison/cache checks pass. These remain Phase 2 work, not visual approval.
- The missing quality schema is now present: non-conformities, complaints, management reviews, uncertainty sources, and environmental conditions have lab-owned paths. The four newer quality-record tables received required laboratory foreign keys after an empty-data check; their owner cannot change through ordinary model updates. Their lists, mutation validation, notifications, and QMS indicators use the active direct-member laboratory. A disposable PostgreSQL replay passed **155 migrations and 175 tables** before the later broadcast-log migration; quality migration rollback/reapply and ownership tests pass. At that checkpoint, ownership for older occurrences, proficiency tests, and ratings was still unverified; subsequent slices are recorded above.
- Account administration now creates users with a supplied strong confirmed password, joins them to the active lab, and restricts staff lookup and mutations to direct members. GET links no longer mutate account status, impersonation, or signature data; bulk archive/restore is atomic. The focused account boundary passes **7 tests with 63 assertions**. The user has now approved restricting shared account-wide changes to the account owner or system administrator according to capability; that implementation remains pending, so this is not a complete account-policy gate.
- The notification admin center is restricted to the active lab's recipients, records, statistics, and exports. Broadcast-log rows have required immutable lab owners, and new broadcast payloads record that owner. Unsupported scheduling and expiry are rejected; the UI no longer promises expiry. Stored lab-tagged alerts disappear from a user's inbox after membership removal, and lab-tagged operational delivery excludes peer staff; private quality, proposal, maintenance, inventory, procurement, and controlled-file producers now attach lab context. Other unowned notification sources remain an audit gate. At that notification-administration checkpoint, the whole PHP suite passed **1,494 tests with two skips**; notification-related affected files, migration tests, frontend contract, and production build also passed. Phase 2 pixel matching remains separate.

Still required before Phase 1 completion: finish the route/validation/relationship/lookup/export/background-job/cache ownership audit, including unowned finance metrics and user security fields shared across laboratories. Shared notification presets now use laboratory-owned overrides. Occurrence and rating paths now have focused owner, recipient, lookup, mutation, export, activity, statistics, and notification coverage; this is not a project-wide isolation claim. The earlier counted clean PostgreSQL replay passed 160 migrations and 175 tables. Current private-schema proposal concurrency and real-commit fixtures successfully replay all current migrations, including the nullable consent-log column; their schemas are removed. No database creation privilege or development-data reset is required. Migration cleanup must target the intended preproduction schema and actual retained data, not hypothetical legacy deployments. Concurrent transfer, result, placement, import, invitation/response, and outbound-delivery execution; remaining notification source/portal guards, shared-cache concurrency/crash recovery and external delivery; physical graph consistency; remaining issued-scope consumers; public media/token access; deletion hooks; and cache isolation remain explicit gates. Real root/nested rollback, enqueue failure recovery, fresh-worker sample delivery/revocation, and final generic staff broadcast suppression now have focused coverage. The whole-suite checkpoint above is not a Phase 1 completion claim.

Resolved bounded route-audit finding: older proposal and agreement archive/restore paths now use DELETE/PATCH; GET/HEAD cannot mutate them. Unused staff GET proposal acceptance/rejection and manual agreement create/edit paths are retired. Real archive/correction operations and consent evidence remain. Tests assert the retired endpoints cannot change records. This repairs the inspected proposal/agreement routes, not every state-changing route in the project.

Success clears selection; rejection retains identity and errors. | The visible target and write boundary must agree, including failed or uncertain requests. |

### Canonical proposal authoring and public confirmation — not visual redesign

Better-ui and emil-design-eng guided a bounded functional repair: explicit persistent decision text, one recovery link, escaped proposal identity, a semantic heading, and existing card/control/brand tokens. No new entrance animation, icon swap, layout system, dependency, or visual direction was introduced. Duplicate editors were removed under the user's approval, not restyled.
