### Canonical proposal mutation and PDF boundaries — not visual redesign

Canonical create/revise/send now use thin controllers, form requests, shared payload validation, and transactional actions. Fresh direct-member permissions are checked inside the transaction; revisions and sends lock the current proposal and reject stale state. Creation derives laboratory, owner, PENDING status, token, totals, and an unacknowledged agreement server-side. Revision preserves captured source identity and existing consent, archives each old item through a checked lifecycle write, inserts checked replacements, and records explicit audit evidence atomically. Model/audit vetoes fail closed. Reference validation runs again after the mutation, and the final source guard checks the original captured identity rather than adopting a write-hook change. Returned revision items are current, not cached archived rows.

The existing pricing precedence, two-decimal rounding, editable/sendable statuses, and generated expiry expression (`created_at::date + tolerance_days`) remain unchanged. No new acceptance, mandatory-positive-consent, revision-reopening, or scientific-stage policy is inferred. The canonical show page now exposes sending for REVISED as well as PENDING, matching the already permitted send transition. Other frontend redesign and complete failure-state affordances remain later work.

The shared PDF service renders fresh bytes outside the action's own write lock, then rechecks current access, source, state, and the loaded graph before publishing or returning. Both staff GET endpoints no longer write proposal PDF metadata or stored artifacts; the older endpoint retains its read/download activity evidence. Send writes a checked, unique attempt-owned path inside the transaction. Rollback cleanup is registered on every pending ancestor transaction because Laravel discards staged child rollback callbacks during an ancestor rollback. Old paths are removed only after real root commit, only inside the owning proposal directory, and only when no retained proposal still references them. Notifications also wait for root commit and recheck current source/status/artifact or item identity; postcommit notification/cleanup errors are reported without undoing or misreporting the committed mutation.

Focused verification: **56 action/request/UI-prop cases with 336 assertions**, including lifecycle/audit vetoes, forged totals/status/token/consent, malformed polymorphic sources, revoked membership/permissions, stale terminal states, render-time source/content changes, partial storage writes returning false or throwing, changed source identity during save, archived references during item writes, and all seven send-button states. **Six native root/nested transaction cases with 83 assertions** prove root and intermediate rollback cleanup, deferred send notification/old-PDF retirement, reported postcommit notification failure, and revision rollback preservation. **Seven additional four-worker PostgreSQL cases with 159 assertions** prove send/revision replay, competition with acceptance, and rotated-token/archive/expiry denial. These use dedicated test data and disposable schemas; development records, credentials, schema, and dependencies were not changed. Three affected list regressions exposed a removed Carbon import; it was restored and all three pass with 95 assertions. The later whole run passes all 304 affected cases with 2,697 assertions. Its only failure is a sandbox-restricted Chrome startup in an unrelated Report Studio end-to-end test; the unchanged isolated test passes outside the sandbox, as recorded above. This does not turn that original full invocation into a wholly green result.

Read-only Laravel review found no introduced blocking defect; its stale returned-item relation finding was corrected and tested. Pint and diff checks pass. Limits: shared source/catalog rows receive fresh checks, not locks against every independent concurrent edit; global settings/Studio defaults are not fingerprinted; physical cleanup cannot be guaranteed after process crashes or failed filesystem deletion. External delivery, durable retry/exactly-once delivery, complete live-browser error/keyboard/mobile walks, and Phase 2 visual comparison remain unverified. No new visual or motion recipe was introduced.

### Staff proposal payloads and canonical template authoring — not visual redesign

Proposal owners, template authors, and revision causers now expose only ID/name in reviewed payloads. Canonical items retain form/source/pricing fields and standard/unit labels, not raw item snapshots. The canonical revision preview retains reason and before/after totals/item counts; full audit evidence remains stored. Explicit revision events and the earlier description-only revision entries appear in history. Incomplete comparisons omit unavailable values without crashing or displaying invented numbers. Canonical edit now loads its displayed customer/site/department/owner labels; inactive selected templates remain selectable. Template management retains its intentional layout/export settings, while authoring options omit them.

Template activity rows use a separate summary resource: ID, proposal number, status, total, customer name, and the existing proposal-view capability. No decision token, source IDs, storage path, or authoring fields are included. Summary queries retain laboratory isolation; template permission is not silently changed into a proposal-view prerequisite. Proposal edit/send/archive and activity navigation controls reflect existing permissions. The older proposal detail view intentionally retains displayed historical `details`; this is not a claim that all stored metadata has been removed from every commercial screen.

Older template create/edit links redirect to the canonical editor. Their POST/PUT routes, request, standalone forms, and copied inline form are removed; Ziggy is regenerated. Retained shared-template listing/search and archive/restore require their corresponding existing permissions. Search returns only live ID/name results and is bounded. Archive uses DELETE; restore uses PATCH; GET/HEAD cannot mutate. Both canonical and retained archive endpoints share the same action: strict batch validation, ordered template locks, all-or-nothing lifecycle/audit writes, idempotent retry, fresh active-account/permission checks, and used-template checks before and after writes. The canonical used-template rule now includes every laboratory and archived proposal evidence, with generic denial and no peer counts. A canonical audit morph alias resolves retained template subjects. Development records, credentials, dependencies, and schema are unchanged.

| Severity | Location | Before | After | Why |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| HIGH, resolved | Proposal/template resources and controller props | Raw owners/authors/causers exposed authentication and personal metadata; revision previews expanded complete historic snapshots. | Explicit identity/item/audit resources and a minimal template activity resource. | A view needs its commercial evidence, not another user's credentials or unrelated metadata. |
| HIGH, resolved | Older template routes/controller and shared archive action | Duplicate authoring bypassed permission checks; GET delete/restore could partially mutate batches. | Canonical authoring redirects; DELETE/PATCH; guarded atomic archive/restore with checked writes. | One authoring path and truthful mutation semantics preserve evidence and recoverability. |
| MEDIUM, resolved | Canonical proposal edit and author/revision Vue previews | Identity labels were absent; deleted authors or incomplete comparison data could break rendering. | Required eager labels, author fallback, and guarded comparisons; real edit items round-trip through revision. | Missing historical data must stay readable without invented evidence. |
| MEDIUM, resolved | Proposal rows/show, template activity, retained template list | Controls advertised forbidden actions; stored paths were used as presence checks; copied forms offered redundant writes. | Existing-permission capability controls, presence booleans, and shared persistent archive feedback with frozen IDs/busy guards. | Controls and recovery cues must describe the actual permitted operation. |

Verification: staff payload checkpoint **175 tests / 1,629 assertions**, including **11 new payload cases / 356 assertions**; template workflow **26 cases / 122 assertions**. Cases cover explicit identity keys, pagination shape, preserved consent/IP/prices/editor settings, inactive selected templates, archived references, template-only viewers, full stored audits, retired writes, forbidden GET/HEAD mutations, live minimal search, missing/invalid batches, idempotent archive/restore, peer/archived proposal use, lifecycle/audit vetoes, stale permission state, post-write account deactivation, and late reference insertion rollback. Nineteen frontend checks execute actual confirmation handlers, native archive behavior/error recovery, compiled permission/document/author/revision states, retired file/route checks, and page compilation. Production build passes with the existing large-chunk warning. Read-only Laravel review found no remaining blocking defect in the inspected slice; review did not independently rerun tests.

Limits: no new authenticated browser, keyboard/focus-return, complete loading/empty/mobile/hover/press, physical-device, screenshot/reference comparison, or 10%-speed motion replay proof. The retained list reuses existing shared table/dialog/feedback styling; no new motion recipe or Phase 2 visual approval is claimed. Better-ui/Emil guided truthful capabilities, static recovery, identity fallback, and reuse of shared affordances. Ordinary transaction tests do not prove native create-versus-template-archive concurrency; template/FK locking and canonical creation revalidation need a dedicated two-connection check. Raw/internal writers are not a universal database invariant. Canonical template create/update/import/toggle hardening remains separate from this retirement/archive slice. Full external delivery and other Phase 1 acceptance gates remain open.

Approved finance preflight, 2026-10-01: read-only development inspection finds zero invoices, credit notes, receipts, payment allocations, and financial document lines. `invoice_receipt` is the allocation table, not a separate `receipt_items` table. Parent/source/allocation ownership must be checked together. Corrected audit finding: the ordinary allocation hook evaluates both balance expressions before updating, so it does not double-subtract. The separate “mark paid” controller does clear the balance before creating an allocation, whose hook then subtracts again. Receipt signing also runs before allocations exist. The finance workflow slice must use guarded balance updates and sign the completed receipt. No numbering or financial network indicator change is authorized by this ownership approval.

### Laboratory-owned finance — first verified boundary, not complete billing acceptance

The six inspected development finance tables were empty. The migration adds required laboratory owners to invoice/credit/receipt parents and their lines/allocations without guessing owners, deleting records, or resetting databases. Composite foreign keys enforce matching laboratory ownership for invoice/credit/receipt links and allocation/line parents, including event-free writes. Child parent IDs become required; model ownership and parent identity cannot be reassigned through ordinary edits. Up/down lock all six tables within a transaction before checking for retained records. An isolated minimal schema proves replay, safe refusal on retained history, and exclusion of a concurrent writer before the rollback check. This is not a new complete baseline replay result.

Staff finance routes require direct membership and the existing per-action module permission. Root and child model queries use the active laboratory; public portal reads retain their separate authenticated customer/site authorization. Registers and bounded live lookups hide peer documents. Financial exports and card summaries use the same owner; quote/trade/quality export joins do not expose another lab's invoice number. Known financial-subject activity details, lists, summaries, and exports require active-lab/source-module permission, including the configured custom activity model. New finance PDF activity is bound to its document. Owner resources expose only user ID/name. Unclassified subjectless historical logs, complete serializers, global settings, and non-finance source modules remain separate audit work.

Receipt creation and mark-paid share guarded actions with consistent operator-before-invoice lock order. Amounts use the existing two-decimal currency semantics, calculated in integer cents; this does not alter four-decimal procurement/material quantities. Client-supplied pending balances are ignored. Invoice/customer/site/lab consistency, cancelled invoices, zero/negative/excess/stale amounts, malformed batches, and duplicate invoices are rejected before writes. Balances and paid dates are updated once, lifecycle outcomes and explicit issued-audit persistence are checked, and receipt signing runs only after allocations exist. Numbering and its existing signature-chain grouping are unchanged; no legal signing compliance or changed financial policy is asserted. Receipt corrections now accept observation metadata rather than pretending to replace allocations through the previously empty update rules.

The payment mutation is POST-only; generated routes and its native Inertia form agree. Its controlled shared dialog stays open on validation/network/HTTP failure, disables repeat requests and changing the payment method while busy, and closes only on success. Better-ui/Emil guided truthful state/recovery and shared affordance reuse, not a new visual design or motion recipe. Two frontend cases execute the real submit handler, failures/retry/success and synchronous dispatch recovery, and compile the page. Queued finance document emails check the current active/verified sender, direct membership, source existence, and corresponding read permission both before PDF rendering and before sending; tests fake all mail and cover render-time revocation. Durable delivery ownership snapshots, post-check revocation/concurrency, external send/crash recovery, and other document types remain unverified.

Verification: **155 PHP cases / 967 assertions across ten affected files**, including **18 finance-boundary cases** and the updated delivery/portal/export/payment fixtures; **21 frontend cases**, production build (existing large-chunk warning), and diff checks pass. Read-only Laravel review's migration race, card-summary scope, audit-veto, and lock-order findings were fixed and tested. Development finance tables still contain zero records after verification. No credentials, dependencies, MySQL data/backups, issued identifiers, or financial network indicators changed. No whole-suite rerun after this finance slice, authenticated-browser acceptance, pixel comparison, native simultaneous payment execution, or complete finance/Phase 1 acceptance is claimed.

### Laboratory-owned billing sources and guarded certificate invoice links

Approved quote/import/export ownership is implemented on parent and item tables. Required laboratory owners and composite parent/invoice keys reject cross-lab raw writes. Up/down lock the source tables before checking retained records; migrations refuse ambiguous retained evidence rather than assigning an arbitrary owner. Isolated PostgreSQL schemas verify replay, retained-record refusal, and source-writer exclusion. The development source tables were empty before applying ownership; no retained documents or issued identifiers were rewritten.

Staff routes require direct laboratory membership and existing per-action permissions, including quote-item corrections. Registers, lookups, exports/card counts, and known root/child subject audits are owner-scoped. Source PDF and document-sharing audits now bind their subjects; user resources expose only identity fields. Source notifications carry laboratory context and only target eligible direct members. Queued source-document emails recheck live lab/sender/permission access before rendering and sending. Fresh financial/trade notification-worker module permission, unknown historical subjectless audits, durable delivery snapshots and external send/crash recovery remain separate gates.

Invoice issuance uses a shared transactional action: lock/revalidate the owning source and its canonical quote-item references, verify party/owner consistency, sanitize server-owned actor/balance/state/source fields, persist the invoice signature and lines, persist the source link, check explicit audit persistence, and recheck operator access. Replays and pre-existing links are rejected. Lifecycle save/signature/audit vetoes and source-party changes roll back issuance. Existing numbering/signature-chain grouping and two-decimal currency semantics are unchanged; native simultaneous issuance and complete financial calculation/legal compliance are not claimed. Certificate creation and invoice issuance now have distinct POST routes.

The approved certificate editor no longer sends invoice ID or billed-state fields; the server rejects them even when empty/false. Model mass assignment excludes both fields, existing links cannot be cleared/reassigned through ordinary eventful writes, and issued billing state cannot be manually reset. Existing archived invoice history remains readable/restorable. Invoice actions are visible only with the applicable permission and never offer issuance for an already-linked or historical billed certificate. Metadata corrections preserve the link. Financial editing follows the separately approved core-locking slice below; wider trade-certificate amendment policy remains separate.

New real HTTP creation tests exposed a pre-existing FK/catalog mismatch: both certificate item tables referenced `products`, while their models/pickers use `phytosanitary_products`. A focused migration aligns the keys under table locks, refuses retained non-null product links in either direction, and preserves unlinked rows. Even matching numeric IDs do not authorize reinterpretation. Replay and safe refusal are tested; no catalog rows or certificate records were deleted.

Whole-quote HTTP save and source-to-invoice tests also exposed missing `is_service` columns already assumed by quote, invoice and credit-note models/forms. Focused transactional migrations add the existing boolean pricing mode only after locking and confirming these tables are empty, and refuse retained records on rollback. Isolated PostgreSQL replay/refusal tests pass; development and test migrations were applied without resetting data. All twelve financial/source tables remained empty in the read-only development count check.

Quote-item corrections now use an action with checked save/audit results and fresh permission checks. A selected CL is sent as explicit `lab_code_id` and resolved to its canonical collection product under the quote's lab/customer/site; an unchanged selection is omitted. Picker results are private to the active directly joined laboratory. Distinct-ID selection, clearing, peer/permission denial, audit-veto rollback, and saving the whole quote after row correction are covered. The row button is non-submit; pending/error states remain local, failures keep draft data, and success synchronizes only the corrected source identity without resetting other drafts. The wider quote create/update process is not yet a fully guarded canonical workflow.

Better-ui/Emil functional-control review (not Phase 2 visual acceptance):

| Severity | Location | Before | After | Why |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| High | Certificate editor | Editable invoice picker/billed checkbox | Read-only persisted billing state and issuance guidance | One authoritative billing path; existing evidence preserved |
| High | Certificate detail | Unconditional invoice action | Permission-aware open/issue actions, no duplicate issuance affordance | Controls reflect actual permitted operations |
| High | Quote row correction | Ambiguous CL IDs; row button also submitted parent | Explicit scoped source selection, non-submit button, local pending/errors | Correct identity and recoverable isolated corrections |
| High | Financial editors | Authoring controls could replace issued parties, amounts and lines | Observation-only form with minimal resource and server core lock | The interface matches the approved correction boundary |
| Medium | Financial correction feedback | Unclear dispatch/connection failure recovery | Preserved text, inline failure, pending guard and accessible success state | Corrections remain recoverable without duplicate requests |
| High | Six billing registers; single and bulk archive controls | GET mutations, partial batches and a broken trade-certificate delete route | DELETE/PATCH through the shared archive form and one transactional action | Archive remains reversible and owner-scoped; failure does not leave a partial batch |
| Medium | Archive confirmation and feedback | Selection could change before submission; invoice bulk confirmation opened payment; failure recovery was unclear | Frozen IDs, distinct payment/archive dispatch, pending guards and existing accessible status/error feedback | The confirmed action is the action submitted, with visible recovery and no visual-system redesign |

Status: functional control changes approved by code/handler/compiler checks. No new motion recipe, authenticated-browser/focus/mobile proof, screenshot comparison, or pixel-perfect acceptance. Playwright found no reusable browser session; passwords were not reset. The full design-contract file also reports ten unrelated existing failures across shared controls/shell, inventory reports, sample/customer detail, and inventory delivery; the affected certificate contracts pass. These remain visible follow-up work rather than weakened assertions.

Source checkpoint: **125 PHP cases / 725 assertions** across ownership, PostgreSQL foundation and picker tests pass. Five actual form/handler/compiler cases and two affected certificate contracts pass; production build passes with the existing chunk-size warning. The completed source-era whole run recorded **1,884 passed, two failures, two expected skips / 15,884 assertions** (612.90 seconds). Its legacy certificate fixture lacked the new owner and was corrected, with the affected file passing afterward. The configured-Chrome test passes independently outside the filesystem sandbox (**one case / five assertions**); its sandbox startup failure was not hidden or weakened. The newer financial-core checkpoint is tracked below.

### Locked issued financial core and observation corrections

Ordinary invoice, credit-note, receipt and invoice-line HTTP corrections accept only observations; unexpected fields are rejected even when null. A dedicated action verifies current direct membership and module permission, locks the owned parent/record, checks the saved observation and unchanged core, records a subject-bound audit, and rechecks the record and operator before commit. Peer IDs fail closed. Save/audit vetoes and lifecycle mutations roll back the correction.

Eventful model writes reject changes to financial identity, parties, amounts, taxes, source links and line content. Signed line creation, deletion, force deletion, `deleted_at` updates and ordinary restoration are rejected. Initial signing is allowed once. Legitimate new invoice/credit-note line assembly uses a scoped capability registered by the actual created hook inside the issuance transaction; root commit, rollback and ancestor rollback expire that eligibility. Invoice settlement/cancellation permits only the specific state fields required by the existing workflows. These are trusted internal capabilities, not general editor flags. Cash-invoice status is initialized before signing; receipt payments still use the guarded allocation action.

The three old authoring-style edit pages are replaced by one observation-only Inertia `useForm` component and minimal API Resource (`id`, document number, observations). Optional/archived relations no longer need dereferencing just to correct text. Better-ui/Emil/Tailwind guide this functional simplification: existing theme-aware controls, labeled textarea, inline errors, preserved drafts, pending/duplicate-submit protection and accessible success feedback. No new animation, password reset, screenshot/pixel acceptance or new financial policy is inferred.

Verification: final **195-case / 1,495-assertion** affected regression across eleven files passes, including twenty-one core-lock cases. Coverage includes real cash-invoice and credit-note creation/signing/assembly, guarded payment regression, signed-line removal/restoration denial, root/child/grandchild transaction-context expiry, explicit post-audit core/observation mutation, and membership/permission/account revocation rollback. Native helper-context tests use real PostgreSQL commits/rollbacks on a separate connection without inserting financial records; they do not claim native simultaneous issuance. Nine actual frontend payload/handler/compiler tests and the affected commercial-editor contract pass; the full design-contract file records **117 passes / ten unchanged unrelated failures**. Production build, Pint and diff checks pass. Read-only Laravel review findings were fixed and tested. A fresh read-only development check confirms all twelve financial/source tables still have zero records. Raw SQL and quiet model writes bypass Eloquent events, so universal database-level content immutability is not claimed. Parent archive/restore policy and financial credit/cancellation semantics remain separate work.

### Atomic financial/source archive and restore

Invoices, credit notes, receipts, quotes, import certificates and export certificates now archive through DELETE and restore through PATCH. A bounded distinct-ID request and one class-allowlisted action replace the six unchecked loops. The action refreshes direct membership, active-account eligibility and module permission, locks all owned parents in ID order, requires exact batch cardinality before any writes, and locks/snapshots every retained line including historical archived children. Save/restore vetoes, incorrect persisted state, root/child mutations, missing or misattributed audits, and final authority revocation roll back the complete batch. End-of-batch checks catch later listeners altering earlier records or deleting earlier audit evidence. Repeated matching requests are no-ops and do not duplicate explicit archive audits or update timestamps.

This retains the existing archive policy for issued/paid/linked documents. Archive is not cancellation or refund: balances, allocations, signatures, identifiers, invoice links and line history remain unchanged. Restoring a source with an archived invoice/customer/site remains allowed; nothing silently unlinks or restores related records. No migration, development financial record creation, credential change, balance reversal, dependency change or financial-network disclosure was performed.

All six registers use the existing Inertia `useRecordArchive` form for both single-row and bulk mutations. Bulk confirmation snapshots the selected IDs, permissions govern available actions, pending work prevents duplicate mutation requests, and the existing feedback component announces pending/success/failure states and offers list refresh after uncertain outcomes. Trade certificates now resolve delete to the real `.destroy` route. Invoice archive confirmation no longer opens the payment dialog; payment uses its existing guarded POST form unchanged. Better-ui and Emil informed these functional recovery states, not a Phase 2 layout or animation redesign.

Verification: **262 PHP cases / 2,056 assertions** across thirteen affected files pass after Pint, including **twenty archive cases / 291 assertions**. Coverage includes all six HTTP route pairs, GET rejection, idempotent timestamps/audits, mixed-lab/missing batches, bounded IDs, lifecycle/audit vetoes, post-audit core/identity mutations, removal of earlier audit evidence, permission/membership/account revocation, archived historical children, retained settled balances and linked-source restoration. **Twenty-six actual frontend helper/handler/compiler/render checks** and five affected design contracts pass, including shared native Inertia failure recovery and invoice payment regression. The old trade-register contract asserted the broken interpolated `.delete` route; it now requires explicit `.destroy`/`.restore` URLs and shared handler/feedback wiring. Full design-contract failures recorded above remain unresolved, not hidden.

The native same-lab archive/payment/source-issuance cases below now pass; that does not establish every financial concurrency scenario. A user decision is pending on capping credits at unpaid balances versus implementing paid-invoice credits/refund balances now. Financial network indicators require separate approval.

### Owned and replay-resistant document delivery

PostgreSQL requires one laboratory owner on every delivery, with a restrictive foreign key and indexed source identity. The migration takes an exclusive table lock before checking retained evidence on both upgrade and rollback; nonempty unowned deliveries stop the migration rather than receiving guessed owners. The empty development and dedicated test tables were upgraded without resetting databases. Recipient/source instructions cannot change through ordinary model writes. Quality certificates retain their existing canonical sample ownership rather than receiving an invented independent laboratory field.

One transactional action authorizes the fresh active/verified directly joined sender using the existing document-view permission, locks/rechecks the owned source, persists the delivery and subject-bound audit, and checks persisted source/instruction/audit identity before root commit. Only the real root commit queues the mail job. Queue insertion exceptions remain reported and are recorded as pre-transport failure; root/nested rollbacks leave no delivery, queue job or queue audit. Arbitrary validated To/CC recipients remain allowed; no separate share permission or recipient policy was inferred.

Workers preserve primitive queued instructions independently of Eloquent model deserialization, recheck current access and the loaded rendered graph, and commit a durable UUID send claim before SMTP. Ambient transaction execution is rejected. Four independently bootstrapped competing PostgreSQL workers produce one local transport acceptance; replays skip claimed/terminal states. Successful delivery means Laravel returned a native transport-acceptance message, not recipient delivery confirmation. A cancelled mail operation, transport exception or completion-persistence failure cannot be marked sent. Ambiguous outcomes become `uncertain`, suppress automatic resending, and use the explicit “Envio por confirmar” notice rather than claiming non-delivery. Stale failure callbacks cannot overwrite sent state or another attempt's claim. Notification failures do not change confirmed mail state. Completion/failure/uncertainty notices carry the owning delivery/lab and recheck the exact sender, current module permission and private source before queued channel/final-broadcast delivery. Trade-certificate document links now use their actual `/show` routes.

Horizon configuration includes mail, notifications and broadcasts alongside existing queues, with the job/supervisor/retry chain ordered at 120/150/180 seconds. The active development queue driver is Redis, but the read-only status check reports Horizon inactive. No worker was started, external email sent, recipient credential changed, or dependency added. Live Redis/Horizon consumption, provider delivery receipts, crash-after-acceptance reconciliation, stale `sending` recovery, an explicit operator review/retry interface, settings/template/media fingerprinting and the final revocation-to-SMTP race remain separate gates. Exactly-once SMTP is not claimed. This backend slice does not alter Phase 2 visual scope.

Verification: the thirteen-file affected regression passes **221 cases / 2,130 assertions** in 97.02 seconds. After the final timeout-notice correction and independent-connection transport-boundary proof, the three delivery files pass **36 cases / 510 assertions** in 69.49 seconds; these overlap the earlier regression rather than representing 257 distinct tests. Coverage includes seven source types, incorrect owners and analytical result lineage, queue/audit/save vetoes and mutations, rendered parent/line/payment-type/result changes, fresh permission/membership/account/lab revocation, immutable queued instructions, native mail cancellation, SMTP ambiguity, post-acceptance persistence veto/mutation, notification broker failure, stale/foreign failure callbacks, replay suppression, real root/nested rollback and commit, database-job serialization/pop/fire, visible committed claims before transport, four competing fresh workers, and empty migration rollback/reapply plus retained-evidence/FK refusal. Tests use native PostgreSQL root transactions in per-test disposable schemas and local array transport; no external mail is sent. The final timeout fixture uses trusted `forceFill` for protected attempt fields, preserving the production mass-assignment restriction. Pint, diff checks and read-only Laravel review pass; no disposable delivery schemas remain. Development delivery and financial/source tables remain empty. The last whole-suite checkpoint remains the earlier recorded run; the ten unrelated visual-contract failures remain open.

### Billing issuance persistence and native financial concurrency

Quote/import/export invoice conversion compares server-derived creation intent with persisted headers and lines before adopting generated fields. It then verifies the entire retained invoice/source/line snapshots and the explicit audit's subject, actor, owner and invoice link after the final lifecycle/audit writes. Receipt issuance similarly checks intended header/allocation values, every affected invoice's planned settlement state, final retained allocations, and the complete explicit allocation audit. Decimal comparisons use existing two-decimal financial storage semantics; fractional inventory policy is unchanged. Early and late hook mutations, vetoes and missing evidence roll back the whole operation rather than becoming a new accepted baseline.

Invoice and receipt signature commands use transactional locked roots, checked saves and exact final content/hash checks; receipt signing also locks and verifies allocations. FR paid-date initialization may change only the intended date, with category selected by `FR` code rather than ID `2`. Existing signed records are no-ops. New issuance rejects prefilled hashes, including a receipt hash injected during allocation creation, rather than allowing idempotent signer behavior to bypass initial signing. New chains use the latest earlier nonempty issued hash, including archived predecessors, within the approved invoice year/type or receipt year group. Unsigned intervening records and other years/types are excluded. Existing signatures and numbering groups are preserved.

Twenty native PostgreSQL cases bootstrap independent workers in disposable schemas. Distinct users compete at a held laboratory lock, proving one invoice per quote/import/export source; exact full/partial/overpayment outcomes; reverse-order multi-invoice allocations; source archive-versus-issuance and invoice archive-versus-payment; and idempotent reverse-order archive/restore batches for all six financial/source classes. Real outer rollback restores source links and invoice balances while removing newly created financial parents, lines and explicit issued audits. These tests fake notifications: their zero-job assertions do not prove real financial notification commit behavior. Normal issuance holds the shared numbering-scope lock through root commit; standalone signing of multiple pre-existing unsigned documents is not a proven ordered repair workflow. Cross-lab shared numbering/signature concurrency, blocked authority revocation and credit concurrency remain separate gates.

Verification: the ten-file finance/delivery regression passes **174 cases / 2,338 assertions** in 153.30 seconds. After the final allocation-hook signature guard, the seven finance files pass **139 cases / 1,834 assertions** in 80.44 seconds; these overlap the prior run, not 313 distinct tests. The final finance set includes twenty-two lifecycle/signature boundary cases and twenty native concurrency/root-rollback cases. Empty signatures, save vetoes, changed core/hash/allocation content, early header/line/signature injection, post-audit corruption, archived predecessors and retained-signature no-ops are exercised. Pint, diff checks and read-only Laravel review pass. No development schema/data, credentials, dependencies, external mail, refund/cancellation policy or UI design changed. The signing algorithm/key configuration is unchanged; fiscal/legal compliance and universal raw-SQL immutability are not claimed. The earlier whole-suite checkpoint and ten unresolved visual-contract failures remain the recorded broader baseline. Phase 1 remains in progress.

### Trade authoring, billed-source locks and laboratory numbering

The user approved observation-only ordinary edits after quotes or import/export certificates are billed, and separate laboratory/year sequences for newly created certificates. New certificate identifiers are `IMP-YYYY-L<lab-id>-00001` and `EXP-YYYY-L<lab-id>-00001`, with independent kind/laboratory/year scopes. Existing identifiers remain untouched and existing rows retain null sequence metadata. The PostgreSQL migration adds nullable metadata and scoped uniqueness; rollback takes exclusive table locks and refuses to remove any retained issued numbering, including archived records. The migration is applied to development and the explicitly named dedicated test database without resetting either database.

Import/export create and update now use one transactional action and shared Form Request validation. Laboratory and actor are server-derived; manual invoice links, issued numbers and protected evidence fields are rejected. Validated line fields cannot supply an owner or parent. Shared customer/site pairs and active product/country/transport/currency directories are locked and rechecked, including after lifecycle/audit hooks. Unbilled line replacement soft-archives old lines instead of deleting history. The action checks header/line saves, exact generated-number intent, retained line evidence, audit actor/subject/properties and final authority. A veto, mutation, retired directory or revoked authority rolls back the whole operation.

Billed source model writes reject core changes and line creation/edit/delete/restore. Root archive/restore remains separately permissioned. The quote observation path reuses the existing action and API Resource, locks and verifies every retained line, and checks exact audit identity. The older full quote editor now takes a fresh owner/authority/root lock and rejects a source that becomes billed after request validation. **Unbilled quote authoring is not yet canonical:** duplicated validation, pricing calculations, prior-year handling, retained line replacement and collection-product bookkeeping are the next slice. No financial cancellation/refund or new pricing policy is inferred.

### Canonical unbilled quote authoring and agreed prices

The approved quote workflow now uses a single transactional authoring action and shared Form Request validation. Inputs name the catalog explicitly (parameter, matrix, product or paid service), avoiding the old assumption that independent catalog IDs identify parameters. Agreed unit prices, fractional quantities and percentage/per-unit fixed discounts use bounded integer-cent calculations and deterministic half-up rounding. Discount totals account for quantity. Catalog descriptions and tax settings are server-derived; caller-supplied actors, laboratory owners, issued identifiers, billing state, financial totals and unknown line fields are rejected. Shared customer/site pairs and units are checked before and after persistence. Historical quote identifiers remain unchanged; quote numbering still uses the existing global/year grouping. On an authorized draft revision, the current signature changes and the prior signature remains in retained revision evidence; billed signatures are not rewritten.

Replacing unbilled lines archives their prior content instead of deleting it. Laboratory-owned collection source reservations are locked, checked and synchronized when linking or removing lines. The existing narrow line-correction path shares that bookkeeping and verifies intended line/source writes and final persisted audit evidence. New authoring verifies allocator identity, checked signature persistence, retained lines, source links, final directories/catalogs and fresh authority before commit. Billed quotes still allow observations only. Trusted line metadata carries catalog type, agreed price and discount intent through quote-to-invoice conversion; certificate caller metadata is not copied as trusted evidence.

Approved draft revision signing uses the existing document-signature provider, with no key or algorithm change and no fiscal/legal compliance claim. The signed payload contains a unique revision identity, the previous signature and previous/current commercial snapshots, including line observations. Root observations are outside the signed core because they remain editable after billing; ordinary document activity retains their changes. Both canonical authoring and narrow row correction lock prior signed history before any lifecycle writes, reject early or late history corruption, and retain it alongside the new signed audit. Row correction checks every retained active/archived sibling line. Billed observation updates verify unchanged signed history, root core and retained lines. The older initial-signing command is a no-op for an already signed quote, so it cannot replace a revision signature.

The authoring resource reconstructs editable agreed prices without silently resolving ambiguous historical catalog IDs. Rows missing catalog metadata ask for explicit reselection; stored historical evidence is not backfilled by guessing. Create/edit pages submit only authoring inputs, bind the agreed-price controls to real line state, use explicit discount modes, display nested validation/transport failures, suppress duplicate full submissions and retain draft state. Directory lookup responses cannot overwrite a newer customer selection. Add-only operators land on an authorized creation page after success. Detail pages receive tax and display agreed prices and a consistent gross-subtotal/discount breakdown; PDF item tables use the authoritative item description instead of a generic service label.

### Native quote concurrency and approved scientific stage integrity

Seven isolated native PostgreSQL cases pass **247 assertions** in 19.02 seconds. Four distinct quote operators, including the original author, compete on reversed source ordering and full draft replacements. Tests establish one successful conflicting creation, coherent successive signed revisions, edit-versus-issuance and edit-versus-archive outcomes, and real root rollback for creation, full revision and partial correction. Independent connections see no uncommitted commercial revision. The deadlock fix preserves current permissions, direct membership and user state checks; it does not weaken locks or add blanket retries. Worksheet, counter-intake and portal-intake test barriers now wait at the laboratory lock, preserving their fresh revocation and outcome assertions. Cross-laboratory shared-actor and unrelated raw-FK cycles remain outside this bounded concurrency proof.

Scientific result stages now use one validation service in HTTP requests and the canonical queued action. Verification requires recorded insertion value/date; approval requires recorded insertion and verification values/dates. Nonblank scalar values include zero. Idempotent same-evidence replay remains available, but ordinary insertion/review cannot replace approved evidence, including re-approval with different evidence. The action checks save vetoes, submitted and unsubmitted sibling values, exact active result ID membership, and explicit audit actor/subject/description/properties before completion and after workflow/event work. Failed writes clean up newly staged signature media. Dynamic audit values are structured properties expanded through a trusted fixed template, so placeholder-like parameter labels remain literal while intended descriptions are independently checked. ### Shared-account restrictions and laboratory-private qualifications

The existing global `admin` role identifies the actual system administrator; a laboratory role or possession of `edit_users`, role/permission catalogue permissions, password-reset, activation or impersonation permissions alone does not. Account owners may change their own profile through their authorized flow, but cannot assign themselves global grants or departments. Email changes clear verification. System administration remains subject to existing operation capabilities and direct laboratory membership; impersonated sessions cannot change shared accounts. Other staff can replace only the active laboratory's qualifications. Global account creation, password reset, archive/restore, activation, impersonation and catalogue authoring are restricted to the actual system administrator. Role/permission archive and restore no longer accept GET requests.

The canonical dossier update action locks the laboratory, ordered actor/target membership rows and ordered shared user rows before mutation. It checks intended root attributes, generated birthday, all global access pivots and the exact complete qualification set, including unchanged peer-lab evidence. Save vetoes, mutated ownership/content, injected qualifications or global grants, and final authority/membership revocation roll back together. The deliberate self-email verification reset still checks the live laboratory and fresh actor capability, without requiring the verification it intentionally cleared. Valid numeric-string selector IDs normalize through integer casts; nullable qualification activation explicitly defaults to true, while false remains false and invalid boolean strings fail validation. Credential, account status and server identity fields are rejected from the dossier payload.

Server capabilities now control the staff dossier, directory and role/permission register controls. The Inertia payload contains only permitted fields. Bulk confirmation captures its IDs before subsequent selection changes, rejects pending duplicate work, remains open until success, and reports transport failures without claiming completion. Shared-table row archive handling avoids a second confirmation. The shared dialog preserves its original close-on-confirm default for other consumers and adds opt-in pending guards; it transitions only opacity/transform and uses ease-out for exit. These are bounded functional safeguards, not Phase 2 layout or visual acceptance.

### Canonical laboratory membership and broader checkpoint

The user chose membership completion before the full PHP checkpoint. Local administrators with `add_users` may add an existing active, verified account by its exact registered email; there is no cross-laboratory account-search endpoint or credential setup in this workflow. New membership explicitly grants neither network overview nor branding management. `delete_users` permits removing another member from the active directly joined laboratory, not deleting or deactivating the shared account. Self-removal is rejected. Global identity, roles/permissions/departments, all qualification evidence and peer memberships remain unchanged. Join replay is a no-op; removal replay is a no-op only when retained evidence establishes a prior removal for that exact lab/user. Foreign removals remain 404. Request bodies cannot choose another laboratory or smuggle global fields or pivot grants; the add endpoint is throttled.

Writes lock the live laboratory, local actor/target pivots and sorted shared users, then check fresh direct-member authority. Membership and audit commit together; the complete intended target membership/account/access/qualification state and explicit audit identity/content are rechecked after lifecycle work. The audit is performed on the owning laboratory rather than the shared user, with only lab/target IDs and the membership outcome in its properties. Membership logs are default-denied without a live eligible local viewer with `view_users`; both activity model types and archived queries apply the same ownership constraint. A laboratory morph alias supports these new audits without changing existing document aliases.

### Canonical dossier history, activity privacy and native dossier races

Canonical dossier saves record only changed approved profile/access fields in `staff_account` history, with before/after values and origin laboratory/target identifiers. Global history is visible only to the fresh eligible account owner or actual system administrator, not another laboratory user with `view_users`. Qualification history records old/new local qualification snapshots in `personnel_qualifications`, performed on the owning laboratory rather than the shared account. Replaced and removed evidence remains in these snapshots; peer qualification notes and unchanged personal/security fields never enter them. Constant audit descriptions do not expand user-controlled placeholder-like values. Profile no-ops do not create new audit entries.

Both audits live inside the existing dossier transaction. Final checks cover explicit persisted audit identity/content, the complete retained account/qualification/membership history, all target memberships, intended shared root/access and complete qualification evidence, and fresh actor/lab/target authority after logging. Disabled/vetoed logging, corrupted or injected history, changes from the second audit hook, peer membership/evidence alteration and authority revocation roll back the entire operation. The intentional own-email verification reset remains permitted and audited without turning into a general verification bypass.

Base/configured activity models, list/detail/statistics/filter options and the actual export query apply the new visibility boundary; the existing raw archive constraint also invokes it. Missing context/viewer, impersonation, unverified/inactive accounts, revoked membership and archived laboratory fail closed. Activity detail uses an API Resource and serializes User causer/subject identities as ID/name only, rather than full security/OAuth/profile metadata. List causers use ID/name/email only and omit model appendages/media. Canonical staff/qualification/membership events cannot be individually deleted or removed by HTTP bulk cleanup/archive. This is bounded action/HTTP retention, not universal database immutability or an audit-policy claim for every existing module.

Thirty-three new HTTP/action history cases cover allowlisted snapshots, global grants/revocations and no-op saves, actual list/detail/custom-model/export visibility, fresh read revocations, secret-marker responses, retained cleanup/archive behavior, own-email reset and nineteen lifecycle/audit faults. Four isolated native PostgreSQL cases prove mixed dossier/two-audit changes are invisible to independent connections until the actual outer commit and disappear on outer rollback; two laboratories can edit a shared user's separate local qualifications without losing peer evidence; opposing cross-lab actor/target pairs complete without a user-lock cycle; and same-lab removal/edit races either record an eligible edit before removal or reject the later edit without changing shared/peer records. These are bounded scenarios, not proof of every global archive/password/status/permission race. The final thirteen-file affected regression passes **324 tests / 3,071 assertions** in 104.39 seconds, after final formatting and the completed production build; output is `/private/tmp/lims-staff-history-regression.log`. It includes both new history files and account, personnel qualification, profile configuration, write smoke, membership, export, financial/source ownership, proposal, occurrence and worksheet boundaries. No development records/schema, credentials, dependencies or external communications changed; private native-test schemas are automatically removed. A new whole-suite run has been offered separately; the older membership checkpoint is not presented as whole-suite evidence for this later slice.

### Global account lifecycle, retained evidence and explicit status intent

Administrator password reset, global soft archive/restore and activation now delegate to one checked lifecycle action. It locks laboratory → ordered local memberships → sorted shared actor/target users, performs complete batch preflight before any write, rejects archived password/status targets, checks every lifecycle save and rechecks fresh administrator authority after all audits. Self archive/restore/status is prohibited; an authorized administrator can reset their own password. Soft archive preserves files, media rows, qualifications, peer memberships and global assignment pivots. No hard deletion or account-wide activation is available to a laboratory permission holder alone.

Final persistence checks compare intended shared root fields and raw membership/department/role/permission assignment evidence, complete qualification/media/token rows, both class- and alias-owned passkeys and retained canonical history. Tests exposed and fixed the passkey owner-type evidence gap. Lifecycle hooks and later batch audits cannot silently rewrite identity, credentials, peer data or prior history. New retained `staff_account` events contain only operation/old-new activation intent and origin/target identifiers; password audits contain a boolean credential-reset marker, never plaintext passwords or hashes. Existing history privacy and HTTP retention rules apply to these events.

Status submission now sends an explicit `is_active` boolean through `users.setActiveStatus`, replacing the inversion-based write. Repeating deactivate/activate intent cannot accidentally invert the account again. Archive/restore/status/password replays that already match intent perform no lifecycle write or new audit. Form Requests reject missing intent, malformed/duplicate IDs and unrelated root fields. The existing Inertia confirmation, frozen IDs, pending guard and persistent failure feedback remain. Better UI/Emil guidance influenced predictable action intent and retained recovery feedback; no new layout, motion or visual direction was introduced. Functional payload/handler checks pass; live browser, focus/keyboard, transport recovery presentation and pixel/reference acceptance remain **Not verified**.

Final lifecycle test file: **124 passed / 374 assertions** in 25.71 seconds, after Pint and the completed production build. The preceding four-file affected regression passes **189 tests / 1,049 assertions** in 48.58 seconds, before seven final lifecycle cases were added; those seven are included in the final 124-case result. Native dossier/lifecycle file: **nine passed / 156 assertions** in 19.13 seconds, including five new scenarios: reversed archive batches and replay, two laboratories requesting the same global status, archive versus local membership removal, membership revocation while a global action waits at its lock boundary, and independent-connection visibility at real outer rollback/commit. The other four native dossier cases still pass. These are bounded scenarios, not proof of every password/restore/catalogue/credential race.

Eighteen staff frontend checks pass, including actual activation payload/replay/frozen-ID behavior and existing confirmation/recovery handlers. Production build passes in 16.56 seconds with the existing large-chunk warning. Pint and diff checks pass. Read-only Laravel review found no additional concrete introduced blocker; it did not run tests or access the database. The authorized whole PHP checkpoint completes with **2,376 passed, two expected skips, one sandbox-only Chrome startup failure / 20,635 assertions** in 820.52 seconds. JUnit is `/private/tmp/lims-phase1-staff-lifecycle-checkpoint.xml`; the unchanged real-Chrome case passes outside the sandbox (one case / five assertions, 3.02 seconds). All new lifecycle/history/membership cases are included. No development data/schema, passwords, dependencies or external communication changed. Global account creation/catalogue safeguards and the remaining Phase 1 gates stay open. ### Canonical administrative account creation and atomic initial membership

`CreateStaffAccount` replaces the controller's unchecked creation transaction. It locks the live laboratory, actor membership and eligible actor, checks actual system-administrator authority before writes and again after both audits, and captures intended root attributes before model lifecycle hooks. Checked persistence creates the account, deduplicated requested department assignments and exactly one local membership with network/branding privileges disabled. Final guards compare the complete stored root and exact membership/department evidence and reject unexpected global roles, direct permissions, qualifications, personal API tokens, either passkey morph encoding, media or peer memberships. Both retained audit records are checked, and unexpected retained history is rejected before commit.

Existing administrative defaults remain active but email-unverified, light theme, no changed-password flag or extra credentials. Existing department eligibility, including archived directory entries, is unchanged. Email and username uniqueness includes archived accounts; creation never silently restores or joins an existing account. The HTTP request bounds scalar types, lengths and gender values and rejects unrelated account-security fields. The action translates only the exact parsed email/username unique-constraint identifiers to validation; unrelated constraints retain their original exception. Review exposed a message-matching bug because exception messages include submitted values; exact Laravel constraint metadata now replaces message substring matching, with marker-text and unrelated-constraint regression coverage. Public Fortify registration and verification/mail behavior are unchanged. No notification or external communication was added.

The final seven-file affected regression passes **252 tests / 1,546 assertions** in 93.10 seconds, after Pint; output is `/private/tmp/lims-staff-create-regression.log`. The native dossier/lifecycle/creation file now has **14 cases / 254 assertions**, including five additional creation cases: competing email and username identities across laboratories, membership and admin-role revocation while creation waits at the lock boundary even with direct `add_users` permission, and independent-connection visibility at real outer rollback/commit. Exactly one successful creation publishes one lab membership, its requested departments and two audits; the losing request publishes nothing. Private native-test schemas and worker markers are removed automatically.

### Shared template archive evidence and canonical proposal-create serialization

Canonical proposal creation now locks the selected live template inside its transaction, after fresh local actor access and payload validation, before reserving or writing the proposal graph. Template archive and proposal creation therefore coordinate on the same row. If archive commits first, creation receives controlled `template_id` validation and publishes no proposal, items or consent agreement. If creation commits first, archive sees the committed reference and refuses the archive. A final stored-template-ID comparison rejects model/audit hooks that substitute another template, including an archived one. Existing inactive templates remain usable; no new activation prerequisite was introduced. Shared-template readers/editors still do not require lab membership. Used templates remain editable/deactivatable under the existing policy, but cannot be archived when any laboratory's live or archived proposal references one.

The shared archive action captures every original template attribute and prior subject audit, keeps ordered template/history locks and strict full-batch validation, and checks the final complete batch after all writes and audits. Only the requested deleted-state transition and a server-bounded update timestamp may change. Both newly created audit identity/content/properties and unchanged prior history are verified; altered, deleted or injected history rolls the entire transaction back. Repeated intent remains a no-op, but skipped members are still checked. Fresh final authority includes active account, verified email and the existing exact permission, matching the HTTP middleware boundary. Review found that excluding `updated_at` left a late-hook evidence gap; the final implementation now captures, bounds and compares it instead. No new lab-owner, admin-role or impersonation policy was added for shared templates.

The final five-file affected regression passes **162 tests / 844 assertions** in 40.46 seconds after final Pint; log: `/private/tmp/lims-template-archive-regression.log`. It includes retained legacy template workflow, shared template access, proposal authoring, new archive integrity and new native concurrency files. The archive integrity file passes **53 cases / 199 assertions**, covering full presentation/creator/root preservation and replay, skipped-member tampering and archive/restore fault cases for root, timestamps, creator, status, JSON layout/export payloads, audit identity/content, retained history and fresh authority. Six added proposal-authoring cases reject creating/created/audit-hook template substitutions and prove whole-graph rollback. Seven isolated native PostgreSQL cases exercise simultaneous creation/archive, both deterministic commit orders, reversed overlapping batches, permission and verification revocation while a worker waits, and independent-connection visibility at real outer commit/rollback. Private schemas and worker markers are removed automatically.

Pint, diff checks and read-only Laravel review pass; review did not run tests or access the database. No frontend code changed, so no new build, browser or reference comparison is claimed. No development records/schema, credentials, dependencies or external communications changed. The latest whole PHP run predates this and the administrative creation slice. This is a canonical writer guarantee, not a universal database invariant for raw/internal proposal inserts or a complete template lifecycle claim. Canonical template create/update/import/desired-status writes, validated nested studio payloads, atomic import/name uniqueness, and live-browser recovery remain open. History cleanup/retention rules outside these transactions are unchanged. Existing source/render/release policies and the other Phase 1 exit gates remain unchanged.

### Shared template desired-status integrity and confirmation recovery

`ProposalTemplateStatusRequest` accepts one required boolean and refuses unrelated fields. `SetProposalTemplateActiveStatus` reloads and locks the active verified permission holder and live shared template, captures requested status and server timestamp before model hooks, checks persistence and exact status-audit identity/content/properties, and compares the complete stored template and unchanged prior subject history before commit. Save/audit vetoes, altered content/creator/layout/export/archive/timestamps, rewritten history and lost authority fail atomically. Repeated desired status preserves timestamps and does not generate another audit. Existing status descriptions and JSON response fields remain; new audits have explicit `activation_changed` events and previous/requested boolean properties. Templates still require no laboratory membership, and used or inactive templates retain their existing policy. History cleanup/retention rules outside these transactions are unchanged.

The new status file passes **73 cases / 207 assertions**, including both desired states, replay, missing/invalid boolean, unexpected fields, missing/archived templates, absent fresh authority and 58 lifecycle/audit/root/history fault cases. The native concurrency file passes **16 cases / 209 assertions**, with nine new scenarios: duplicate desired state from two actors, status/archive competition and both deterministic commit orders, four waiting authority revocations, and independent-connection visibility at real outer rollback/commit. It reuses the private test-schema and worker-cleanup safeguards; development records and schema are not modified. The seven-file affected PHP regression passes **274 tests / 1,630 assertions** in 74.09 seconds after PHP formatting; log: `/private/tmp/lims-template-status-regression.log`. The subsequent final status-file rerun still passes all 73 cases / 207 assertions in 7.38 seconds after strengthening replay assertions with a ten-second clock advance, so unchanged timestamps are not merely a same-second coincidence. This is not a new whole-suite run.

`VAPProposalTemplates/Show.vue` no longer mounts both confirmations on initial render or supplies unsupported props, slots and event names to the shared dialog. Status uses Inertia v3 `useHttp` and a frozen template ID/desired boolean, blocks pending replacement/dismissal/repeated submission and retains validation/HTTP/network/cancellation feedback. Archive uses the existing guarded record-archive composable and a frozen ID. Success is reported only for the confirmed boolean. Confirmed state remains displayed if refresh fails or a delayed same-template response arrives out of order; mismatched refreshes show saved-state guidance, matching state clears the override, and another template never inherits it. Better UI and Emil guidance influenced static loading/error cues and retained recovery context; no new motion, tokens, layout direction or Phase 2 styling was introduced.

All **46 focused frontend checks** pass (twenty new template status/render/refresh cases plus existing archive and staff-confirmation cases). Production build passes in 16.17 seconds with the existing large-chunk warning; final Pint and diff checks pass. Read-only Laravel review found and verified fixes for failed and out-of-order refresh recovery; it did not edit files or run tests/database calls. The existing browser CLI was reused without dependency changes; an initial sandbox cache error was resolved by an approved browser invocation. The browser reaches the local application's sign-in screen but has no authenticated session. No password was reset, role broadened or development data rewritten. The only observed public-page console error is the existing missing favicon; authenticated template interactions, keyboard/focus return, hover/active states, physical-device interaction and 10%-speed motion replay are **not verified**. Functional code/test review approves the inspected fixes only, not browser or visual acceptance. Browser sign-in has been requested separately. Canonical template create/update/import, validated nested studio payloads, atomic name/import invariants and other Phase 1 exit gates remain open.

| Correct state indication — HIGH, fixed | `resources/js/Pages/VAPProposalTemplates/Show.vue` | Successful write followed by failed/stale refresh displays the previous status | Matching-ID confirmed state and visible refresh guidance | The displayed state and next requested decision agree with confirmed evidence |

### Full-suite memory checkpoint and bounded verification

Permanent launcher follow-up: the project now provides `php scripts/run-php-tests.php --compact` and the equivalent `composer test -- --compact`, rather than depending on a manually maintained temporary INI directory. It queries the running PHP binary's actual scan list when unset, preserves explicit scan-directory order and loaded main INI, rebases relative scan paths to the caller's directory, then appends a unique 0700 directory containing only a 0600 `opcache.enable_cli=1` file. Existing extensions, instrumentation, memory/reporting directives and environment remain inherited. A preflight requires actual CLI OPcache operation; failure is reported instead of silently launching an uncached suite. Dotenv PHP runtime overrides that Collision would clear are rejected, as are detected default/custom application config caches, including Laravel's `null`/`(null)` fallback forms. The launcher does not remove user configuration or load the application to establish these checks. PHPUnit's existing forced PostgreSQL isolation and 512 MiB setting remain authoritative for its child; the outer Artisan bootstrap still precedes PHPUnit configuration.

Arguments, stdin/stdout/stderr and child exit status are forwarded. Active-child SIGINT/SIGTERM are forwarded and return the conventional 128+signal status. Finally plus shutdown cleanup removes only the invocation's own INI and directory. Uncatchable termination such as SIGKILL cannot guarantee cleanup; a signal during the bounded preflight is recorded but may wait for that probe's 15-second timeout. No blanket application teardown, renderer/instrumentation removal, error suppression, global Herd configuration or memory-limit increase is introduced. Composer's timeout is disabled only for the `test` script, not dependency operations; [Composer documents this scoped helper](https://getcomposer.org/doc/06-config.md#disabling-timeouts-for-an-individual-script-command).

The 20-case launcher file checks default/explicit/relative scan configuration, main-INI preservation, loaded extensions and runtime settings, exact argument/input/environment forwarding, exit 39, private permissions, cleanup after failure and both signals, config-cache guards, safe parser diagnostics and the real Artisan-to-PHPUnit/JUnit bridge. The compilation provider additionally checks the new launcher with cold `E_ALL` compilation. An initial real bridge assertion still expected two compile cases; it was updated to the provider's exact case count after adding the third source. A malformed-dotenv fixture initially used an unfinished quoted line that phpdotenv discards; parser-confirmed trailing invalid text now exercises the intended secret-safe failure. Read-only Laravel review found and prompted the cache-null sentinel repair; its earlier review did not independently execute tests.

Initial verification after Pint through the actual Composer entry: **48 passed / 653 assertions**, no failures, errors or skips, 30.61 seconds; `/private/tmp/lims-project-launcher-final-corrected.{log,xml}`. Case-level reconciliation verifies all 48 cases and 653 assertions. Coverage includes the full 20/131 launcher file, three compilation cases, eight lifecycle cases, twelve PostgreSQL foundation cases, three four-worker financial issuance datasets and two consumption/adjustment worker cases. Current canonical discovery: **3,625 cases / 176 classes**, `/private/tmp/lims-project-launcher-manifest.xml`. The older failed targeted run is not added to final counts. The complete green 3,604-case run below predates this launcher/new-test slice. The subsequent approved full launcher checkpoint now passes **3,623 tests, two expected skips / 27,188 assertions**, exit 0, with exact 3,625-case/176-class reconciliation; details and evidence paths are recorded at the top of this plan. The prior manual comparison INI file/directory has been removed after all dependent processes finished. The new launcher owns and cleans each invocation.
