The next lifecycle slice adds three bounded cases using the original staff authority-revocation, membership operator-evidence and consumption late-authority fault fixtures. Each executes the original assertions and Laravel setup/transactional teardown eight times, retains the fixture objects deliberately, and checks middle application/event/connection/PDO/exception-handler weak references after collection. Explicit environment guards require `testing` and `lims_unleashed_test`. Reflection invokes Laravel fixture methods; this is not a simulation of PHPUnit's complete `runBare` hooks, mock verification or process-global restoration. Setup is now inside the helper's cleanup `try/finally`. Final lifecycle/foundation verification after Pint passes **20 cases / 434 assertions**, zero failures/errors, in 11.93 seconds; `/private/tmp/lims-fault-lifecycle-final.{log,xml}` and `-events.log`. The eight lifecycle cases are verified separately from the earlier complete 3,594-case checkpoint; no refreshed monolithic run is claimed.

Temporary 64-cycle diagnostics narrowed the remaining memory gate. The three identical warmed fault fixtures each retain approximately 93–96 KiB of live PHP heap per application lifecycle despite explicit collection every eight cycles, while declared class counts plateau and the tracked old graphs collect. Plain application/transaction setup without HTTP shows comparable growth; deliberately releasing fixture objects reduces it only slightly (approximately 91–93 KiB remains per cycle). A bounded aggregate inspection found no growth in static arrays or the directly inspected static-singleton array properties; it does not exclude deeper object/native roots. Evidence is `/private/tmp/lims-fault-lifecycle-warmed.{log,xml}`, `/private/tmp/lims-plain-lifecycle-warmed.{log,xml}`, `/private/tmp/lims-plain-lifecycle-released.{log,xml}` and `/private/tmp/lims-plain-lifecycle-static.{log,xml}`. A proposed direct-PHPUnit CLI OPcache comparison was rejected by safety review because it bypassed the required Artisan test launch path; it did not run, and no runtime configuration changed. Temporary memory/global-state printing and enlarged loops were removed before final validation. These measurements do not establish the cause or a repair. The 512 MiB limit, automatic GC, real renderer, runtime instrumentation and all original fixture assertions remain intact. Browser verification remains deferred; Phase 1 remains open.

The approved single-process checkpoint stopped after 2,991 pass markers and two skip markers with a 512 MiB exhaustion during `StaffAccountLifecycleTest`'s `deactivate_actor_state` case. It exited 2 and produced an empty JUnit file, so it is not a completed whole-suite checkpoint. Its console evidence remains `/private/tmp/lims-phase1-membership-checkpoint.log`. The exact case passes alone (one case / two assertions), and the membership/lifecycle pair passes 174 cases / 620 assertions. Source inspection has not established the retained-memory root; no unproven Ignition/Flare attribution or instrumentation removal is claimed.

A measured PHPUnit periodic-GC experiment increased event-observed peak memory from 118.64 MiB to 199.11 MiB and final memory from 115.44 MiB to 197.33 MiB on those same 174 cases. It was reverted. `phpunit.xml` keeps its original settings and 512 MiB limit. A foundation test verifies the unchanged limit and normal automatic garbage collection. No assertion, renderer, dependency or application behavior was weakened.

Complete verification finished in four sequential fresh PHP processes, with 42 explicitly enumerated test files per batch, per-batch JUnit and memory telemetry. Batches report 368/3,492, 1,099/8,493, 800/6,063 and 933/6,973 cases/assertions; batch 4 includes two expected skips. All four exit 0. Aggregate evidence is **3,198 passed, two expected skips / 25,021 assertions**, zero failures/errors, with 1,407.29 seconds of combined JUnit test time. The canonical `--list-tests-xml` manifest has 3,200 cases in 168 classes; every reported case, including named/numeric dataset identities, reconciles exactly once, with no omission, duplication or unexpected case. The real configured-Chrome test passes (1/5); both missing-driver guards skip because that optional driver is installed. The updated foundation file passes 12/94, including the unchanged-memory/automatic-GC guard. Event-observed batch peaks are 188.47, 254.46, 261.88 and 240.83 MiB. These are event observations, not a continuous runtime peak profiler.

Batch evidence is `/private/tmp/lims-membership-all-batch-{1..4}.{xml,log}`, telemetry adds `-events.log`, and the manifest is `/private/tmp/lims-membership-all-manifest.xml`. Tests ran in sorted-file batches, which differs from the default suite order, with no PHP/fixture edits during execution. This is bounded complete-suite verification, not proof of order independence or that a single 3,200-case PHP process fits the current limit. The single-process memory issue remains an explicit test-process gate; its root is still unproven. The GC experiment and runner configuration change remain reverted.

### Canonical proposal-template validation and nullable-list rendering

Create, update and unsaved PDF preview use `ProposalTemplateAuthoringRequest` and `ProposalTemplateValidation`. Authorization preserves their existing add/edit permissions and shared-template semantics. The validation service allows only declared root and nested studio fields, requires dense block/variable/chart lists, rejects non-object rows and nested chart scalars, and validates media/CSS/chart inputs using the existing canonical editor contract. Request transport fields are allowed only at the HTTP boundary and are not persisted. The trusted bound update record alone is excluded from name uniqueness; archived names still collide as before. Preview does not require a unique name or persist a template.

Preflight caps are 500 blocks, 500 variables, 1,000 entries per chart list and 10,000 chart entries overall, checked before wildcard rule expansion. Literal chart zero survives text parsing and budget counting. Explicit null category becomes the supported `general` value; explicit null nested block/catalog lists become `[]`. Omitted optional fields and whole-null layout/export values retain their existing behavior. Three reproduced null-list authoring/preview failures now pass. A separately reproduced stored-template PDF HTTP 500 is repaired by read-only null-list normalization in the shared builder; missing keys still inherit defaults, and explicit empty lists still replace defaults. The PDF test uses the real builder and checks every stored template attribute remains unchanged. No record repair or migration is performed.

Final five-file regression (`ProposalTemplateValidationTest`, `ProposalTemplateAccessTest`, `LegacyProposalTemplateWorkflowTest`, `ProposalWorkflowTest`, `ReportStudioWorkflowTest`): **252 passed, two expected driver guards skipped / 1,880 assertions**, in 36.88 seconds. Output: `/private/tmp/lims-template-validation-renderer-regression.log`. This invocation runs outside sandbox restrictions so the real configured-Chrome test can start, without weakening or mocking that case. The new validation file has 104 cases. Final Pint and diff checks pass; read-only Laravel review found no concrete introduced defect. Generated-proposal null-list rendering is not separately fault-tested, though it uses the same helper and existing proposal/renderer regression passes.

At this validation checkpoint, checked transactional template create/update writes, importer atomicity and native name races remained separate work. The later checked-authoring checkpoint below completes canonical create/update persistence. The requested archived-name import policy still has no answer; importer behavior and database name constraints are unchanged. A future raw importer must explicitly account for HTTP trimming/empty-string normalization before reusing the shared validation service. No authenticated browser, focus, pixel/reference or Phase 2 acceptance is claimed.

### Checked canonical proposal-template authoring

`SaveProposalTemplate` is the single create/update workflow used by the canonical controller. It locks the current verified active actor, then the live template for updates, and revalidates against that fresh row. Creation uses server-owned creator/defaults/timestamps; updates preserve creator, archive state, timestamps other than the requested server update time, and all omitted optional fields. Existing shared editing and used-template semantics are unchanged. A genuine unchanged update returns without firing save hooks or adding audit/timestamp changes, while still checking fresh authority.

The action captures intended persisted attributes before model hooks, checks save success, writes a subject/actor-bound allowlisted creation/revision audit, then verifies fresh authority, the complete stored root, exact audit identity/content/properties and retained prior subject history including the legacy morph alias. Vetoes, altered content/root/audit fields, deleted evidence and lost authority abort and roll back the transaction. Revisions retain submitted old/new authoring values; ordinary editing does not change the original creator. Internal callers cannot bypass the shared payload contract. The 56-case fault matrix failed against the former controller: most cases returned successful redirects, while vetoed creation produced an uncontrolled HTTP 500. The old controller had no authoring audit, so post-audit fault hooks become reachable only with the new action; their rollback evidence comes from the final passing run.

Twelve additional create/update cases cover the supported boolean forms `false`, `true`, `0`, `1`, `'0'` and `'1'`. Before normalization, eight valid integer/string submissions returned HTTP 409 because PostgreSQL returned a canonical boolean while raw intent retained the transport type. Shared validation now canonicalizes only an explicitly present, validated `is_active`; unsupported values remain subject to the existing boolean rule and omission remains absent. Equivalent encoded-value no-op replay is not separately fault-tested; unchanged revisions and native competing replay are verified.

The native concurrency file adds fifteen cases: two-actor same-revision replay publishes one audit; authoring/archive ordering and authoring/status races preserve their respective fields; eight waiting create/update authority revocations fail closed; and real outer rollback/commit controls visibility of both the template and history. An initial eight-case snapshot failure compared an unsynchronized fixture with a persisted row; the baseline now reads the fresh stored fixture before the race, retaining strict full-row assertions. No assertion was weakened. All thirty-one cases in that file pass within the final affected regression.

Final verification after Pint: **410 passed / 2,194 assertions across eight PHP files**, in 115.17 seconds; no failures or skips. Output: `/private/tmp/lims-template-authoring-regression-final.log`. The files cover authoring, shared validation/access, retained legacy template workflow, proposal workflow, archive integrity, desired status and native concurrency. Pint and diff checks pass, and read-only Laravel review found no concrete introduced defect. This is not a new whole-suite checkpoint or browser/visual acceptance. Importer atomicity and database/name-level concurrent uniqueness remain open; row locks and validation alone are not claimed to enforce unique concurrent names. The pending archived-name import decision has not been inferred. No UI, external transport, development records/schema, credentials or dependencies changed.

### Checked atomic proposal-template import

`ProposalTemplateImportRequest` validates a supported JSON/TXT/CSV/XLSX upload of at most 5 MiB and rejects authority fields. `ProposalTemplateImportFileReader` has no persistence side effects. It rejects empty files, partial rows, malformed JSON columns and more than 100 data rows. Spreadsheet preflight limits worksheets, physical rows and columns; XLSX archives are bounded to 512 entries and 20 MiB of expanded content before loading. Blank spreadsheet boolean/JSON transport cells receive canonical defaults. Identifier fields are trimmed, while authored content, descriptions, CSS and nested text remain byte-for-byte unchanged. Raw JSON retains object/list identity until root and declared layout/chart list shapes are checked. Known parser failures become validation feedback; unexpected runtime failures propagate for framework reporting instead of being misreported as invalid user files.

`ImportProposalTemplates` prevalidates every row, then locks the current verified active importer and live matching roots in stable ID order. Each row reuses the checked save action with the distinct import permission, original creator preservation, explicit import audit origin, full root intent and exact new/retained evidence checks. Pending existing rows must still match the initial batch snapshot; every final row and its history, including no-op rows, must match its frozen result. Later hooks changing earlier or pending roots, previous/new history or authority abort and roll back the entire batch. Replay does not trigger saves or duplicate history. Ambiguous live names fail before writes. Archived names retain the existing behavior pending the unanswered policy decision; no universal concurrent absent-name uniqueness is claimed, and no name constraint was added.

The import file passes **80 cases / 274 assertions**. Twenty-one red reader cases reproduced whitespace-cell failures, swallowed programmer errors, authored-string trimming and numeric-object/list bypasses before repair. Six new native PostgreSQL cases cover reversed existing-row import replay, four waiting importer authority revocations and real outer commit/rollback visibility; the native file now passes 37 cases. Final affected verification after Pint: **496 passed / 2,551 assertions across nine files**, 137.95 seconds, no failures or skips; `/private/tmp/lims-template-import-regression-final.log`. Read-only review found no further concrete introduced defect. XLSX exports bind formula-looking content as literal text and round-trip it unchanged. This does not protect CSV when opened in spreadsheet applications; retiring CSV exports versus an explicit escaping policy has been requested separately. CSV behavior is unchanged until that decision. No new browser, pixel/reference, production build or Phase 2 acceptance is claimed for this backend-only slice.

### Checked scientific workflow parents and bounded native insertion concurrency

`ProcessLaboratoryResults` now captures the locked analysis/counter root, target sample and laboratory code, collection product, laboratory-owned intake entry, and active sibling code/sample/analysis graph before result writes. Parent transitions use checked Eloquent saves with frozen intended fields and timestamps; vetoes and collateral changes roll back the entire result stage. Genuine unchanged parent transitions preserve timestamps and avoid unnecessary lifecycle writes. Completion still follows the existing issued-parameter and all-analysis completion rules; no amendment or report-release policy is introduced.

Counter completion clears the requested source flag through the same checked save rather than a bulk relation update. All original source-sample result rows, including archived evidence, are preserved except the explicitly intended requested flag and update timestamp. Exact source-result ID sets reject additions/removals. Existing automatic model audit hooks remain enabled; the explicit result-stage audit test now selects that audit by its properties instead of assuming that it is the newest activity of any Result. This does not establish a complete retained scientific-history or universal raw-writer invariant.

Final result, explicit audit and workflow checks use inert query-builder reads, retaining the appropriate soft-delete scopes and typed cast comparisons without dispatching model retrieval hooks. Full-row checks are batched by table rather than issuing one query per captured row. An unchanged replay still reloads current authority and qualifications, then rechecks both result and workflow evidence. Source-root, requested-parent and collateral save failures, graph insertions, original source-sibling changes, archived source evidence, late authority-reload changes and final-read hook faults have regression coverage. Approval parent failures preserve existing verification signature file bytes and remove only newly staged files. The query-count regression adds twenty sibling samples/analyses without increasing verification query count; graph memory and row-lock scope still scale with graph size.

Eight isolated-schema PostgreSQL cases exercise four-worker identical insertion replay, two sibling-root insertions sharing one collection parent, five waiting authority revocations, and real outer commit/rollback visibility for roots, results, explicit audits and intake/product state. Workers record backend IDs; the harness proves every worker is waiting on a PostgreSQL lock before releasing the laboratory access row. The established laboratory-first lock order is preserved. These are bounded insertion-stage concurrency checks, not proof of verification/approval/counter completion races, external event delivery, deletion races or every lifecycle writer. Deferred scientific events retain their existing after-commit behavior.

Final affected verification after Pint: **383 passed / 2,374 assertions across seven files**, in 144.21 seconds, with no failures or skips; `/private/tmp/lims-result-workflow-regression-final.log`. The files include both changed result tests, existing workflow/schema/notification checks and canonical membership plus its native concurrency checks. The full-suite checkpoint completed as recorded below. Pint and diff checks pass; read-only Laravel review found no further concrete introduced blocker. No development data/schema, credentials, dependencies or UI changed. Phase 2 reference matching remains separate.

The first full checkpoint's JUnit report records all **2,997 cases: 2,994 passed, two expected skips, one configured-Chrome failure, zero test errors / 23,410 assertions**, in 1,004.49 seconds; `/private/tmp/lims-phase1-result-workflow-checkpoint.xml`. After execution, Collision exhausted 512 MiB while formatting the Chrome test's 2.6 MiB HTML failure message, so that console invocation exited 255 without a completed summary. `ReportStudioWorkflowTest` now bounds only the assertion's optional diagnostic to 2,000 UTF-8-safe bytes; expected HTTP 200, renderer header, download and PDF-content checks remain unchanged. The exact real-Chrome case passes outside the sandbox after the fix: **one case / five assertions**, 2.97 seconds; `/private/tmp/lims-result-workflow-chrome-final.log`. No assertion, renderer, memory-limit configuration or environment guard was weakened. ### Checked scientific review signatures and native stage races

`LaboratoryResultSignatures` now checks Media save vetoes and compares the complete frozen identity/attributes of target-result and original counter-source Media before commit. The source set includes archived sibling results. Raw locked source reads establish the baseline without dispatching `retrieved` hooks; normal save/audit hooks remain enabled, including Spatie's later fresh source read. Corruption during those later hooks aborts approval rather than being accepted as the initial state. Existing source/target Media additions, removal, reassignment, filename/hash changes and altered intended collection fail closed within the captured set.

New signature files use a unique UUID identity/filename, frozen rollback references and content-hash checks. Failed creation cleans only UUID-owned rows/files and preserves the original exception as the cause of a controlled persistence error; unpersisted Media never enters directory-wide removal. Existing signatures remain intact on failed replacement or outer rollback. Successful replacement still retires the previous signature and removes it only after the real outer commit; this slice does not introduce a new historical-signature retention or amendment policy. Whole-batch Media/file verification runs at fixed batch checkpoints rather than repeating whole-batch hashing after every parameter.

Fault coverage includes 54 signed persistence cases and one invalid-ID cleanup case, eight live/archived source-Media faults and two early/late source-read cases. Twenty-four original update faults and eight source faults reproduced successful unintended commits; the two source-read cases reproduced the unsafe baseline. Six creation-veto cases instead reproduced the installed vendor's null-ID TypeError before file copying; the original failure cause is preserved, not hidden, and no disk-root deletion was demonstrated. Media Library guidance influenced UUID-scoped cleanup, frozen staged references and physical-file rollback checks.

The native file now contains 32 isolated-schema cases. New cases cover four identical signed workers, sibling verification/approval, counter insertion/review replay, twelve waiting review authority/qualification revocations, distinct competing approved values and signature hashes, and four independent-connection signed-stage commit/rollback cases. Parent and workers share one per-test temporary media root, guarded before execution; workers must demonstrably wait on a PostgreSQL lock. Own processes, directory and schema are cleaned. Deferred application events are faked to exclude external delivery, while real Eloquent/Spatie hooks and storage writes remain active.

Final seven-file verification after the last source-read fix and Pint passes **477 tests / 3,220 assertions** in 226.12 seconds, with no failures or skips; `/private/tmp/lims-result-signed-regression-verified.log`. The files cover result mutations, native concurrency, scientific workflow/schema, notification staleness, integration import and analysis lifecycle. Pint, diff checks and read-only Laravel review pass. This supersedes the overlapping affected runs, not the earlier whole-suite checkpoint; the whole PHP suite has not been rerun after this slice. No UI, development data/schema, credentials, dependencies or external communication changed. Complete retained scientific history, broader lifecycle/inter-stage/individual-result races, external retained-file byte integrity, crash recovery and Phase 2 visual acceptance remain open.

### Checked inventory transfers and native fractional races

`ManageInventoryTransfers` now uses the existing laboratory mutation-access service at transaction entry and before commit, rejecting removed membership, unverified/inactive accounts, archived laboratories and revoked operation permissions after real lock waits. The common laboratory row is locked before transfer/item/warehouse/stock rows. Bulk creation pre-locks the complete distinct warehouse set in ascending order; row input order no longer determines pair-by-pair acquisition. This proves serialization for the canonical transfer action, not every inventory writer.

Stock writes now calculate exact scaled-integer balances and use checked saves instead of unchecked increment/decrement. Transfer creation, receipt metadata, cancellation save/delete, ledger creation and newly required transaction types must persist their intended evidence. Raw locked baselines include relevant live/archived item, warehouse, position and transfer records plus retained batches/ledger rows. Fixed final comparisons reject changed quantities, identity, timestamps, history or record sets. Model hooks remain enabled. Receipts validate positive four-decimal quantities at the action boundary, not only in HTTP validation. Partial receipts still return the difference to the source; repeated terminal operations retain the existing 422/404 response semantics without moving stock again.

Required global transaction types are scoped to the actual operation. Nested creation uses a savepoint and resolves a same-code unique collision to the committed row, while unrelated shared type creation cannot invalidate another lab's transfer. A focused test reproduced the initial over-broad exact-set conflict. New issuance requires a live measured item; an already-issued transfer can receive/cancel against its archived owned item without restoring or changing the catalog record. Two regressions reproduced and repaired an initial overly strict terminal-item guard. Cancellation checks the model-generated deletion timestamp; time comparisons retain microseconds. Current migrated columns have zero fractional timestamp precision, so five subsecond fault cases explicitly exercise a higher-precision variant inside rolled-back dedicated-test transactions. No subsecond corruption was demonstrated in the default schema, and no development schema change was made.

The integrity file passes **52 tests / 202 assertions**: 44 persistence/late-state faults, four invalid quantity cases, one unrelated shared-type case, two archived-item settlement cases and one generated-cancellation timestamp fault. Four of the 44 faults plus the cancellation fault use the higher-precision test variant. The original transfer regression failed 36 cases (32 fault scenarios and four invalid receipt quantities) before safeguards; these red/green results are not additive verification counts.

Thirty-two isolated-schema native PostgreSQL cases cover competing fractional reservations, preserved batch stock, single terminal winners and unchanged rejected replays, receipt versus cancellation, shared destination receipts, reversed bulk route input, twenty waiting authority revocations and four real independent-connection outer commit/rollback cases. The independent-lab type case adds a creation barrier proving both workers reach the absent-code insertion attempt before either inserts. Every worker must actually wait on a PostgreSQL access lock before release. Processes and private schemas are cleaned; no development data is used. Final nine-file verification after Pint passes **117 tests / 1,016 assertions** in 90.46 seconds, with no failures or skips; `/private/tmp/lims-transfer-regression-verified.log`. It includes both new transfer files, retained HTTP/laboratory and fractional workflow tests, transfer reads, ledger workflow, stock adjustment, owner constraints and quantity unit tests. Pint, diff checks and read-only Laravel review pass. The whole PHP suite has not been rerun after the transfer or preceding signature slices.

Open gates: request-UUID replay for new transfer creation needs the user's policy decision; a retry remains a separate reservation today. Warehouse archive/restore coordination with canonical transfers is now verified in the later slice below. Broader adjustment, consumption, batch, procurement, item/warehouse authoring and cross-writer races remain unproven. Exact retained-history snapshots grow with lifetime item/warehouse history and are not a global scalability guarantee. Remaining HTTP Form Request consolidation and GET lifecycle paths stay in the wider route audit. This is not Phase 1 completion, Phase 4 network-stock acceptance or Phase 2 visual acceptance.

### Atomic warehouse lifecycle and private audit evidence

Two lifecycle-veto regressions reproduced partial bulk archive and restore in the former controller loops. `SetInventoryWarehousesArchived` now acquires the common laboratory lock, fresh membership/account authority, ascending raw warehouse rows, retained stock/movement/procurement/consumption references and subject history within one transaction. Archive preserves the existing rule that any retained reference blocks deletion, including archived rows and zero stock; restoring a referenced warehouse remains allowed. Desired-state replays do not write or duplicate audit evidence, but still recheck authority and evidence. Full raw laboratory, actor and fixed membership-pivot baselines reject collateral writes, pivot replacement and late revocation. Checked lifecycle results, bounded generated timestamps and frozen audit attributes reject model save vetoes or evidence corruption and roll back the complete batch.

Warehouse audit evidence has an explicit morph alias and private log. A reproduced peer-lab activity leak is fixed across the regular and configured activity models, direct reads and exports: access requires eligible direct membership, warehouse-view permission and the owned warehouse, including archived subjects. Network grants alone are insufficient; no-context reads fail closed. This does not introduce a permanent audit-retention policy or repair every global activity lifecycle path.

Bulk archive now uses DELETE and restore PATCH; old GET paths cannot mutate records. Nested routes derive identity only from the warehouse path, ignoring conflicting body targets. The shared bounded Form Request and action both reject sparse, duplicate, invalid or oversized identifier sets. Generated Ziggy routes reflect the same verbs.

The warehouse page reuses `useRecordArchive` and static feedback. Confirmations freeze the selected identifiers, remain open during failure, disable pending actions and clear only after success; row and bulk operations share the same handler. Better UI and Emil design-engineering guidance informed consistent existing components, contextual archive/restore feedback and invisible pending-state correctness, without new motion or visual-system changes. Five new frontend tests plus existing shared archive regressions pass **20 tests**, with no failures or skips; `/private/tmp/lims-warehouse-archive-frontend-final.log`. The production build succeeds in 18.53 seconds, retaining its existing large-chunk warning; `/private/tmp/lims-warehouse-archive-build-final.log`. Pint and scoped diff checks pass. No live browser session remained when Playwright sessions were inspected; authenticated hover/focus/active/empty/error rendering, pixel comparison and 10%-speed motion checks are **not verified**. Programmatic handler, pending/failure/cancellation and compiled-binding checks are not visual approval.

Final six-file PHP verification passes **177 tests / 1,512 assertions** in 141.63 seconds, with zero failures, errors or skips; `/private/tmp/lims-warehouse-archive-final.{log,xml}`. Event-observed peak is 154.89 MiB. Files cover warehouse lifecycle (30/100), transfer laboratory boundaries (9/106), transfer integrity (52/202), native concurrency (42/593), staff account history (33/345) and exports (11/166). Ten new native cases prove both archive/transfer winner orders, restore releasing a waiting transfer, reversed warehouse batches without duplicate audits and six waiting archive/restore authority revocations. No PHP/fixture edits occurred during this final invocation.

The common-lock guarantee applies to canonical transfers and this lifecycle action, not non-cooperating stock, procurement, consumption or direct writers. ### Explicit inventory category-type foundation

Categories now carry a bounded `material` or `equipment` enum, defaulting to material for new records. Existing categories are backfilled from the actual prior ID-based classification only: existing ID `1` is equipment, all other existing IDs are material, regardless of names, parent categories or archive state. Issued item identifiers, item category assignments and laboratory ownership are not rewritten. Parent hierarchy does not inherit classification.

The three migrations separate schema, data backfill and final non-null enforcement. The first schema migration installs row and truncation guards before backfill. Item insertion, reassignment and deletion retain old/new category first-use evidence under ascending category locks; use between migration steps cannot disappear before backfill. Used category type cannot be changed by raw writers, and retained usage cannot be updated, deleted or truncated. Schema rollback refuses when retained usage exists rather than destroying that evidence. All three steps must finish before the new category UI is served; intermediate nullable schema is not an accepted application rollout state. Native tests cover use-versus-classification winner order and real outer commit/rollback. This does not claim zero-downtime DDL or arbitrary non-cooperating writer guarantees.

Category create/update use the new dedicated Form Request and `SaveInventoryCategory` action; the unrelated equipment-subcategory request is not silently changed. Fresh active/verified operation authority, full raw actor/category snapshots, intended root persistence and the retained usage ledger are rechecked in one transaction, including no-op saves. Save vetoes, nullable-field changes, hierarchy cycles, late impersonation and hydration/save hooks that add false first-use markers roll back rather than return success. Entry routes open the existing canonical category manager; authoring-only access does not grant the full catalogue list. Absent-name concurrent creation still lacks a physical global name invariant; this action's existing-root locks are not a universal uniqueness guarantee. Lifetime snapshot cost remains unbenchmarked.

The new type control reuses the existing native select, field tokens and density. Better UI and Emil guidance informed a clear label, material default, bounded choices, processing/used disabled state, retained selected value and static lock explanation; no layout redesign or new animation was added. Four compile/contract tests pass. Code review found no actionable UI-polish findings within this new control. Existing 150ms field-state tokens, hover/focus/disabled/error styles and reduced-motion rules were inspected; actual browser keyboard interaction, contrast, hover/focus/active/error rendering, 10%-speed motion and reference pixels are **not verified**, as browser acceptance is deferred. Approve the inspected control code only, not the existing generic manager's whole lifecycle or Phase 2 design.

Final frozen eight-file regression passes **279 tests / 2,066 assertions** in **291.53 seconds**, zero failures, errors or skips. Category integrity is **34/191**, migration replay/gap preservation **3/27**, category native concurrency **4/44**, item creation **63/210**, metadata update **73/269**, laboratory ownership **4/39**, item detail **1/16**, and unchanged native inventory concurrency **97/1,270**. All manifest identities reconcile exactly once; no PHP/fixture/configuration edits, competing suites or bundling occurred during the run. Evidence: `/private/tmp/lims-category-final2-manifest.xml` and `/private/tmp/lims-category-final2.{log,xml}`. New fault tests reproduced false usage publication before the ledger guard; an initial no-op fixture compared an incomplete unsaved attribute set with a full stored row and was corrected to snapshot raw persisted evidence. Earlier PostgreSQL CASE parsing and migration-gap fixture failures were repaired before the clean final invocation; failed runs are not passing evidence. Production build succeeds in 17.54 seconds with existing large-chunk warnings. Pint, diff checks and read-only Laravel review pass.

The development database initially contained zero categories and items. Only the three authorized additive migrations were applied after test validation; read-only schema/trigger checks confirm installation and unchanged empty catalogue counts. No seeded records, password changes, account grants or dependencies were introduced. Main-lab data and existing MySQL backups remain untouched.

Open gates: replace numeric-ID/name-based classification in canonical and retained consumers, enforce approved separate equipment/material operation and read capabilities, retire duplicate writers while preserving required fields, replace unchecked/GET catalogue lifecycle paths, and resolve the separately requested item archive policy for non-zero stock or open commitments. Full PHP rerun is authorized at that broader catalogue checkpoint. Category-type foundation is verified; catalogue consolidation, Phase 1 exit and Phase 2 pixel acceptance are not complete.

### Type-aware canonical catalogue authoring and reads

`InventoryCatalogueAccess` centralizes the material/equipment permission mapping without adding a global item scope or granting permissions. The create/update actions derive their exact operation permission from locked raw category evidence and repeat fresh authorization before commit. An edit cannot substitute submitted category/type hints for issued classification. Archived categories retain their classification for existing records; an unclassified item fails closed rather than being assumed to be material. Its record and issued identifier remain unchanged, and ordinary editing still cannot replace issued category identity.

The canonical catalogue list, item-derived statistics, detail/editor targets and category choices are limited to the actor's allowed types and owning laboratory. List rows use an explicit API Resource allowlist and server-derived edit/archive capabilities. Canonical and retained legacy document paths require the exact item-kind view/edit permission, owning laboratory, inventory-item morph and `documents` collection. Canonical attachment deletion takes its identity from the URL, ignoring a conflicting body identifier. This guards access and target selection; it does not establish checked deletion, retained bytes, crash recovery or a new media-retention policy.

Duplicate `iitems`/`iequipments` POST/PUT writers, old form requests and their unused Index/Show pages are removed. Existing entry/detail/editor URLs delegate to canonical pages with an explicit kind; actual equipment-import pages remain until their separate writer is consolidated. Existing fields, records and issued category assignments are preserved. Legacy archive/restore target sets now reject mixed kinds before entering their existing loops, but these GET loops remain unchecked lifecycle gates.

Legacy exports delegate to the canonical export with a frozen type. Its dedicated Form Request validates dates, category and the optional enum filter; the exporter always requires an explicit permitted type set. Requested filters can only narrow that set. Export-only access does not grant catalogue viewing; archived categories retain their labels in exports. The unused numeric category-list argument is removed. Other search/status/type/supplier export filtering remains a separate consistency audit.

Better UI and Emil guidance informed permission-aware affordances, consistent existing controls, retained kind context on new-item links, explicit type-driven equipment fields and accurate XLSX labeling. No page layout, theme or motion redesign was introduced. The inspected capability and classification changes have no remaining actionable UI-polish findings. Existing button/field tokens and hover/focus/active/disabled/reduced-motion code were inspected; live keyboard, pending/error/empty rendering, contrast, 10%-speed motion and reference pixels are **not verified**. The shared button's existing press treatment remains a later-phase polish finding, not approval of the whole stylesheet.

### Bounded catalogue selectors and maintenance-history access

`InventoryCatalogueLookup` replaces duplicated full-row autocomplete queries with one laboratory-owned, explicit-type read boundary. A typed Form Request validates a scalar search of at most 100 characters and an optional category-type enum. Results are capped at 25, ordered by name and ID, and limited to selection fields through `InventoryCatalogueOptionResource`; notes, costs, ownership IDs, document paths and unrelated metadata are not returned. Search is case-insensitive and treats percent, underscore and backslash literally. Empty searches return no rows. A caller cannot enlarge the cap or substitute another laboratory through query parameters.

The canonical `vap-inventory.items.lookup` returns only the user's separately permitted material/equipment types; a requested type narrows access and cannot grant another kind. Retained material and equipment aliases freeze their respective type. The unused equipment/reagent alias is removed. Stock, procurement and delivery selectors use the permitted canonical union; maintenance selectors explicitly request equipment. Both legacy maintenance-history endpoints first resolve an owned target and then require its actual kind-specific view permission plus `view_maintenance_tasks`. Related category/supplier/equipment data is eager-loaded. Wrong-lab and unclassified targets fail closed.

Reagent lookup no longer assumes category ID `2`, but deliberately retains the current consumption classification until a separately requested decision is answered. Investigation found that the model's `is_reagent` accessor ignores the saved flag and guesses from category names. An archived reagent category remains visible in general catalogue lookup but is excluded from reagent-selection eligibility, matching the current consumption action's active-category check. Making the saved flag authoritative across lookup, expiry and consumption is pending approval; this slice does not silently change that business rule.

The new shared Inertia v3 `useHttp` loader keeps each selector's requests independent, cancels superseded requests, ignores stale success/failure callbacks, and disposes pending requests and their reactive scopes on page exit. Identifier-bearing labels keep code/internal-code matches visible under existing local combobox filtering and distinguish same-name items. No page layout, theme, component skin or motion values changed. Better UI and Emil guidance informed these interaction fixes, not Phase 2 visual acceptance.

### Catalogue-report type permissions and bounded inputs

`InventoryCatalogueReportRequest` authorizes the three report entry points using actual catalogue read capabilities, validates scalar filters and report-specific sort/status values, and bounds pagination to 1–100 rows. The low-stock operation also requires its existing stock-view capability, rather than treating material/equipment view as a substitute. Forged category, warehouse and laboratory filters cannot broaden the allowed item graph. Calibration remains a schedule for any permitted item carrying a next-calibration date; it is not silently changed to equipment-only. Reagent expiry retains the existing active category-name predicate instead of adopting the unanswered saved-flag policy.

Calibration and expiry use the same typed laboratory query for page rows and summary totals; category options are limited to permitted kinds. Expiry stock relations, sums, warehouse counts and warehouse filtering all verify the stock and warehouse laboratory. The new low-stock position query checks position, retained item and retained warehouse ownership, plus the actual archived-category type. Main rows, zero-stock counts, totals and chart inputs share that boundary. Archived items, categories and warehouses do not make live positions disappear or lose their authorized identifying context; matching category filters preserve that same result. Unknown catalogue classification is excluded rather than treated as either permission kind.

`InventoryLowStockResource` allowlists the identifiers, four-decimal quantities, thresholds, item/category labels, calibration warning and warehouse/location labels used by the existing screen. Private notes, costs and unrelated model fields are not serialized. No Vue/template/style edits were made, no purchase-price semantics changed, and this is not browser or pixel acceptance. The existing full low-stock collection aggregation, name-based chart grouping, item-name sort implementation and purchase recommendations still require separate process/performance review; bounded pagination alone is not a high-volume memory guarantee.

The new **25-case / 243-assertion** matrix covers material-only, equipment-only, both and no catalogue permissions; independent stock permission; rows, totals, charts and category-option privacy; foreign/forged filters; malformed scalar filters and pagination limits; current permission/membership revocation and laboratory switching; unclassified items; archived calibration context; unchanged reagent-name eligibility; and filtered/unfiltered archived stock context. The initial run exposed an eager `HasMany` versus `Builder` callback type error; the callback was corrected rather than weakening the failing assertions. Read-only Laravel review also identified the inconsistent archived-item category filter, which is fixed and explicitly tested.

Final post-format regression: **75 passed / 887 assertions across five files**, exit 0, **22.74 seconds**. The 75-case manifest reconciles exactly once against JUnit with no missing, unexpected or duplicate cases and zero failures, errors or skips. The controller, request, resource and five relevant test-file digests match before and after that final invocation. Pint, whitespace checks and read-only Laravel review pass. Evidence: `/private/tmp/lims-catalogue-reports-manifest.xml` and `/private/tmp/lims-catalogue-reports-final.{log,xml}`. No development database records/schema, accounts, credentials, permission grants, dependencies or external delivery changed. The broader catalogue consolidation/full-suite checkpoint and Phase 1 exit remain unfinished.

### Operational reports, downloads and retained ledger reads

`InventoryCatalogueRead` centralizes permitted kinds and owned item/warehouse positions for operational stock, movements and consumption. Retained item, category and warehouse rows remain eligible for authorized history; unclassified items fail closed. Nullable consumption warehouses remain valid historical evidence. `InventoryOperationalReportRequest` adds operation-specific authorization, validated scalar/date/sort/filter inputs and bounded pages. Its shared validated filters now constrain rows, summaries, chart inputs and downloads consistently, including joined valuation queries. Zero balances remain outside the existing positive-stock valuation/low-stock download definitions; this slice does not change those quantity semantics.

`InventoryOperationalReportResource` explicitly projects page rows. User options and most-active-user statistics also project only ID/name, rather than relying on selected SQL columns to hide `User` appends. Archived movement types remain in displayed labels, inbound/outbound totals, downloads and recent activity. Daily averages divide the scoped count or unreversed consumption by inclusive selected dates, falling back to the actual matching first/last dates when bounds are absent; empty data returns zero. The legacy ledger register shares the same lab/kind boundary and supports literal, case-insensitive search over quantities and retained item/type/warehouse names. Installed Laravel 13 already casts PostgreSQL LIKE columns to text; the earlier reviewer warning about numeric ILIKE was withdrawn, not presented as a repaired defect.

CSV exports prefix formula-like text cells while preserving signed numeric quantity/value columns, and use an explicit CSV escape argument compatible with PHP 8.4. Low-stock severity now narrows downloads. The dashboard requires stock and catalogue view rights, separately gates recent movements and pending transfers, and scopes material/equipment stock and consumption. The mixed-order indicator is deliberately `null`, rather than revealing orders whose retained lines have not yet received an approved disclosure rule. Matching read/export-kind intersection is implemented and tested in the shared service, but is not imposed at HTTP entry until the separately requested export-permission choice is answered. Existing report downloads currently require their operation/read-kind permissions. Neither pending decision grants accounts or changes permission definitions.

The new **60-case / 500-assertion** matrix covers four kind-grant combinations, operation permissions, independent export-scope preparation, page/aggregate/chart/option privacy, laboratory switching and current membership/permission revocation, forged filters, invalid scalar/date/order/page inputs, retained labels, inclusive historical date averages, CSV formulas and fractional quantities. Cross-lab item/warehouse mutations are rejected by existing composite foreign keys; tests assert those database failures and unchanged readable records instead of disabling constraints to manufacture inconsistent fixtures. Early fixture omissions and a temporary synthetic-Request/helper type mismatch were fixed before final verification and are not passing evidence.

No competing suites, source edits or bundling occurred during the final invocation; no frontend changes or authenticated browser/pixel acceptance are claimed.

### Consumption register, retained detail and authorized intake navigation

The separate reagent-consumption register and detail now share `InventoryCatalogueRead` with operational reports. Reads require `view_inventory` plus at least one permitted catalogue kind, then restrict every row to its owning laboratory and allowed material/equipment classification. Register input uses the existing typed operational-report request, including validated dates, scalar filters, allowed sort columns and pages capped at 100 rows. Filtered unreversed data drives net totals, grouped summaries, inclusive daily averages and the actual highest-consumption day; reversed records remain visible without increasing net quantities. The average calculation is now shared with operational reports instead of duplicated in the controllers.

The two reagent resources explicitly project identification, quantity, stock and retained actor/reversal evidence. Detail alone includes the selected internal code, brand/model/serial and supplier ID/name. They do not expose complete account profiles, appended signature URLs, item costs, private notes or owning-laboratory fields. Retained item/category/unit/warehouse/operator/supplier relations remain readable; one combined eager-load declaration preserves the parent `withTrashed` callbacks. Item archive state is a boolean, not a raw deletion timestamp. Other retained location-directory behavior remains outside this bounded change.

Creation retains the existing `add_reagent_consumption` authorization and name-derived reagent eligibility pending the requested policy decisions. It supplies only active own-lab reagents with a known material/equipment type, minimal owned stock/warehouse choices and the signed-in operator's ID/name. It no longer lists every staff account. This is not a claim that creation requires catalogue/stock view rights: the user has not answered whether those additional read capabilities should be required. Recording/reversal still use their separately approved operation permissions; no account grants were added.

The server selects an authorized successful-write destination: the consumption register for users with its read capabilities, intake for add-only operators, otherwise the dashboard. The Vue form no longer overrides that response with a second register visit. Back/cancel uses a server-derived register or dashboard URL. Form defaults initialize the signed-in operator and restore it on successful reset; changing reagents resets only dependent warehouse/quantity choices, not the technician already entered. The dashboard now hides stock-alert counts for unreadable catalogue kinds and users without `view_inventory`, so the write-only fallback does not leak stock counts.

### Integration equipment classification and retained selection

`IntegrationHubController` now uses the existing explicit `equipment()` scope for selector choices and its lab-constrained retained equipment relation. Archived categories retain their established type; archived items are not new choices. The relation returns only item identity fields and an archive boolean, rather than a full inventory model. Wrong-lab, material and unclassified links are not projected. No stored link, classification or existing record is rewritten by this read repair.

`StoreIntegrationConnectorRequest` retains the existing equipment-or-settings manage permissions and checks the bound connector's owning lab before validation. The submitted item must belong to the active directly joined lab and have equipment classification. An exact unchanged archived equipment ID can be retained; a different archived ID cannot be attached. Omitted links remain unchanged and ordinary explicit null clearing remains valid. Integer shape validation runs before the database existence rule, including malformed array input.

Read-only review exposed a separate metadata-edit regression: filtering an old invalid relation made the form submit a blank ID and silently clear it. The response now exposes only `equipment_link_unavailable`, not the hidden ID or item details. The actual Inertia submission transform omits a blank ID while editing that flagged connector. Choosing eligible equipment submits its ID normally. Unavailable-link removal is deliberately not offered through this blank state; a future explicit unlink operation would need separate intent. Ordinary unflagged clearing and new connector creation keep their existing behavior.

### Checked catalogue archive/restore and reachable recovery

`SetInventoryItemsArchived` consolidates canonical and kind-specific item/equipment lifecycle writes. Shared request/internal validation requires a distinct positive-integer list of 1–100 IDs, rejecting boolean, fractional, malformed and overflowing identifiers before database lookup. The action locks the lab, exact membership, actor, sorted own-lab roots and retained categories; complete-batch kind and current permission checks precede writes. Archived categories retain their explicit immutable classification. DELETE/PATCH replace legacy GET mutations, and only lifecycle bindings include archived roots. Replays preserve timestamps and do not duplicate audits. Existing ability to archive referenced/nonzero-stock items is unchanged while its separately requested policy remains unanswered.

Checked lifecycle writes, intended root attributes, laboratory-tagged audits, previous activity and related stock/procurement/consumption/maintenance/media rows are verified before commit. `InventoryItemEvidence` is reused by metadata edits rather than duplicating their graph. Read-only review identified a reversal linked through a retained movement but another item's consumption; both links are now included, with archive/restore fault tests. Normal soft-delete keeps media rows and stored file bytes. This does not promise filesystem recovery after arbitrary destructive hooks or crashes, preservation of every unrelated lookup/history row, or cooperation from every internal/raw writer.

Catalogue activity now requires a live owning lab, eligible direct member and the exact material/equipment view permission, including archived roots/categories. Network visibility does not widen it. Canonical lists expose an active/archived filter and correct restore-only archived capabilities. The actual shared confirmation component's props/events replace the prior incompatible named slots; confirmation freezes identity/operation, blocks pending replacement/cancellation, rechecks current capabilities and keeps failure/refresh feedback visible. Unsupported archived exports and active-only detail/edit links are suppressed. Return navigation accepts only the exact generated catalogue-index URL or its query string, otherwise using the named index; external/detail/path-extension Referers cannot redirect successful lifecycle operations elsewhere.

The initial six tests reproduced the unchecked canonical deletion veto and four GET mutation paths. Expanded fixtures were corrected to order counters by `scope_hash`, preserve fractional JSON types, mutate category metadata rather than its database-locked type, and refresh retained reversal rows before exact ordered-array comparisons; these fixture faults are not application regressions. The final frozen ten-file PHP run passes **305 tests / 1,931 assertions**, exit 0, in **103.27 seconds**. It includes **57 integrity/read/transport cases / 228 assertions** and **10 native PostgreSQL cases / 106 assertions**. Workers actually wait on the common lock, then observe committed or rolled-back competing archival, permission revocation and membership removal; reversed batches replay without duplicate evidence. An independent connection verifies archive and restore visibility only after real outer commit, and exact root rollback without surviving audits. All manifest identities reconcile once, with no failures/errors/skips, and all 26 relevant source/test digests remain unchanged. Forty-nine frontend checks, build (15.71 seconds), Pint, whitespace checks and bounded Laravel review pass. Evidence: `/private/tmp/lims-item-archive-final-manifest.xml`, `/private/tmp/lims-item-archive-final.{log,xml}`, `/private/tmp/lims-item-archive-native.log`, `/private/tmp/lims-item-archive-frontend.log` and `/private/tmp/lims-item-archive-build-final.log`.

### Transactional connector authoring and fresh equipment eligibility

`SaveIntegrationConnector` consolidates HTTP create/update and direct internal authoring. It uses the established lab → exact membership → actor boundary before loading the fresh live connector by its owning lab and numeric identity. A submitted nonnull equipment ID then locks the item and category, checks lab and explicit equipment type, and permits an archived item only if its ID equals the freshly locked connector's current FK. An omitted field preserves the current association, including an unavailable retained link; explicit null clearing remains supported. Fresh active/verified direct membership and the existing `edit_iequipments || edit_settings` contract are checked before and after writes. No new impersonation restriction or ownership/grant policy was introduced.

`IntegrationConnectorValidation` supplies the same rules to the Form Request and action. Only validated authoring fields enter the write; laboratory, creator, UUID, ingest hash and signing secret are supplied by the action. Configuration filtering, blank-credential preservation and nonblank merging now operate on the freshly locked root. Generated global keys retain the suffix convention while respecting the 80-character column; explicit valid 80-character keys are preserved. A specific database key collision returns a field validation error instead of an unhandled error. Per-lab locking does not promise automatic global suffix allocation across unrelated labs.

Creation freezes the complete current-schema connector intent, including database-defaulted health, archive and nullable fields, before model hooks. The token hash is included in that checked root save, not rotated after a separate commit. Initial mapping and required laboratory-tagged audit are persisted and checked in the same transaction. JSON fields are compared as values, encrypted fields remain encrypted, and physical/expected column counts must agree. This deliberately fails closed if a future migration adds columns without updating the authoring contract. Existing root metadata is loaded raw and preserved outside the validated changes. A truly identical update does not save or touch the root timestamp, while the existing per-request audit behavior remains. This does not claim preservation of every unrelated integration child or prior audit row against arbitrary internal writers.

The first HTTP fault run reproduced **20 false-success responses and one unhandled 500** across root/mapping/audit vetoes, changed item ownership/archive state and late authority changes. The first post-change fixtures also assumed middleware would not update `last_activity_at`; they now initialize a current activity timestamp and retain the full actor-row assertion. Read-only review then identified omitted database-defaulted root fields and a no-op timestamp regression; both were repaired and have dedicated tests. The native audit count initially used a class name instead of the configured morph alias; the tests now use `getMorphClass()` and keep the required audit-count checks rather than dropping them.

Final frozen post-format regression passes **125 tests / 978 assertions across six files**, exit 0, in **50.61 seconds**, with zero failures, errors or skips. It includes **37 integrity/direct cases / 321 assertions** and **12 native PostgreSQL cases / 150 assertions**. Native workers actually wait on the held lab-first PostgreSQL boundary, then observe committed or rolled-back equipment archival, archived-link reassignment, connector archival, permission revocation and membership removal. A distinct connection proves connector, mapping, token hash and audit visibility only after the real outer commit, with no surviving evidence after outer rollback. Disposable schemas/processes are cleaned by the tests. All 125 manifest identities reconcile exactly once with JUnit and all 15 relevant source/test digests match before and after; no source edits, competing suites or bundling occurred during the final invocation. Pint, tracked/untracked whitespace checks and bounded read-only Laravel review pass.

There was no frontend change, so no new build, browser interaction or visual acceptance is claimed. ### Checked warehouse authoring and shared retained evidence

`SaveInventoryWarehouse` replaces direct controller create/update with one checked action. It acquires the common laboratory lock, raw membership/account and sorted retained warehouse rows, validates inside the lock, then obtains fresh operation permission. It freezes metadata and timestamps before model hooks, checks actual persisted roots, freezes the new lab-private audit, and rechecks authority plus root/reference/history evidence before returning. Both `{iwarehouse}` and `{warehouse}` PUT contracts now work; the request captures its target through an inert raw read, and the action re-reads it after acquiring the common lock.

### Checked stock adjustments and opening-position graphs

Three original adjustment cases reproduced unsafe commits after stock/batch save vetoes or an unpersisted ledger. A fourth type veto already rolled back through a database error; it now fails through the checked persistence boundary. `AdjustInventoryItemStock` validates direct calls, locks laboratory → membership → actor before the raw item/warehouse/position/evidence graph, and rechecks fresh active/verified authority before commit. Item ownership, archive state and unit come from the locked current row, not a stale bound model. Quick increment/decrement also freeze the requested inventory ID and reject a replacement position. Their archived-item relation is read with trashed records so the action returns 404 instead of receiving a null typed argument.

Intended saves are frozen before model hooks. Exact stock, optional batch, new movement and required transaction-type writes are checked, including save vetoes, timestamps, full retained attributes and fixed-ID evidence moved outside its original scope. New rows use one instant for created/updated timestamps, and existing batches do not acquire an unsupported updated_at column. Existing four-decimal quantities, batch-reserved availability, positive submitted ledger magnitudes, add/remove/set semantics and prior/new balance notes are unchanged. Request-UUID replay policy and duplicate-submission guard semantics are not changed.

`CreateInventoryPosition` moves the stock-register opening path under the same lab-first authority boundary before any position is written. It retains `add_inventory`, rather than silently requiring edit permission; zero opening creates no ledger. A positive opening uses the checked adjustment and exactly one initial-stock movement. Numeric-string IDs reproduced a valid-input intent mismatch and are normalized before construction. The returned position has correct persisted balances, synced originals and the normal recently-created marker. Canonical catalogue-item creation remains a separate authoring path and is not claimed repaired here.

The canonical adjustment HTTP endpoint now uses a typed Form Request instead of an inline controller rule set. Existing JSON result/error fields and the duplicate-submission guard remain. Fifty-two integrity cases pass **120 assertions**; they cover checked/late persistence faults, invalid direct quantities, fresh authority, replacement identity, archived quick-route rejection, stale item eligibility, add-only/zero opening, graph preservation and the final type read.

Twelve new native cases expand the inventory concurrency file from 42 to **54 cases / 745 assertions**: adjustment versus transfer fractional contention, parallel batch adjustments, two labs reaching the same missing type's insertion boundary, five waiting authority revocations, both opening-position/warehouse-archive winner orders, and independent-connection outer rollback/commit visibility for adjustment and positive opening. Workers demonstrably wait on PostgreSQL locks; owned processes and private schemas are cleaned. The common-lock guarantee applies to these canonical actions, not every inventory writer.

### Checked canonical catalogue-item creation and private new documents

`CreateInventoryItem` replaces unchecked controller writes with direct-call validation, the common laboratory → local membership → actor lock boundary, locked current directory/warehouse rows and checked exact item/position/ledger saves. Submitted values beyond the migrated decimal precision fail validation rather than being silently rounded. Archived scoped items are included in reservation/evidence checks. Positive opening persists exactly one `stock_in` movement per position with reason `Existências iniciais`; zero opening has no movement. Shared missing types use a nested savepoint and committed unique-collision reread. Same-scope retained items, stock, batch/consumption/transfer history and document rows are locked and compared by fixed identity before commit.

The original flow reproduced successful commits with a vetoed ledger, altered item identity/sequence/metadata, changed opening quantities and late collateral writes. Other vetoes already rolled back through database errors and are not described as unsafe commits. The active duplicate-position fault initially hit the existing database constraint; the final archived-extra-position case exercises the checked complete-child-set boundary instead. Files have private `local` storage, UUID-owned paths and server-derived owner/collection/name/type/size/order/hash evidence. Downloads require local ownership and `view_iitems`; the existing display URL resolves to the guarded download. Unmarked retained public documents retain their original ID-based paths and bytes. This does not migrate older public files, fix edit/delete file lifecycle, guarantee process-crash recovery, or claim cleanup succeeds when storage itself is unavailable.

### Checked reagent consumption and retained reversal evidence

The user approved separate `add_reagent_consumption` recording and `delete_reagent_consumption` reversal permissions on 2026-10-02, plus retained original consumption and compensating stock movements instead of destructive reversal. Both canonical recording endpoints use one Form Request and the checked `ConsumeInventoryReagent` action. Direct action calls validate independently. Sibling checked catalogue creation/update, stock adjustment and stock-position opening now retain reversal rows as part of their existing consumption graphs, with change/delete faults tested.

`ReverseInventoryReagentConsumption` rereads the source under that same boundary and checks the original negative movement's laboratory, position, item, warehouse, batch, operator, type and exact quantity. It restores only the original active position and matching batch, never a replacement for an archived position. Archived catalogue/warehouse metadata does not prevent correction of already-issued consumption. Overflow and inconsistent source/replay evidence fail without partial credit. A checked positive `consumption_reversal` movement and immutable model-level reversal record retain the reverser and timestamp; original consumption and movement rows remain unchanged. Repeated reversal requests recheck current authority and matching evidence, then return the existing reversal without additional credit.

The new reversal migration uses composite source/movement laboratory FKs and unique consumption/movement identities. Rollback refuses to discard retained reversals; empty reversal-schema down/up replay preserves issued consumption in a private PostgreSQL schema. The old canonical DELETE route is removed; reversal uses POST. Default seeding registers only the two approved permission definitions, is repeatable, and grants no account or role access. Existing grant pivots are tested unchanged.

The retained consumption register and detail remain readable with explicit reversal state. Net-consumption reports, analytics and exports exclude reversed sources. Stock-movement reports retain both ledger rows, classify the compensating movement as inbound and count signed consumption as a positive outbound magnitude; totals, daily charts and type mix are tested. This corrects the former subtraction of a negative consumption amount. It is not a generalized rewrite of every historical inventory movement convention.
