It includes **81 native PostgreSQL cases / 1,083 assertions**, covering consumption-versus-consumption and consumption-versus-adjustment overspending, duplicate reversal credit prevention, missing shared-type collision, waiting permission revocation and real independent-connection outer rollback/commit. Every race worker actually waits on the held PostgreSQL boundary; disposable schemas and processes are removed. The empty reversal migration round trip is included. The final five-file frontend check passes **30 checks**, and final production build passes in **19.91 seconds** with the existing large-chunk warning. Pint, tracked/untracked whitespace inspection and read-only Laravel review pass. The two approved permission definitions are registered in the local database, verified by a read-only query; account/role grants remain unchanged. No PHP/fixture edits or bundling occurred during the final regression. These overlapping results are not added to the complete-suite checkpoint, which the user approved and which completed against the exact 3,594-case manifest as recorded below.

The user-approved complete PHP checkpoint subsequently passes **3,592 tests, two expected skips / 26,708 assertions** across four fresh processes. All **3,594 manifest cases / 173 classes** reconcile exactly once with no failures, errors, omissions or duplicates. The actual configured-Chrome preview passes; the two missing-driver guards skip because the optional driver is installed. Complete-run details and telemetry are recorded at the top of this plan. The targeted 502-case inventory result and overlapping narrow runs are not added to these full-suite counts.

Phase 1 remains open. Broader transfer/batch/procurement and lifecycle cross-writer evidence, exact automatic activity-log persistence, raw/internal writer invariants, classification/consolidation, old-file lifecycle, crash recovery, lifetime-history cost, retained runner memory and final Phase 1 exit revalidation after remaining changes stay separate gates. No production deployment, dependency change, external delivery, credential reset, account activation or role grant was performed.

### Explicit stock classification, retained reads and truthful navigation

Stock thresholds and the equipment quick-menu action now use `inventory_type`, not category ID `1`. Both named-route and fallback navigation select the equipment kind. The stock picker already carries this allowlisted scalar; opening an existing position now seeds it from the API resource. Equipment selection clears threshold fields, materials retain their limits regardless of incidental category IDs, and an unknown or unselected type does not guess material. Creating after editing resets the new defaults before opening, so an old position ID cannot turn a new-position submission into an update. These presentation hints do not become server authority or add writable model fields; the existing request allowlist, fixed position identity, balance-update prohibition and separate material/equipment permissions remain unchanged.

One laboratory-scoped eager-load contract serves stock index/show/edit payloads. It retains archived item/category labels and immutable enum classification without serializing full related models; items remain constrained to the owning lab. Category-name search explicitly includes those archived relations and remains behind the owning position/warehouse boundary. A four-position query test verifies one item read and one category read rather than per-row queries. Resource `can_open_item` requires a live item, a known type and the matching kind's view permission. Register, retained slide-over selection and detail navigation consume that capability; stock-only read permission does not grant item/equipment dossier access, and archived items do not advertise an active-only dossier link. The missing stock-register movement icon import is repaired.

| Severity | Location | Before | After | Why |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| HIGH, repaired | `Pages/Inventory/Index.vue` create/edit state | Create reset used previous edit defaults, potentially retaining the issued position ID | New defaults are applied before reset | A new-position action cannot accidentally submit an update |
| MEDIUM, repaired | Stock threshold controls and `Components/quick-menu.vue` | Category ID `1` stood for equipment | Explicit material/equipment type governs controls and both navigation modes | Classification survives arbitrary category IDs; unknown type is not guessed |
| MEDIUM, repaired | Stock register/detail/slide-over and `InventoryResource` | Links used material-only permission or advertised inaccessible archived dossiers | Shared exact-kind, live-item server capability controls links | Truthful affordances preserve equipment-only access without widening permission |
| MEDIUM, repaired | Stock read resources and category-name search | Archived labels disappeared or could not be found by their displayed name | Laboratory-scoped retained labels/type and consistent archived-relation search | Readable stock history remains searchable without peer-lab disclosure |
| HIGH, open | `InventoryController::edit` standalone entry | Renders `Inventory/Edit`, but that Vue page is absent | Recorded for canonical stock entry-route consolidation; not repaired in this slice | HTTP/resource assertions do not establish a renderable page |

better-ui and emil-design-eng influenced functional truthfulness, stable task state, permission-matched navigation and reuse of the existing stock editor, not a visual redesign. Approve only the inspected and programmatically tested type/state/navigation contracts. Authenticated rendering, keyboard/focus return, hover, responsive/theme contrast, physical-device behavior and screenshot/reference comparison remain **Not verified**. The standalone entry and broader stock metadata/lifecycle safeguards remain open; Phase 2 pixel matching stays separate.

The initial tests reproduced missing classification/capability payloads, archived labels and four frontend behavior failures before implementation. Final new feature file passes **33 cases / 602 assertions**, including both kinds across active/archived read relations, six exact-kind permission cases, peer-lab isolation, unknown-type handling, retained category search and query batching. The final post-Pint eight-file regression passes **176 cases / 1,787 assertions**, exit 0, with no failures, errors or skips. The four-file frontend regression passes **52 checks**; production build passes in **16.10 seconds**, with the existing large-chunk warning. Pint, scoped whitespace checks and read-only Laravel review pass. Overlapping singular runs are not added to the regression count or the earlier complete-suite result. Evidence: `/private/tmp/lims-stock-type-regression.log` and `/private/tmp/lims-stock-type-build.log`. No development records, schemas, credentials, account grants, dependencies or global PHP settings changed. Remaining raw CSV/read consolidation and independently approved business-policy gates are not treated as finished.

### Canonical stock entry routes and recoverable authoring state

The standalone `/inventory/create` and `/inventory/{inventory}/edit` handlers no longer render absent Vue pages. Both route to the existing `Inventory/Index` slide-over through validated create/edit intent. The index requires `view_inventory` to return a list; an add-only or edit-only operator may open the matching authoring context, but gets an empty stock list and no stock metrics. Edit context loads only the exact active position in the active owning lab. Peer, archived and missing positions are unavailable. The shared index request keeps route identity authoritative for legacy redirects, rejects malformed/overflowing IDs and conflicting intent, and validates query shapes before query construction. Neither view permission alone nor an unrelated authoring permission opens another operation.

Existing item-kind and warehouse lookup permissions are preserved. `add_inventory` alone does not enumerate those selectors; creating through the UI additionally needs permitted material/equipment read access and `view_iwarehouses`. The form explains this requirement and prevents an unavailable selection flow from being submitted. A dedicated HTTP test verifies actual selection and canonical stock-position creation with those existing grants, while add-only entry tests verify the selector denials. No users or role grants were changed and no new cross-kind or network visibility was inferred. Editing a known position remains possible with edit-only permission; its issued item and warehouse display as labelled read-only fields, while threshold submission retains the existing no-balance-replacement rule.

The editor opens from primitive route-intent/record-ID watchers, so a preserved-state save response with the same ID does not reopen it. Pending writes prevent draft replacement, picker mutation, duplicate submit and manual panel closure. HTTP/network failures leave input visible with persistent feedback. Read-only review caught a success-ordering issue: installed Inertia calls user success before clearing `processing`, so routing success through the new pending-close guard would leave the panel open. Success now closes directly, and a behavior test explicitly invokes it while still processing. Existing slide-over/field/alert components and tokens are retained; this is functional foundation repair, not a Phase 2 redesign.

| Severity | Location | Before | After | Why |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| HIGH, repaired | Stock create/edit handlers | Successful HTTP payloads referenced missing Vue pages | Validated redirects open the existing canonical editor | Entry routes now resolve to a compiled component rather than a nonexistent page |
| HIGH, verified | Stock index authoring contexts | No working add/edit-only entry without stock-list access | Exact operation grants, one requested own-lab position, empty non-view list and hidden metrics | Authoring access does not silently grant stock enumeration |
| MEDIUM, repaired | Stock identity controls | Item/warehouse looked replaceable although server identity was immutable | Labelled read-only fields and persistent lock explanation | Affordances match issued identity and avoid predictable validation failures |
| MEDIUM, repaired | Pending/error/success state | Draft could be replaced or closed during submission; generic errors lacked local recovery | Disabled pending closure/submission, persistent failure feedback and success-specific closure | Stable task state with truthful result handling and no optimistic stock mutation |
| MEDIUM, explained | Create selectors | Add-only users saw an unexplained empty selector | Explicit existing read-permission requirement and unavailable-flow guard | No permission broadening just to make a picker appear usable |

better-ui and emil-design-eng guided these capability-matched controls, identity explanations and recoverable task state. Approve only the inspected programmatic route, permission and state contracts. Authenticated runtime rendering, focus/keyboard recovery, hover, responsive/device behavior, theme contrast and screenshot/reference comparison remain **Not verified**. Browser verification is still deferred. Stock metadata/lifecycle final-persistence safeguards and remaining GET mutations remain separate Phase 1 gates.

The new entry file initially reproduced **22 failures / one passing membership-denial case**. It now passes **25 cases / 141 assertions**. The prior classification matrix now follows edit redirects and checks the canonical destination's retained resource, rather than treating a missing-page HTTP payload as browser success. Final post-format affected regression passes **73 cases / 973 assertions**, with no failures/errors/skips. The four frontend files pass **55 checks**, including real Vue watcher behavior and success-before-finish ordering. Build passes in **21.24 seconds** with the existing chunk-size warning; Pint, tracked/untracked whitespace checks and read-only Laravel review pass. Evidence: `/private/tmp/lims-stock-entry-regression.log`, `-front.log` and `-build.log`. The approved complete checkpoint has **4,164 manifest cases / 193 classes** and uses `/private/tmp/lims-stock-entry-full-manifest.xml`, `-full.log`, `-full.xml` and `-full-before.sha256`; its outcome is pending. No competing tests, builds or source edits will occur during that invocation. No schemas, development records, credentials, account grants, dependencies, production settings or global PHP configuration were changed.

## Phase 2 — VAP visual system ("Plano" implemented; awaiting visual approval and the PHP checkpoint)

Revised on 2026-10-03 at the user's request: the earlier pixel-match brief is replaced. The target is a unified, production-grade interface built from the VAP Brand Identity System 1.1 and the two video references, not a recolour of the previous screens.

**Approved direction: "Plano"** (approved by the user on 2026-10-03; implemented the same day). The flat design language of motion.dev carrying the VAP brand, designed from scratch. The proposal stays in `output/design-proposals/motion/` (`system.css` is the reference the implementation is ported from; `01`–`06` sheets and `png/` renders).

Decisions made with the approval, now implemented:

- **Light is the default theme.** Dark is an explicit choice (account menu or Shift+D) remembered per user on the server and per browser; the system preference no longer switches it. `:root` carries light, `:root[data-theme='dark']` and `.dark` carry dark (`.dark` keeps Tailwind's `dark:` variants working).
- **Eight areas.** Início, Amostras, Análise, Certificados, Comercial (Clientes merged in), Inventário, Qualidade, Admin.
- **Laboratory colour lives only on the laboratory seal** in the area column (Plano structure sheet: "A cor própria de cada laboratório fica só no seu selo da coluna"). Branding no longer recolours actions in the shell, portal or sign-in; status colours never followed branding. The branding dialog is now "Selo do laboratório". White-label name and logo still apply.

### What is in place

1. **Tokens, type and Tailwind theme.** `app.css` ports the Plano tokens verbatim as `--pl-*` (light and dark) and re-values the stable `--ds-*` API onto them, so every screen written against the semantic contract inherits Plano. TASA Orbiter and Geist Mono (OFL, variable `woff2`, latin + latin-ext) are self-hosted in `resources/fonts`; Inter and JetBrains Mono are removed. Root is 16px, body 14px. Tailwind's radius scale is 0 and its shadow scale is empty; a base-layer rule enforces zero radius everywhere (including arbitrary `rounded-[…]`), drop shadows, gradients and backdrop blurs from older utilities are neutralised. The grey scales are remapped to the Plano neutrals.
2. **Shell.** `Shared/Navigation/area-bar.vue` is the 64px area bar (brand, eight areas with a sliding underline, ⌘K search, notifications, "Receber amostra", account menu with the ⇧D theme toggle and the laboratory seal dialog). `app-sidebar.vue` is the 240px text column (laboratory switcher with its seal, the area's groups under mono headings, catalogues folded under "Catálogos"); `side-nav.vue` slides the accent plane with `layoutId`. Older screens get an automatic path line (area / trail). ≥1280px shows all zones; 768–1279px folds the column into a "Menu" drawer; <768px moves the areas into a bottom bar of four areas plus Menu. The command palette had no open/visit handlers in the Bancada shell (⌘K threw); both are implemented. The Bancada rail, column, sheet, hero flattening into a band and `AnimatedIcon` are removed.
3. **Shared components.** Buttons (mono caps on a 1px frame, inverting on hover, 0.97 press), fields (layer plane, strong hairline, inset accent focus, striped disabled), mono field labels (also applied to older `label[for]`), checkbox, diamond radio, square switch, chips with a square dot, tabs, state cells, pager, tables (mono headers, 46px rows, 2px accent edge on hover), menus and option lists, dialogs with a colour strip (red only for the irreversible), flush slide-over, bottom-right toasts with a state edge, dashed empty states, skeletons, blinking loaders (nothing spins), calendar and chart defaults (flat bars, 1.5px strokes, dashed grid, mono axes). New template parts live in `Components/plano`: `PageHeader`, `StateCells`, `NextStepBar`, `StatusChip`, `Journey`.
4. **Templates and rebuilt screens.** Today (`LaboratoryWorkbench`: blue band, ink-on-paper hero, work tiles, bench table, workflow bars), queue (`VAPSamples/Queue`: header, state cells, filter row, table, pager, slide-over quick view), dossier (`VAPSamples/Show`: path, journey, tabs, facts rail, next-step bar), report (`LabNetwork/Index`), sign-in and every Fortify/portal auth page (`AuthExperienceShell`: band, ink panel, journey along the foot), Inertia and Blade error pages, and the client portal layout. The queue controller now returns per-status `counts` for its cells (same grouped query as Today; feature test added).
5. **Module pages.** All other pages resolve to Plano through the contracts: the first hero card of an older page becomes the page header (frame removed, 42px title, decorative icon tile hidden), uppercase labels become mono labels, and every control, table, dialog and menu takes the Plano geometry.

### Verification

- Production build passes. Case-sensitive imports that only resolved on macOS (`Pagination.vue`, `Modal.vue`, `@/composables`) are corrected so the build runs on Linux.
- Frontend suite: **425 pass / 0 fail**. The contract tests describe Plano instead of Bancada; the eleven F1 cases are reconciled with the approved Phase 1 behaviour (validated export button, consumption reversal, archived documents, fractional deliveries, laboratory-owned customer dossier, prepared quote lines). The `design-system-contract` file had case-mismatched `VapLabels` paths and could not load on Linux; corrected. F1 is closed.
- Renders of Today, queue, dossier, network, sign-in and the customer directory in light and dark at 1440×900 and 390×844 are in `output/plano-implementation/`. They come from a preview harness that mounts the real built pages with fixture props (no backend), so they show layout and states, not real records.
- **Not yet run:** the PHP suite. This environment has PHP 8.3 and no route to install 8.4, and the application requires 8.4. Changed PHP: `LaboratorySampleQueueController` (status counts), `LaboratorySampleQueueTest` (new case), `ErrorPagesTest` (Plano fonts and mark). Run `php artisan test --compact tests/Feature/LaboratorySampleQueueTest.php tests/Feature/ErrorPagesTest.php` and then the complete suite.

### Mobile, theme and layout pass — 2026-10-04

- **Theme.** A sun/moon toggle sits in the staff area bar and the portal header at every width (account menu and ⇧D remain); the portal had none. `theme-color` follows the chosen theme from first paint, so phone browser chrome matches the canvas.
- **Phones.** Pinch-zoom is no longer disabled; touch screens get 16px fields so focusing does not zoom. The bottom bar shows glyphs with short names, and the current area always holds a slot. The drawer carries "Receber amostra". The portal gained the same bottom bar and a drawer "Novo pedido". Toasts, next-step bars and selection bars clear the bottom bar and the safe area.
- **Tables.** Below 640px every `DataTable` stacks each row into a labelled record (labels read from its header). Reading matrices opt out (`:stack="false"`), and spanning headers or self-stacking registers keep their grid. Row actions stay visible on touch screens.
- **Layouts.** Filter state cells become one swipeable strip on phones. Header actions stack with the primary action on its own row. Next-step actions wrap. Billing command bars stay at the foot and wrap. Pages whose earlier pass had put stat cards or ledes into `PageHeader` slots (maintenance dashboard, tasks, categories, task detail, nonconformity detail, products, vehicles, workflow, ISO revision, movement, proficiency, trade certificates, collection correction) now use the header, fact cells and next-step bar. The maintenance counts filter the task list. Path crumbs name places, not "Voltar…". Untranslated server crumbs ("VAP Maintenance", "Tasks") are labelled.
- **Tablet and desktop.** The area strip keeps the current area in view and fades whichever edge hides more; all eight areas fit from 1280px.
- **Verification.** Every one of 241 parameterless staff routes renders at 375px without horizontal overflow (in-browser audit). Spot checks covered light and dark at 375, 820, 1280 and 1440. `plano-shell-theme-mobile` passes 15 checks. The frontend suite shows only the 43 stale pre-Plano pins recorded earlier. 85 affected PHP tests pass. The portal shell was not checked in a browser (no fictional portal password available; none was reset).

### Charts and analytics pass — 2026-10-04

- **One chart language.** `PlanoChart` and `Support/charts.js` now draw every live chart: one categorical palette in a fixed order (VAP Blue first, never cycled; a ninth series folds into "Outros"), one blue ramp for magnitude, and status tones only where a series means good or bad. The palette was validated for colour-vision deficiency and contrast on both Plano canvases. Legends are HTML, tooltips lead with the value, whole counts never show fractional ticks, donuts show at most six slices beside a value list, and every chart offers its data as a table. Target lines are a labelled rule (`reference`).
- **Migrated screens.** Samples report, the four inventory reports, inventory analytics, nonconformity trends, proficiency tests (index and detail), ratings, maintenance dashboard, proposal detail and notification analytics. Their hand-built ApexCharts options and per-page dark-mode plumbing are gone.
- **Unlike units.** Consumption, stock-movement and inventory analytics charts used to add quantities across items with different units. They now plot counts of records or positions; quantities stay per item. Server payloads gained the count series additively.
- **Custom charts.** `/analytics` ("Análises", in the Início area) is a personal board per laboratory. A builder picks dataset, measure, grouping, optional split, form, period and colours, with a live preview; combinations that do not fit are disabled with the reason. Definitions are whitelisted in `app/Metrics/ChartDatasets.php` (samples, nonconformities, occurrences, maintenance, consumption, movements, invoices, proposals), gated by the same permissions as their modules, scoped to the active laboratory and computed live. Saved charts (`custom_charts`) belong to their owner and laboratory. Chosen colours that readers would confuse raise a warning.
- **Verification.** `CustomChartBoardTest` (14 cases, including every dataset × measure × grouping against the schema) and the four changed report tests pass: 52 tests, 697 assertions. `plano-charts` passes 9 checks. The board and the builder were checked in the browser on desktop and at 375px, in light and dark, signed in as the seeded demo account. The phone check led to three changes: wrapped segmented controls keep a divider between rows, fieldset legends have space below them, and below 900px the preview leads the builder and stays pinned while the fields scroll under it (drawn 150px tall, capped at 46% of the screen height).
- **Stale pins rewritten.** With the user's approval, the 48 pre-Plano markup pins (45 tests in `design-system-contract`, one each in `counter-analysis-mutations`, `financial-observation-form` and `notification-template-overrides`) were rewritten one by one to the element that now does the same job. None were deleted. One pin exposed a real gap: the chart's data view was a raw `<table>`; it now uses the shared `DataTable`. The frontend suite passes 565 of 565.
- **Full PHP suite (PHP 8.5.10 CLI, eight sequential batches; one process exhausts the 512M limit in a worksheet zip download).** 4,673 passed, 2 skipped, 3 failed, none from this pass. Two `PhpTestLauncherTest` cases are the environment-only failures recorded earlier and still fail here. The third, `PortalDocumentBoundaryTest` (`foreign_collection` case), could not build its fixture because `quality_certificates_live_collection_unique` (migration `2026_10_03_160806`) allows one live certificate per collection; with the user's approval the fixture now archives the other certificate before reusing its collection, and the file passes 30 of 30. The complete suite was not re-run after that one-test change.
- **`PhpTestLauncherTest` repaired (2026-10-04).** The two cases were not environment-only on this machine. PHP 8.5 prints the paths of `php --ini` inside double quotes; `scripts/run-php-tests.php` read the quoted default scan directory as a relative path, so the launcher would have dropped the default `conf.d` on 8.5. The launcher and the test's own helper now strip the quotes, and the first case asserts the default directory survives. The other case counted every case in the JUnit report against the compile-source list and broke when `PhpCompilationCompatibilityTest` gained a second test in the Phase 5 checkpoint; it now counts the compile cases by name. Launcher file 20 of 20; `composer test` runs through the launcher. The earlier OPcache explanation applied to a different PHP build and was not re-checked. Full PHP suite afterwards, on `1e651bacd` plus this fix (PHP 8.5.10, eight batches): **4,675 passed, 2 skipped, 0 failed**.
- **Removed afterwards.** The six unused ApexCharts wrappers (`Components/apex-chart/*`, `Components/charts/inventory-dashboard.vue`); ten Chart.js components in `Components/charts/` with no importers and their only support file `Composables/useChart.js`; and the two `ReagentConsumption` pages, which no controller renders (the route redirects, the old endpoints answer 410). The global `apexchart` tag and `applyChartDefaults` left `app.js` (PlanoChart loads the library itself and sets every option), and the catalogue lookup composable lost its unused `reagentsOnly` option; the server endpoint `iitems.getReagentInventoryItem` was then retired as well (route, controller method and the lookup service's `reagentsOnly` flag); `vap-inventory.items.lookup` is the one catalogue lookup and still returns reagents with `is_reagent`. `resources/js/ziggy.js`, a generated snapshot the application does not load at run time (three frontend tests read it), was regenerated with `php artisan ziggy:generate`: 1,231 routes, 14 stale names out, 14 current names in. The legacy `ChartConfiguration` model is unused and untouched.

### Generated documents — 2026-10-04

Basis: ISO/IEC 17025:2017 clause 7.8 (reporting of results) and the Portuguese accreditation body's application guide (IPAC OGC001), which fixes the statements a report carries. The earlier PDFs were page-like layouts: a four-page test report with the results on page three, a chart of workflow states, general phrases in place of statements, and a different visual design per document.

- **One document system.** `App\Support\ControlledDocument` and `PDFs/partials/premium-document-style` give every document the same control elements: letterhead with the laboratory's identity, a control box (title, unique number, revision, issue date), a verification QR code, a running header, a footer with "Página x de y" on every page, numbered sections that leave out what was not recorded, an authorisation block and a marked end. White page, hairline tables, tables only for layout, so mPDF and Chrome draw the same page.
- **Test report (`Relatório de Ensaio`).** `App\Support\TestReportContent` builds it in the order of 7.8: customer (7.8.2.1 e), item and its condition on receipt (g), sampling (k, 7.8.5), dates of receipt, performance and issue (h, i, j), results with method, unit, uncertainty, limits and appraisal (f, m, 7.8.3.1), statement of conformity with its decision rule (7.8.6), observations, statements (l, 7.8.2.2, reproduction in full), authorisation (o) and end mark (d). A report that is not authorised, or that contains unapproved results, says so at the top and names nobody. An amended report states the revision, its date, the reason and that it replaces the earlier version (7.8.8). Customer-supplied data is flagged "(c)". A result is appraised only when it is numeric and has a limit; the rule printed is the one applied (simple acceptance) unless the intake recorded another.
- **Report Studio defaults.** All nine document types (test report, executive summary, proposal, export and import certificates, proforma, invoice, receipt, credit note) use the letterhead, running header and footer; the commercial bodies are parties, lines, totals beside payment details, and signature. There is one default per type (`ReportStudioDefaultTemplates`); the duplicated fallbacks in `ReportStudioPdfBuilder` (about 560 lines) are gone, and a saved template that leaves a surface empty inherits the default. The Studio canvas receives the same stylesheet.
- **Other documents on the shared page** (`PDFs/partials/controlled-layout`): sample receipt, sampling term (now portrait), bench worksheet, sample disposal record, contracting guide, nonconformity record and list, purchase order, internal requisition, maintenance report and calendar, revision history and comparison, inventory reports. None carries styles of its own.
- **No filler text.** Label-as-value fallbacks ("Local do serviço", "País de origem", "Pessoal autorizado") and invented observations ("Factura emitida com base no documento comercial aprovado…") no longer print: an unrecorded value shows "—" or its line is left out. The real proposal's item and total tables use the shared table classes.
- **Fixes found on the way.** The bench worksheet's barcode was EAN-only and failed on alphanumeric laboratory codes (now Code 128). Chrome left a blank band above the first-page header. Result methods read a column that does not exist (`standards.name`); the code and description are used.
- **Tests.** `TestReportContentTest` (16 cases, clause by clause, including the appraisal rules and escaping) and `ControlledDocumentTest` (7). The preset, table-style, nonconformity and contracting-guide pins that described the old layouts were rewritten in `ReportStudioWorkflowTest`, `NonConformityReportingTest`, `PortalDocumentsDemoTest`, `report-studio-css` and `maintenance-reporting`. Every document was rendered with both renderers and inspected as an image, from the Studio previews and from development records.
- **Fields added afterwards (same day)** for what the report could not state:
  - **Laboratory address (7.8.2.1 b):** the setting is `app_client_lab_address`, for where the laboratory works. The letterhead of every controlled document uses it and falls back to the organisation's address.
  - **Accreditation:** the settings are `app_client_lab_accreditation_body` and `app_client_lab_accreditation_number`.
  - **Per test (7.8.2.1 p, 6.6):** the profile definition (`parameter_profile`) holds `accredited`, `subcontractor` and `uncertainty_coverage_factor`. They travel in the issued analytical scope and are copied onto the result when it is inserted, so a later catalogue change does not rewrite issued reports.
  - **Deviation from the method (n):** analysts record it on the result (`results.method_deviation`) at insertion, verification or approval.
  - **How the report states them:**
    - The report marks tests outside the scope with `*` and subcontracted tests with `(s)`, naming the provider.
    - The accreditation statement is printed only when a certificate number is configured and at least one test is within the scope.
    - Results recorded before these fields (`accredited` null) are not claimed.
    - Deviations get their own numbered section.
    - The uncertainty is called expanded only with a recorded k: stated once when all results share it, in each cell otherwise.
  - **Not printed on other documents:** the accreditation stays off the shared letterhead, so commercial documents make no claim.
  - **Still open:** the fields are empty on this install. Fill the settings and mark each profile's tests before reports state them.
- **Left as they were:** the user manual (a brochure, not a record) and labels.
- **Removed afterwards (63 files):** the earlier per-document views nothing rendered any more (`PDFs/invoice`, `quote`, `receipt`, `creditnote`, `proposal`, `proposals`, `export_certificate`, `import_certificate`, `analysisreport*`, `includes/analysisreport/*`, `sample_label`, `proposals/pdf`), their partials `document-letterhead` and `brand-logo`, and the two Studio body fallbacks (`studios/executive-summary-body`, `studios/proposal-body`) with the unreachable branches that loaded them. Still present and used: `multiple_sample_analysis`, `sample_labels` and the four label views the label renderer loads.
- **Public development routes retired.** `/sgs`, `/cast`, `/labels` and `/multiple-sample-analysis` answered without sign-in (one returned a database record, one a legacy report template). They are removed with `PDFs/sgs_report_template`, and `GuestDebugRoutesTest` keeps them out. The two views with no reachable caller (`reports/batch_genealogy`, `PDFs/labels/pdf`) are removed too.
- **Accreditation path checked end to end.** A development certificate was rendered with fictional accreditation data inside a transaction that was rolled back: the letterhead carries the laboratory address, tests outside the scope are marked `*`, subcontracted tests `(s)` with the provider, the uncertainty is stated as expanded with k = 2, and the deviation has its own section. Nothing was saved. The development install now holds fictional values marked `[DEMO]` (address, accreditation body and number; the two demonstration tests marked accredited with k = 2), chosen by the user so demo reports show every element; the saved demonstration certificate prints them in both renderers. **Before production, replace them** in Settings and on each profile's tests with the laboratory's real address, accreditation and scope: a `[DEMO]` value must never reach an issued report.

### Document logo and control charts — 2026-10-04

- **Logo on generated documents.**
  - **Upload:** Settings → "Informação institucional do laboratório" → "Logótipo dos documentos" uploads a PNG or JPEG (up to 1 MB, 32–4000 px). It goes to the public disk under `branding/`, and its path is kept in the `app_document_logo` setting.
  - **Endpoints:** its own `generalsettings.document-logo.update` and `.destroy`, so the main settings form never overwrites it. It needs `edit_settings`, the path is locked like the other settings, and replacing or removing the logo deletes the previous file only after the setting is committed.
  - **On the documents:** every controlled document embeds it as data, fitted to 38 × 18 mm with the size written on the element (mPDF ignores `max-width`). When it is missing, documents fall back to the brand logo URL, then to nothing.
- **Control charts (ISO/IEC 17025 7.7.1; ISO 7870-2; Nordtest TR 569).** Quality → Controlo → Cartas de controlo.
  - **Chart types:**
    - Mean chart (X): CL ± 2s warning, ± 3s action limits.
    - Range chart (R) of duplicates: UWL 2.512 R̄, UAL 3.267 R̄, no lower limits.
  - **Limits:** entered with their basis, or computed from at least 10 included points (20 recommended). Every change is kept in the activity log and shown as the limit history.
  - **Out-of-control rules:** a point outside the action limits; two of three outside the same warning limit; seven steadily rising or falling; ten of eleven on one side of the centre line. A point between the warning and action limits is a warning.
  - **Corrections:** a recorded value is never edited. A wrong one is excluded with a reason, and stays on record outside the rules and the computed limits.
  - **Corrective action:** recorded with who and when on each point out of control. Points still without an action are counted on the list and on the chart.
  - **PDF:** a controlled record with the drawing (SVG both renderers draw), limits, statistics, points, actions and the rules.
  - **Code and tests:** `App\Support\ControlChartEvaluation` (pure, tested in `tests/Unit/ControlChartEvaluationTest`), `ControlChartDocument`, `ControlChartController`, and the `ControlCharts/Index` and `ControlCharts/Show` pages. Workflow, permission and laboratory-scope tests are in `ControlChartManagementTest`. Permissions are `view|add|edit|delete|restore_control_charts`; a migration creates them and gives them to `admin`.
  - **PlanoChart:** reference lines now always fit inside the value axis. A line chart can show its points and colour some of them by state (`points`); its limit labels sit at the right end.
- **Quality control samples feed the charts (same day).**
  - **Marking:** a catalogue product can be marked "Material de controlo interno" (`products.is_control_material`). Every sample of it is a quality control sample, and the sample page says so and names the charts it feeds.
  - **Chart side:** a chart names its control material (`control_charts.control_product_id`, only marked products are accepted) and its parameter.
  - **Feeding:** `ControlChartFeed` turns each approved, numeric result of that parameter, on samples of that material received in the chart's laboratory, into a point. A range chart takes the first two approved results of a sample (analysis and counter-analysis) as duplicates.
  - **When it runs:** on approval (`FeedControlCharts` listens to `AnalysisResultsApproved` and `CounterAnalysisResultsApproved` after commit; a failure is reported and never undoes the approval), when the chart is saved, and from "Trazer resultados aprovados".
  - **Each result plotted once:** points keep `result_id` / `paired_result_id` / `sample_entry_id`, unique per chart, so a result is plotted once and an excluded point does not come back. Manual entry stays available.

### Report Studio canvas checked in the browser — 2026-10-05

Checked signed in as an administrator, on a new model of the test report and of the invoice, with and without a theme.

- **A new model starts from the system document of its type.** The editor used to open on an older hard-coded template, so saving a new model as default brought the old report back. New, reset and edited models now take the server's document for the type as their base, and changing the type of an untouched model brings that type's document with it.
- **The canvas shows what the preview prints.** The server sends the values each type's fictional preview is printed with (`canvasSampleValues`; never a customer's record, shared values such as the logo once), so every token of every system document is filled on the canvas: letterhead, control box, verification code and the test-report sections.
- **Application styling stays out of the document.** The application's table rule (14 px, 46 px rows) and its typography wrapper (28 px table margins) reached into the canvas and bloated the letterhead. The document is now shown with the document styles alone.
- **A theme restyles, it does not replace the letterhead.** One click on a theme used to swap the controlled letterhead for a decorative header and leave an unfilled token. A theme now keeps the controlled letterhead, running header and footer; with a theme applied the PDF is still the controlled document.
- **QR codes are always square.** The letterhead code had a fixed height, so on a narrow canvas its width was squeezed and it showed 63 × 79 px. Every QR code is now sized by its width alone (`ControlledDocument::squareCodeStyle`: height follows, `aspect-ratio: 1 / 1`), on documents, proposals, both Studio canvases and the two record screens. Measured in the PDFs of the test report, invoice and proposal: 21.0 × 21.0 mm in mPDF, 20.9 × 20.9 mm in Chrome.
- **Known limit:** the canvas shows one page per `<pagebreak>`; a body longer than a page is cut at the page edge there. "Pré-visualizar PDF" shows the real pagination.
- **Tests:** `ReportStudioWorkflowTest` (canvas values for every token of every system document; a draft from the system document previews as a PDF) and `report-studio-css.test.mjs` (starting document, value order, table rule, body wrapper, themes).

- PHP checkpoint above.
- Not yet reviewed in a browser against real data: report studio, label studio, worksheets, public proposal page and portal pages behind a customer login (the file manager and inventory were rebuilt on Plano on 2026-10-03, see below). They inherit Plano through the contracts; page-specific layouts (nested panels, per-page stat strips) may still need individual passes.
- Four native-control surfaces remain deliberate exceptions listed in `design-system-contract.test.mjs`.
- Generated PDFs keep their document typography; they are documents, not interface, and are outside this phase.

Exit: the representative screens are approved in light and dark at desktop and mobile sizes, the open items above are closed or explicitly deferred, and the complete PHP and frontend suites pass.

## Phase 3 — Core laboratory journey

Deliver this phase as small, reviewable workflows:

1. Shared customer selection → laboratory-private proposal → approval/version history. Verify totals, attachments, exports, and customer pages do not reveal another laboratory's proposals.
2. Accepted proposal or authorized direct intake → sample registration → collection/accession → assignments. Remove duplicate entry, make next actions clear, and preserve traceability and laboratory-specific numbering.
3. Assigned analysis → result entry → review/approval → controlled report. Define permitted transitions, rework, amendments, and audit history. Prevent repeated submissions from creating duplicate records or reports.

Extract transactional business actions from controllers, use validated inputs and consistent resource payloads, and apply Inertia form helpers or standalone HTTP requests according to the interaction. Use deferred data only with explicit loading states; use optimistic changes only when safe and reversible.

Exit: realistic workflows succeed with two laboratories, shared customers, different roles, invalid inputs, retries, and concurrent changes. Browser checks confirm the same flows in the approved UI.

Approval gate: proposed changes to scientific review, report approval, retention, or other consequential business rules require explicit agreement before implementation.

### Phase 3 status — 2026-10-03 (Claude Code)

Decisions agreed by the user on 2026-10-03 under the approval gate:

- **Three-person result review.** Whoever inserted a result cannot verify it; neither the inserter nor the verifier can approve it. Replays of one's own stage remain idempotent. The sign-in page now claims "Três etapas" instead of the earlier unbacked "Quatro olhos".
- **Certificates come only from the workflow and lock once validated.** Manual certificate creation is removed; identity (customer, site, product, laboratory code, accession) is fixed; only the observation is editable until validation; after validation corrections go through the existing ISO revision workflow.
- **Signing on behalf of an absent validator stays** (user request): the signed-in validator signs and stays accountable, and the absent validator is recorded alongside. The absent validator must be an active member of the same laboratory with validation rights and cannot be the signer.

Journey 1 — shared customer → private proposal → decision/versions:

- Totals are recomputed on the server (client totals ignored); a revision keeps the previous version's values and items in history; a repeated revision is refused (409) until the new version is sent; the public decision accepts once.
- Laboratory B cannot open, revise or find laboratory A's proposal; the shared customer's page shows B nothing of A.
- Proposals created outside the authoring form (imports, demo data) had no public link token, so they could not be sent and their page crashed. The model now always assigns one; a migration backfills missing tokens without changing tokens already shared.
- Proposal and laboratory-workflow searches are case-insensitive.
- Plano queue (state cells, search), dossier (journey from proposal to issued report, items and server totals, compliance evidence, version history, customer link, one next step) and create/revise forms (two-column fields, wider item rows). The dead "save draft" and quick-add controls, the misleading "expected code" tile and the unused activity chart query are removed.

Journey 2 — accepted proposal or direct intake → registration → accession → analyses:

- The intake page receives samples and corrects exactly one issued intake (opened from the sample dossier, `?edit=id`, laboratory-scoped). It no longer loads every sample, chart and shortcut: the register is the sample queue and trends are in sample reports.
- The discard picker is bounded (200 eligible samples, longest retained first) and can be opened from the dossier with the sample preselected; other laboratories' samples are never offered.
- The sample dossier offers "Corrigir registo" and "Registar descarte" to users holding those permissions.

Journey 3 — analysis → results → review → certificate:

- Certificates are laboratory-private (list, open, PDF, validate, lookups), including those of archived accessions.
- One live certificate per accession (partial unique index plus row locking); repeated generation returns the same certificate.
- Validation requires every result behind the certificate to be approved, is idempotent for the same validator and refuses a second validator. The dossier shows the same release rule; the validation dialog lists each result with who inserted, verified and approved it and when.
- Results entry tells a reviewer up front when they inserted or verified the rows.
- Archive/restore of certificates use DELETE/PATCH instead of GET.

Evidence:

- `LaboratoryJourneyEndToEndTest` drives the whole chain over HTTP with two laboratories, a shared customer and six people (commercial, reception, analyst, verifier, approver, releaser) plus a peer in laboratory B: invalid inputs, retries, four-eyes refusals, on-behalf signing rules and every cross-laboratory read and write.
- Browser check (dev server, real data, two-laboratory account): from laboratory B, laboratory A's proposal, sample and correction URLs return 404; from laboratory A they open. Proposal, certificate and intake screens checked at 1440px, in dark, and at 390px.
- 404s raised before the Inertia middleware (route-model binding, for example another laboratory's proposal) used to render a blank page because the error page had no shared routes; the exception handler now shares them (regression test in the journey test).
- PHP suite (complete run on the Phase 3 source, PHP 8.4.11, PostgreSQL): **4,218 passed, 3 skipped, 2 failed (31,994 assertions)**. One failure was a sample-access test still reading the intake register this phase removed; it now asserts the bounded payload and the cross-laboratory 404 (20/20 pass). The other two `PhpTestLauncherTest` cases fail only in this environment: its hand-built PHP loads OPcache as a shared extension that the launcher fixture's ini does not load; the launcher and its test are unchanged by this phase. Changed files after the run were re-run (error pages, access, accession, smoke, portal, queue: all pass). Frontend suite: 426 pass. Production build passes.
- This also closes the Phase 2 PHP checkpoint.

Still open in Phase 3:

- Intake form options (all customers, all products with profiles and parameters) still load in full; they need searchable endpoints before customer and catalogue volumes grow.
- Proposal and certificate numbering per laboratory remains with the later numbering slice (sample numbering is already per laboratory and year). Certificate codes are still timestamps.
- Analysis queue and results bench keep their Phase 2 layout; they work but are not yet rebuilt on the Plano templates.
- Concurrency for certificate generation and validation is covered by locking and the unique index; there is no multi-process race test for them yet.

### Inventory and document manager on Plano — 2026-10-03

The Inventário area (items with equipment/reagent views, item form and dossier, stock positions, needs, purchase orders, transfers, reagent consumption, expiry, calibration schedule, analytics and the four reports) and the document manager now use the Plano queue, form, dossier and page templates: one page header with its own crumbs, state cells only where they filter, one next-step bar per dossier or form, European Portuguese copy, tables that scroll inside their panel. Decorative stat tiles, duplicate action panels, readiness side cards, English kickers and decorative charts were removed.

Behaviour fixes found on the way:

- Purchase-order statuses are stored in uppercase, so the queue's pending and open-line counts were always 0 and the order value included cancelled orders; comparisons are now case-insensitive and sorting accepts only the offered columns.
- The item dossier read model accessors the payload does not carry (stock status, expiry, calibration days), so stock chips were blank and always "ok", an expired reagent read "Dentro da validade" and calibration warnings never fired; it now uses the values actually sent.
- Days to reagent expiry are whole calendar days (Carbon 3 returned fractions on screen).
- The order form's supplier/item/warehouse locks never applied (`disabled` instead of the combobox's `disable-input`); a task card never showed its current status; the low-stock pager never appeared; the file list drag image used `innerHTML` with the file name.
- Error pages raised before the session starts no longer throw while sharing props (regression from the early-404 fix).
- Decision (user, 2026-10-03): the low-stock report and its download also list positions with no stock, first and labelled "Sem existências"; severity buckets no longer overlap.

Evidence: full PHP suite 4,221 passed / 3 skipped / 1 environment-only failure (`PhpTestLauncherTest`, OPcache loaded only through this build's php.ini) before the last three changes; the inventory/document/procurement files were re-run after them. Frontend 427+ pass. Every reworked screen checked in the browser with a `[DEMO]` inventory and document set (created through the application's own endpoints in the development database only), in light, dark and at 390px, with no horizontal page scroll.

Still open: the item dossier controller still computes chart data the page no longer draws (two tests use it to check laboratory-scoped figures); the needs/orders controllers likewise still send unused `charts`; the analytics "áreas" rows use whole-inventory figures while the metrics strip follows the filter; the file manager's whole-page drop overlay is never activated (pre-existing).

## Phase 4 — Network stock and summary visibility

Explicitly started at the user's request after the Phase 1 handoff. This does not reopen Phase 1 or authorize Phase 2 implementation.

Approved indicator policy: exact stock-position and replenishment-alert counts per visible laboratory; active-sample counts shown only as `0`, `1–4`, `5–9`, `10–19`, `20–49`, or `50+`. Active means `POR_INICIAR`, `EN_PROGRESO`, or `EN_PAUSA`, excluding archived samples. These whole-laboratory indicators are independent of material filters, have no sample/customer drill-down, and are not included in stock exports. A replenishment alert means the sum of usable quantities in a position is at or below its reorder point. No quantities with different units are summed.

Implemented: the network page and CSV share `LabNetworkStock`, a validated read-only query with explicit stock/item/warehouse laboratory equality. Search supports laboratory, warehouse, material/code, lot, expiry interval and availability; pagination counts are scoped before counting. Each lot partitions the position balance, with a separate unallocated remainder. Physical balance is never duplicated across lots. Committed positions report reserved quantity; expired/held/inconsistent quantities are blocked. Batch allocation alone does not make a valid lot unavailable. Pending outgoing transfers are reported separately because their quantity has already left the recorded physical balance. Inconsistent batch totals expose only the recorded position balance with zero availability. The application date controls expiry; expiry today is still valid.

Freshness distinguishes the query time from the nullable last position-write timestamp; neither claims a recent physical count. Explicit API Resources exclude costs, observations, customer/sample details, record mutation links and attachments. HTML/Inertia and CSV responses are private/no-store; authorization is queried afresh and streamed export rechecks it at execution and between bounded chunks. No shared data cache, new database table, mutation permission or cross-laboratory transfer workflow is introduced.

Final focused verification: **72 PHP tests / 934 assertions**, zero failures, errors, skips or risky tests, across `LabNetworkStockTest`, `LabNetworkAccessTest`, `LabNetworkManagementTest` and `InventoryCataloguePermissionTest`; **six frontend checks** in `tests/Frontend/network-stock.test.mjs`; production build **23.74 seconds**, Pint and source whitespace checks pass. The build manifest references 612 existing files. PHP ran through the unchanged project launcher and 512 MiB limit; this Herd binary ignores `PHP_INI_SCAN_DIR`, so a temporary process-only `PHPRC` file enabled CLI OPcache while retaining the Herd configuration. No global configuration was changed. Final logs: `/private/tmp/lims-phase4-network.log`, `/private/tmp/lims-phase4-network.xml`, `/private/tmp/lims-phase4-frontend.log`, `/private/tmp/lims-phase4-build.log`. The user chose focused verification rather than another complete PHP run.

Coverage includes lot partition/expiry arithmetic, reservations and already-deducted transfers, inconsistent/zero/fractional balances, all approved sample-count bands, scoped filters and pagination, CSV formula escaping and private headers, revocation before streaming and on subsequent/partial requests, laboratory moves/switching, separate catalogue authority, and absence of inventory writes. Frontend checks cover exact four-decimal formatting, dependent filters, server-derived status, duplicate-submit protection, stale-detail removal, keyboard focus restoration and Vue compilation. Live browser reaches staff sign-in; authenticated network-screen/device verification has not been performed. Shared UI controls and existing theme tokens are reused; Phase 2's global visual-system work remains separate. Changes are local and uncommitted; no Phase 4 publication or deployment was requested.

- Provide network-scoped material search by laboratory, warehouse, item, and relevant lot/expiry fields. Distinguish physical quantity, reserved/blocked quantity, available quantity, units, and data freshness using verified inventory semantics.
- Limit main-laboratory indicators to agreed aggregates. Do not expose proposal prices, customer-specific work, individual sample results, or private attachments through drill-downs or exports.
- Keep visibility permissions separate from local inventory mutations. Reservations, cross-laboratory transfers, and changes to another laboratory's records require separately approved workflows and permissions.
- Test unrelated networks, removed memberships, laboratory switching, exports, pagination totals, and cache separation.

Exit: a main-laboratory user can find material availability in its network without gaining unauthorized operational access. Indicator definitions and any small-count disclosure rules receive user approval.

### Phase 5 closure contract — scope frozen 2026-10-04

The user requested sensible boundaries because this phase has continued too long. This closure contract takes precedence over broader Phase 5 audit suggestions and historical statements that every unverified variant must be completed here. Phase 5 establishes representative functional workflows for all seven retained areas; it does not certify production readiness. Moving a release check to Phase 6 does not approve its omission from the first release.

**Do not start another module-wide audit, redesign, migration consolidation, dependency replacement, hypothetical race investigation or new policy feature.** Preserve completed evidence; do not re-open a passed workflow without a relevant source change or a reproducible contradiction. Previously answered policy choices stay answered, including separate nonconformity verification permission without a two-person rule.

Only these five closure packets remain:

Use the previously requested permission decision; do not invent authority. A revoked actor/membership, wrong lab or missing operation permission must cause no receipt, stock, ledger or dossier writes. Existing successful/replay/concurrency checks must remain green. Quarantine is a separate release-policy decision, not an invitation to redesign receiving here. |
| P5-C2 | Bounded quality ownership/assignment check | Verify one lab-owned dossier's assignment choices and writes cannot target an ineligible/foreign-lab assignee, and new dossier identifiers cannot collide in the existing scope. Repair only a reproduced defect in these two contracts, with a named regression. Retain the current numbering presentation unless the user approves a change. Do not add configurable numbering or a new assignment engine. The already verified resolution → verification → closure workflow is not re-audited. |
| P5-C3 | Finish the current commercial browser packet | Verify all six existing observation-only editors using fresh fictional fixtures; demonstrate one HTTP/network failure with retained text, retry and reload. Compare issued core/lines before and after. Quote creation/revision and the existing financial integrity regression are retained evidence. Do not turn this into every document variant, full mobile editor redesign, production fiscal-signing setup or live customer delivery. |

Do not drain the existing unreviewed queue backlog or send external messages. Retain existing after-commit, rollback, eligibility and template-recovery regression evidence. Stop after this representative delivery passes. |
| P5-C5 | Final exact-state checkpoint and handoff | Freeze a test manifest for these packets and their affected module regressions; run it on the final source state, all frontend checks, production build, required formatting and scoped whitespace checks. Reconcile failures and expected skips without weakening assertions. Record the source fingerprint, remaining release holds and seven-area acceptance matrix. After C1–C4 and this checkpoint pass, declare Phase 5 functionally complete and stop; do not start Phase 6 automatically. |

Packets are finite acceptance contracts, not permission to continuously append adjacent work. Any newly found defect must have reproducible evidence that it prevents one of C1–C5. Otherwise place it in the Phase 6 release backlog. A genuine critical security/data-integrity finding remains a release blocker and must never be silently waived; request a specific scope amendment if it does not fit these contracts. A pending consequential decision is reported once against its packet; it does not justify expanding unrelated work.

These remain explicit release holds where applicable. All seven module families remain release requirements. No production deployment is authorized.

Current closure status: **C1–C5 PASS; Phase 5 functionally complete. Stop here.** This is not production release approval, full visual-spec parity or permission to begin Phase 6 automatically.

Fixed C5 file manifest, to run after C1 is implemented on the final source state: `PurchaseOrderIntegrityTest`, `InventoryReceivingConcurrencyTest`, `InventoryProcurementLaboratoryOwnershipTest`, `NonConformityAuthoringTest`, `NonConformityLaboratoryBoundaryTest`, `NonConformityLifecycleTest`, `FinancialObservationDemoTest`, `IssuedFinancialDocumentIntegrityTest`, `TradeCertificateAuthoringTest`, `BillingSourceLaboratoryOwnershipTest`, `LaboratoryNotificationCommitTest` and `NotificationTemplateTest` (all under `tests/Feature`, `.php`). The case-level manifest and source fingerprint must be captured after the approved C1 tests exist, before the checkpoint; reconcile the run against that manifest. Add only tests directly covering the C1 change if a new dedicated test file is necessary. Also run all `tests/Frontend/*.test.mjs`, production build, Pint and scoped whitespace checks. Retain earlier unchanged maintenance, portal and administration workflow/browser evidence; do not restart those audits. The complete PHP suite remains the explicit Phase 6 release checkpoint.

Approved maintenance rule: bulk completion requires an existing nonblank result for every selected task, rejects the whole batch if any result is missing, and leaves already-completed tasks unchanged on retries. No invented completion evidence.

Approved portal rule: new portal service requests require a lab-issued invitation and have one immutable laboratory owner. Invitations are bound to a verified portal site and its current customer, expire after 30 days, and are consumed atomically with one request. Reissuing an available invitation is idempotent. Only eligible members of the owning lab with request-view permission are notified, after commit. Existing unowned requests remain stored but are excluded from lab queues, lookup, export and intake; no ownership is guessed.

Working acceptance matrix (all seven representative workflows remain required; the closure packets above define the remaining Phase 5 work, while broader variants/policies are explicit Phase 6 release holds):

Current frontend checkpoint supersedes the older twelve-failure notes below: a complete run found **16 stale contract failures**, then passed **all 489 frontend checks** after reconciliation and repairs. Contracts now enforce retained consumption reversal and dossier/document archive history, canonical download controls, issued request identity, approved sample-detail geometry, lab-private customer context and four-decimal dispatch quantities. The native CSV picker remains an explicitly tested labelled control. No tests were removed, and no retired destructive workflow was restored to satisfy an old assertion.

One live defect was repaired during that reconciliation: reagent-consumption PDF export used an Inertia visit for a binary response. All five inventory reporting entry points now share CSRF-protected ordinary downloads with duplicate-submit guards, pending feedback and persistent recoverable errors; existing GET downloads keep their behavior. Quote catalog-derived lines normalize array/resource responses before preparing prices, including explicit zero. Customer dossier wording now directs users to permissioned document areas rather than claiming lab ownership is absent. Focused export/report PHP regression passes **62 tests / 556 assertions**. Final production build passes in **16.32 seconds** with existing chunk warnings, and source whitespace checks pass. No PHP source changed in this reconciliation slice.

Browser export verification confirmed the consumption page title, disabled pending button, retained search filter after a locally simulated 422, and a successful retry producing an actual one-page PDF while remaining on the same page. Recovery screenshot `output/playwright/phase5-consumption-export-retry.png` was visually inspected; transport evidence is `output/playwright/phase5-consumption-report.pdf`. PDF contents/layout were not re-audited because report rendering was unchanged. The known unavailable realtime websocket remains; the browser's 422 entry is the intentional test response. Full PHP suite, physical-device/complete keyboard/motion checks and the commercial/portal-document representative browser workflows remain outstanding.

| Quality/nonconformity | Open a lab-owned record, assign corrective work, update and review it without losing action history | Representative workflow and browser evidence retained; bounded assignment/number checks pass under C2. No further Phase 5 quality audit. |
| Equipment/calibration | Schedule work, record its result, complete once and update calibration dates atomically | Representative authoring/completion/lifecycle/browser evidence retained. Completed-evidence correction policy is a Phase 6 release hold, not another Phase 5 redesign. |
| Purchasing/inventory | Submit a need, review every line, approve/reject once, convert approved quantities into one purchase order, receive stock | Decision/conversion, receiving recovery/replay/rollback/reference/native-contention and browser evidence retained. C1 current actor/operation authorization passes. Quarantine policy is a Phase 6 release hold. |
| Commercial documents | Author/revise a lab-owned draft, use guarded financial workflows, preserve issued evidence | Quote creation/revision and financial integrity regression retained; six observation-editor browser save/recovery checks pass under C3. Fiscal/payment rehearsal and broader device/spec coverage are Phase 6 release checks. |
| Customer portal | Sign in as a customer site, submit a lab-invited request and see only that site's authorized documents | Representative invitation/request, private staff queue, six document libraries/PDFs, search recovery, private-download denials and navigation evidence retained. Contract-guide ownership/legal-policy work remains an explicit Phase 6 hold. |

General-settings security checkpoint: an explicit API Resource now excludes the stored signing private key from both initial HTML and Inertia JSON. The shared branding payload was already safe. Private-key replacement is write-only; omitted, null or blank input preserves the stored key. Validation failure no longer flashes the key into session old input. The form clears replacement input after validation, HTTP, network or cancellation failure, preserves ordinary draft fields, prevents pending submissions and restores safe server-derived defaults after success. Read-only viewers receive no editing controls. A persistent error summary exposes validation failures from inactive settings tabs. Better UI/Emil guidance informed visible recovery and configured-state feedback without changing the approved design direction.

Final settings verification passes **9 PHP tests / 91 assertions**, **511 frontend checks**, production build (**18.67 seconds**, existing chunk warnings), Pint and scoped whitespace checks. Browser verification used real demo viewer/admin sign-ins: the viewer received no private-key prop or editor; the administrator recovered from rejected validation and a simulated HTTP 503, then saved unchanged settings without sending a key. The entire persisted settings digest stayed unchanged. The final cross-tab validation retry showed a readable email error while Security remained selected, cleared the dummy replacement key and was cancelled without a write. Screenshots `output/playwright/phase5-settings-readonly.png` and `phase5-settings-validation-summary.png` were inspected. Initial browser-selector mismatches were corrected; they were harness errors, not application failures. Current browser errors remain the unavailable local realtime websocket.

The user reconfirmed that separate verification permission is sufficient for nonconformities; no two-person rule applies. The unchanged lifecycle regression passes **15 tests / 152 assertions**, including one authorized operator resolving, verifying and closing. Global settings still use the existing `edit_settings` policy pending the separate system-administrator-only decision. Key rotation semantics, broad accessibility/device coverage and the complete PHP suite remain open. This checkpoint does not complete Administration or Phase 5.

Settings persistence follow-up: the controller now delegates to `SaveGeneralSettings`. It locks the general-settings rows in deterministic order, re-fetches the active verified actor, shares request normalization/validation, loads fresh settings under the lock and verifies the persisted result before a final authority check. Omitted values and blank private-key input cannot be overwritten by an old scoped settings object. A changed locked property rejects the entire patch; identical saves do not dispatch save events or rewrite rows. Exceptions, unexpected persisted values and authority loss roll back the whole save. Temporary settings objects are not installed into the container, and the prior scoped binding is discarded afterward.

General settings explicitly use the existing database repository. That repository now disables value caching independently of the default cache setting: Spatie's load/save events otherwise publish values before the outer transaction commits. Events remain enabled; no package was replaced or modified. The action fails closed if value caching or a separate authorization/settings connection is configured. No development settings, permissions or schema were changed by this slice.

Post-format verification passes **31 PHP tests / 234 assertions** across persistence, native concurrency, secret security, landing/settings and white-label messaging, in **18.61 seconds**. Six native PostgreSQL cases prove real lock waits, commit/rollback preservation of omitted contact/key values, permission/activation/verification revocation while waiting, and visibility only after a real outer commit through an independent connection. Disposable schemas/processes are cleaned. Pint, scoped whitespace checks and read-only Laravel review pass. No frontend source changed in that slice. The following revision checkpoint supersedes its stale-browser-draft gap.

Settings revision follow-up: HTTP and direct saves require an opaque HMAC of the complete settings snapshot and property-lock state. It includes the private key only inside the server-side calculation and uses a versioned purpose prefix; neither the key nor an encoded settings snapshot is sent to the browser. This is a state fingerprint, not a monotonic history version. After row locking and current authorization, a changed stale draft is rejected before persistence. An identical lost-response retry remains a no-op. The token is never stored as a settings property. Inertia retains the ordinary draft and its original token on every failure; fresh redirect props do not silently authorize an overwrite. Both save buttons stay disabled on a revision conflict. Explicit discard resets values and token together from current server props, and replacement-key input remains cleared on errors.

Final revision verification passes **43 PHP tests / 294 assertions** in **21.84 seconds**, including the updated six native PostgreSQL cases; **512 frontend checks**, production build (**16.95 seconds**, existing chunk warnings), Pint, scoped whitespace checks and read-only Laravel review pass. Missing/malformed/tampered tokens, private-key-only intervening changes, lock-state changes, retained input, atomic rejection and no-op retries are covered. The real browser exercised a deliberately invalid revision through the actual server, retained the draft, disabled both save controls and discarded through keyboard activation. Concurrent commits themselves are tested in PostgreSQL, not claimed as a two-browser test.

Browser verification also found that an implicit grid track expanded to **482 px** inside a **290 px** content area at the 320 px viewport. An explicit shrinkable single-column track, a shrinkable navigation item and wrapping footer controls fix the clipping; shortening the discard label alone did not. Final measurements show a 290 px track, 250.5 px input and 258 px error summary within the viewport. Desktop/mobile screenshots at `output/playwright/phase5-settings-conflict.png` and `phase5-settings-conflict-mobile.png` were inspected. Better UI/Emil guidance informed static, persistent conflict feedback and reachable controls without adding motion or changing the approved visual direction.

| Severity | Location | Before | After | Why |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| HIGH | `resources/js/Pages/SystemSettings/Index.vue` | Stale drafts could overwrite newer configuration | Conflict feedback retains drafts and requires explicit discard before a fresh edit | Recoverable state; no silent overwrite |
| HIGH | `resources/js/Pages/SystemSettings/Index.vue` | Implicit mobile grid clipped fields and error text | Explicit single-column sizing and wrapping actions fit at 320 px | Reachable controls and readable recovery |

Approve only the inspected conflict, discard, focused-control and viewport states. Full keyboard traversal, hover/active/theme/physical-device coverage and slow-motion replay are **Not verified**. Local realtime remains unavailable; the full PHP suite was not run.

Browser-test incident and recovery: the first fault-injection handler removed itself before continuing the request, accidentally allowing a valid save of a demo application name and dummy signing key. Browser writes were stopped immediately and the user was informed. The original key was recovered from the existing local key file. Before restoration, the candidate complete configuration fingerprint was checked against the pre-test browser snapshot; after restoration through the guarded action, the fingerprint matched exactly. All configuration values and property-lock state were restored; save timestamps advanced and were not fabricated back to their former values. Subsequent browser checks kept an invalid email as an independent server-side write blocker until the interceptor simultaneously replaced it and injected the invalid revision. They completed without altering configuration values. Future global-settings fault injection must use isolated fixtures or independent rejection safeguards; a request interceptor alone is not sufficient protection. Global-write policy, signing-key lifecycle and remaining Phase 5 module gates stay open.

Administration/notification checkpoint: seven focused PHP files pass **180 tests / 1,057 assertions** across membership, shared-account boundaries, template ownership, administrative delivery, inbox access, notification ownership and operational broadcast. A subsequent template-save repair replaces unchecked `updateOrCreate` with a locked explicit save, verifies persisted editable fields and server-owned identity, and rechecks current operator authority before commit. Vetoes, unexpected persisted changes and membership revocation roll back the transaction; unchanged retries avoid another model write. The expanded template file passes **25 tests / 145 assertions**, including nine new cases. Final template/real-commit-worker regression passes **55 tests / 580 assertions** in 73.97 seconds; these counts overlap. Pint and source whitespace checks pass, and independent read-only review found no actionable defect in this patch. No schema, delivery policy or two-person verification rule changed. Browser membership/template recovery, recipient-selector eligibility/count alignment and truthful queued-versus-delivered feedback remain outstanding; these modules are not declared complete.

Notification audience follow-up: new composition now uses the same active, verified, non-archived direct-membership rule as queued delivery. Picker/group counts and queued recipient counts agree; invalid specific recipients reject the entire request, duplicate IDs are rejected, and the undeliverable unverified group is retired. Historical member labels and notice visibility keep their previous scope. A dedicated Form Request validates only the selected audience, so an empty individual selection no longer invalidates the normal all/group form payload. The picker aggregates unread counts instead of querying each member separately. Success feedback says queued, not delivered. Composer controls are disabled during submission, audience/indexed errors remain visible, and connection failures preserve the draft with a warning to inspect history before retrying. Better UI/Emil guidance informed visible pending/recovery feedback, reduced-motion support and retention of the approved design tokens; no visual redesign was introduced.

This audience slice passes **58 PHP tests / 372 assertions**, **493 frontend checks**, production build in **16.20 seconds** (existing chunk warnings), Pint and source whitespace checks. Browser verification confirmed the composer title, fixture-only selection, visible failure recovery and a successful retry; recovery screenshot `output/playwright/phase5-notification-composer-recovery.png` was visually inspected. The real send created exactly one lab-4 broadcast with one recipient, but no database inbox delivery existed at the read-only checkpoint; Redis contained 22 notification jobs. No unreviewed queue backlog was drained. Actual local-worker inbox/read-unread verification therefore remains open, as does the already-unavailable realtime websocket. The register also lacks a useful document title; resolve it in the remaining notification page pass. Full keyboard/responsive/motion-state coverage and the complete PHP suite were not run. The current controller issuance path still needs guarded persistence/late-authority verification before declaring notifications complete.

Notification issuance follow-up supersedes the open controller-integrity item above: `IssueLaboratoryNotification` now owns explicit-lab validation, ordered laboratory/member locks, current sender authority, complete audience validation and checked persistence. Server-owned fields cannot be submitted. Vetoes, persisted-field tampering and authority/eligibility changes before commit roll back the issuance. Dispatch is registered only after commit, rechecks current authority and limits delivery to the frozen audience. A queue failure preserves the committed issuance and returns an explicit potentially-partial-send warning; no automatic retry or exactly-once delivery claim is introduced.

The final focused notification regression passes **88 PHP tests / 482 assertions** in 19.23 seconds. Separate real-root-commit/fresh-worker coverage passes **35 tests / 489 assertions** in 74.00 seconds, including outer rollback, commit visibility, late sender revocation and broker failure before or after a partial queue insertion. Laravel intentionally does not report authorization exceptions; the test expectation was corrected without weakening the no-queue assertion. **10 focused frontend checks**, Pint and scoped source whitespace checks pass. No frontend source changed in this issuance slice, so the previous production build remains applicable. The lifecycle approval was reconfirmed by **15 tests / 152 assertions**: the same authorized person may resolve, verify and close, with a distinct permission and evidence check for each stage.

Template browser verification used only the existing fictional demo lab: an unsupported variable produced an inline validation error while preserving the draft, a valid override survived reload, restoring the shared preset removed the local override, and the original database-only local settings were recreated and verified after reload. No email was enabled or message issued during this template check. The screenshot `output/playwright/phase5-notification-template-roundtrip.png` was captured and visually inspected.
