Two automation locator waits were corrected; these were not application failures. Current console errors are limited to the known unavailable realtime websocket. Template network-failure recovery, meaningful notification page titles, membership browser coverage, actual development-queue inbox delivery and the full PHP suite remain open. No unreviewed queue backlog was drained, and Phase 5 remains incomplete.

Notification browser-recovery follow-up supersedes the template transport/title gaps above. Save and preset restore now expose transport/cancellation errors without discarding the draft; handled network/HTTP failures suppress Inertia's default error overlay. Composer failures use the same explicit suppression while preserving the warning to inspect history before retrying. Six administrative pages have meaningful document titles. Browser inspection found clipped mobile save controls and desktop horizontal tabs inheriting full-width sidebar styling. The action group now wraps, while scoped utility overrides preserve the shared tab tokens. Active tabs follow the reactive Inertia URL and expose `aria-current`, rather than relying on an ineffective current-route match. Better UI/Emil guidance informed persistent feedback, responsive reachability and restrained styling; no new visual direction or dependency was introduced.

Final recovery checkpoint: **496 frontend checks** pass; production build passes in **18.75 seconds** with existing chunk warnings. Scoped whitespace checks pass. A temporary custom-style approach failed the existing design contract and was replaced with existing utility conventions; no assertion was weakened. No PHP source changed, so PHP/Pint were not rerun for this frontend-only slice. Browser verification simulated one aborted save and one HTTP 503 preset restore, observed retained input and enabled retries without an error iframe, then performed successful real retries. Original demo title and database-only channels were restored. All five notification navigation destinations expose correct titles and exactly one active tab; the detail-page title is compiler-tested but not browser-verified against a delivered notification. Desktop links now fit, and the save button is fully within a 320 px viewport. Representative keyboard order and reduced-motion emulation were checked, not a full accessibility audit. Screenshots `output/playwright/phase5-template-save-recovery.png`, `phase5-template-mobile-actions.png` and `phase5-notification-navigation.png` were captured and inspected. Full hover/focus/motion timing, physical-device coverage, membership browser flow, actual development-worker inbox delivery, realtime and the complete PHP suite remain open. Phase 5 remains incomplete.

Administration membership follow-up supersedes the membership-browser gaps above. The local-only demo command now creates a fresh manager with exactly `view_users`, `add_users` and `delete_users`, plus a peer-lab target without global roles or permissions and an inactive fictional peer qualification. Existing accounts, passwords and role grants are unchanged. Membership transport/cancellation errors remain inline and preserve the entered email or pending operation; handled HTTP/network failures suppress the default error overlay. The directory has a meaningful document title, and the slide-over footer wraps with explicit button spacing. Better UI/Emil guidance informed recoverable feedback and reachable mobile actions without changing the approved visual direction.

Membership verification passes **94 PHP tests / 584 assertions**, including four demo-fixture cases, and **496 frontend checks**. Production build passes in **18.39 seconds** with existing chunk warnings; Pint and scoped whitespace checks pass. An initial fixture test lacked a request session for its direct capability call; the test context was corrected without changing application authorization. Browser verification used real manager/reviewer sign-ins, rejected an unknown email while retaining it, recovered from one aborted join and one simulated HTTP 503 removal, then completed real join/remove operations. Before/after account and peer-qualification digests match, the peer membership is unchanged, the target ends with no main-lab membership, and exactly one join and one removal audit remain. The read-only reviewer sees three members and no add/remove/edit controls. At 320 px, the save button stays within the viewport and keyboard navigation reaches Cancel. Screenshots `output/playwright/phase5-membership-recovery.png`, `phase5-membership-joined.png`, `phase5-membership-mobile.png` and `phase5-membership-readonly.png` were inspected. Qualification/configuration browser workflows, full accessibility/device coverage, actual notification inbox delivery, realtime and the complete PHP suite remain unverified. No queue backlog was drained, no schema or dependency changed, and Phase 5 remains incomplete.

Qualification preservation follow-up: unchanged submitted qualifications now retain their row IDs, timestamps and original qualifier, even when another row changes. Matching consumes each existing editable-value tuple once, preserving duplicate multiplicity. Changed/removed evidence still enters the retained qualification history; peer-lab rows, final authority checks and transaction rollback remain guarded. Identical retries no longer invent a qualification change or audit. Reproducing tests initially failed because unchanged records were deleted; they now pass, including a duplicate `2 → 1 → 2` case. One fixture snapshot was corrected to compare two database-hydrated records rather than an incomplete newly created model.

The dossier submits only changed, permitted sections, so qualification editing does not rewrite unchanged shared identity or global grants. Successful saves refresh form defaults from the returned server record before any separate password operation, preserving real IDs, status and qualifier; discard returns to that saved state. Fields are disabled during submission. Network/HTTP/cancellation feedback retains drafts and suppresses the default error overlay for handled failures. Page titles are meaningful, and blank role/permission labels fall back to their stable names. Department/role searches now settle empty queries, show recoverable lookup failures and clear those lookup errors on retry without clearing save errors. Better UI/Emil guidance informed honest pending state, persistent recovery feedback and reachable mobile controls, without a new visual direction.

Qualification checkpoint: **77 PHP tests / 869 assertions** pass across history, account boundaries, qualification ownership and native concurrency. After the final label repair, the three non-concurrency files pass **64 tests / 620 assertions**; these counts overlap. **501 frontend checks**, production build (**15.72 seconds**), Pint and scoped whitespace checks pass. The old two-argument payload source assertion was updated to require the new baseline-aware payload; behavioral checks prove unchanged identity/grants are omitted. Browser verification used an existing fictional demo administrator: blank-capability validation retained input, an aborted save disabled controls then restored retry without an overlay, and the real retry saved exactly one inactive qualification. The request contained only `personnel_qualifications`; before/after account digests and direct permissions match. Server-derived inactive status and qualifier appeared immediately. Reopening the editor starts clean, and discarding a changed note restores saved evidence. A simulated department-search 503 showed a recoverable error; a real retry cleared it and empty searches ended loading. At 320 px, there is no horizontal document overflow and both footer actions remain reachable. Inspected evidence: `output/playwright/phase5-qualification-recovery.png`, `phase5-qualification-saved.png`, `phase5-qualification-picker.png` and `phase5-qualification-mobile.png`.

Qualification validation follow-up: `PersonnelQualificationValidation` now owns the editable-field allow-list, normalization, validation rules and readable error labels used by both `UserRequest` and `UpdateStaffAccount`. The action validates after current authorization and target membership checks, before account/access/evidence writes. Invalid collections, rows, booleans, dates, date ordering, department references and oversized text are rejected with validation errors rather than reaching persistence. Omitted qualifications preserve existing rows; an empty list removes only the owning lab's rows with retained history. Sparse defaults, explicit false, duplicate entries, option values and server-assigned ownership match across both paths. Direct-call strings receive the same trimming/empty-to-null normalization as HTTP input. Existing no-op identity preservation remains in place. The initial direct-call regression reached the final persistence guard instead of returning a validation error; the repaired regression passes.

Validation checkpoint: **108 PHP tests / 997 assertions** pass across shared validation, account history, account and qualification lab boundaries, and native PostgreSQL concurrency in **67.86 seconds**. This includes **30 new cases / 123 assertions**, not an additional total. **23 frontend staff-account checks**, Pint, PHP syntax and scoped whitespace checks pass. Read-only Laravel review found no concrete defect. No frontend source changed, so this slice did not repeat the production build or browser run. The full PHP suite was not run.

Non-admin qualification browser checkpoint: fresh local fixture creation now includes a separate qualification editor with only `view_users` and `edit_users`, no role, and one main-lab membership without network/branding grants. The membership manager remains unchanged. Six distinct generated passwords and real editor login are tested; repeated fixture creation does not reset earlier accounts. In the browser, the manager added the fictional peer target through the canonical membership workflow, then the qualification editor signed in and saw only local evidence and editable qualification controls. Blank-capability validation retained the reference and explicit false state. A simulated save 503 preserved the draft, showed recovery feedback and enabled retry without an overlay. The real retry created one inactive local qualification attributed to the editor and exactly one retained audit. Shared-account and peer-qualification digests are unchanged; original peer membership and empty global grants remain. A real forged roles update returned HTTP 403. Reopening the editor is clean with Save disabled, and keyboard-only toggle changes were discarded without another write.

The browser exposed a shared `ToggleField` containment defect: its absolutely positioned screen-reader input lacked a positioned ancestor, expanding document scroll height from the 879 px viewport to 1,420 px and allowing focus to displace the app shell. Adding `relative` to its label contains the input without changing the visual design or native keyboard behavior. After rebuilding, the document returns to its 888 px desktop shell baseline; at 320 × 740 it measures exactly 320 × 740 with no document overflow. Shift+Tab reaches the toggle, Space changes it on/off, and its visible focus ring and footer remain accessible. Better UI/Emil guidance informed stable control containment and preservation of the existing tokens; no new motion or design direction was introduced. Screenshots `output/playwright/phase5-qualification-editor-recovery.png`, `phase5-qualification-editor-saved.png`, `phase5-qualification-editor-keyboard.png` and `phase5-qualification-editor-mobile.png` were inspected; the recovery image records the pre-fix scroll defect. The desktop keyboard image includes an open date picker, so it is not evidence of complete date-picker dismissal behavior.

This checkpoint passes **60 PHP tests / 603 assertions**, including five fixture cases and shared validation/account-boundary tests; **502 frontend checks** pass, including the new containment regression. Production build passes in **17.28 seconds**, with existing chunk warnings; Pint and scoped whitespace checks pass. Test counts overlap the prior validation checkpoint. No schema, dependencies, existing passwords or existing account grants changed. One fresh fictional fixture set and its canonical local join/qualification evidence were created. Broader configuration workflows, complete keyboard/motion/physical-device coverage and the full PHP suite remain open. Local realtime remains unavailable; Phase 5 is not complete.

Procurement implementation: `ApproveInventoryNeed`, `RejectInventoryNeed` and `ConvertInventoryNeedToOrder` use transactional, freshly locked records and current operator authority. Only submitted needs accept a decision; all lines require explicit positive approved quantities; conversion never falls back to unapproved requested quantities. Repeated conversion cannot create a second order. Write vetoes roll back the whole operation; archived material references are rejected. Form Requests own input/operation validation, and the UI exposes validation errors and prevents overlapping submissions. A missing historical approver no longer crashes notification recipient collection after conversion. Focused regression passes **39 tests / 260 assertions** across workflow integrity, need flow and supplier assessment boundaries. Browser verification rejected an excessive `0.6` approval against requested `0.5`, accepted `0.25`, then created one purchase order for exactly `0.25` / AOA 25. Receiving remains unverified.

Receipt reporting follow-up: purchase receipt movements (`RECEIPT`) were omitted from incoming-stock totals and charts, and the ledger page used a different classification again. One model-owned direction definition now drives model accessors, report totals/trends/type counts and resource flags consumed by both Vue screens. Archived movement types retain their direction; missing or unknown types remain neutral. A regression reproduced the omission before repair. The report/ledger/consumption-read checkpoint passed **88 PHP tests / 827 assertions**; the final report-only run after ledger-resource assertions passed **62 tests / 556 assertions**. These counts overlap. **17 frontend checks** pass, including four-decimal signs and page counts for receipts, reversals, consumption and transfers. Browser inspection also exposed missing document titles on the two touched pages; explicit titles were added without changing the approved visual direction.

The browser also exposed a shared empty-state defect: the read-only ledger still offered record creation despite `createAction=false`. The table now applies the same permission/capability condition to both normal and empty-state creation controls. Final related frontend regression passes **54 checks**, including the expanded 18-check consumption/ledger file, billing archive, counter-analysis, collection accession and shared-account table coverage. Final production build passes in **16.80 seconds** with existing chunk warnings; Pint and source whitespace checks pass. Browser verification confirms both page titles, the empty movement report and the absence of a creation button in the empty read-only ledger. Screenshots `output/playwright/phase5-receipt-movement-report.png` and `phase5-receipt-ledger.png` were visually inspected. The demo lab has no movements, so receipt signs/counts are covered by automated tests, not claimed as a browser receiving test. The ledger's legacy breadcrumb/Administration navigation classification remains for the receiving UI pass. Keyboard/motion/responsive coverage, realtime delivery and the full PHP suite were not verified in this slice; the known broader frontend failures remain open.

Receiving integrity remains a finite open workflow: current receipt submission has no operation-level permission, no durable idempotency key, unchecked write vetoes and incomplete locked reference revalidation. A repeated partial receipt can add stock twice; its client pending flag also ends before the Inertia request completes, and an explicit zero receipt price falls back to the order price. No receiving mutation was changed in this reporting slice. Decisions are pending on using existing `edit_iorders` plus `add_occurrences` for optional nonconformity creation, and on quarantining nonconforming receipts. The next receiving action must retain structured receipt evidence, atomically update stock/ledger/order lines, reject stale or foreign references and leave retries unchanged. Quarantine is not assumed before approval. Browser receiving remains unverified.

Maintenance implementation: `SaveMaintenanceTask` revalidates the freshly locked task, references and current operator within one transaction. Canonical create/edit and individual completion delegate to the same guarded completion action as bulk completion. Equipment reassignment is applied before calibration dates are updated; write vetoes roll back metadata, completion and equipment together. Existing results (including zero) can be used for completion without resubmission, and identical retries leave dates and timestamps unchanged. Number, year, sequence and derived dates are server-owned. The category is part of the already-immutable issued sequence scope; attempts to replace it now produce validation errors rather than server errors. Missing supplier references are permitted for internal work by an additive nullable-column migration applied to both local databases; external execution still requires a supplier. No existing records were rewritten.

Canonical maintenance reads, writes, exports, category endpoints and notification triggers now have explicit ability middleware. The interface receives operation abilities, exposes real create/edit routes, and no longer fabricates a result through the list's individual completion button. The detail form permits result entry before completion, uses pending/error states and prints through the supported task-export type with a scoped single-task filter. Completion notices require completed work with evidence and filter recipients by task-view authority. Authoring uses an API Resource with date-only values; browser verification exposed and prevented a UTC serialization bug that otherwise moved a 3 October due date to 2 October during editing. Metadata forms no longer resend hidden completion/result fields. Equipment history follows updated props and aggregates the full lab-owned history independently from its 50-row preview.

Maintenance verification: **36 PHP tests / 264 assertions** pass across `MaintenanceAuthoringTest`, `MaintenanceModuleTest` and `CalibrationScheduleReportTest`; **eight frontend checks** pass. These replace the earlier overlapping nine-case checkpoint, not add to it. Coverage includes missing-result rejection, zero, unchanged retries, write-veto rollback, reassignment, absent metadata flags, forged identifiers, external-supplier requirements, malformed flags, archived references, peer labs, revoked membership, impersonation, explicit read/write/export abilities, selected-task export, date-only editing, and uncapped history totals. Browser verification confirmed invalid bulk/individual completion, result entry while pending, and successful completion advancing the due date exactly once from 2026-10-03 to 2027-10-03. A real Inertia v3 `useHttp` behavior was corrected: 422 responses resolve without throwing, so selection cleanup runs only in `onSuccess`. The final browser run also saved notes without changing the issued number, recorded result, completed state or the 3 October 2027 due date, and confirmed the equipment history updates to two completed tasks. The post-completion screenshot was inspected at `output/playwright/phase5-maintenance-completed.png`. The exact-state production build passes in **34.42 seconds**; Pint and source whitespace checks pass. Build-time stale asset requests were resolved by reloading after completion; the remaining current browser error is the already-known unavailable local realtime websocket. Print-file rendering, physical-device/responsive and full keyboard/motion coverage were not verified in this slice. Legacy task/category routes, import authorization/validation, atomic bulk reschedule/archive, report/export controls and the pending completed-evidence policy remain open; the maintenance module is not declared complete.

Pending maintenance policy decision: whether completed equipment/category/result and execution/calibration dates become immutable under ordinary editing, with notes still editable and corrections requiring a separately defined workflow. No new locking policy has been implemented while that decision is pending.

Maintenance lifecycle follow-up: `UpdateMaintenanceTaskLifecycle` checks current operator authority and locks the complete lab-scoped selection before any mutation. Archive/restore use separate permissions, preserve evidence, skip already-achieved states and roll back the complete batch on a write veto. Rescheduling delegates to canonical authoring inside that same transaction, recomputing recurrence rather than leaving stale next dates. Requests cap batches and pagination at 100, validate date-only schedules and allow-list sorting. Archived tasks have a scoped list and permissioned restoration. The list uses Inertia v3 `useHttp` for all bulk mutations, retains modal/selection on failure and clears action/date state before completion. Single-task archiving reuses the shared guarded archive composable. The unused reschedule-notification checkbox was removed; notification requests are rejected rather than silently ignored. Four duplicate task pages, their Form Request and their write/GET-mutation routes were removed. Read-only legacy bookmarks redirect only after permission and laboratory checks. Embedded equipment history links to canonical task authoring; archive/restore happen in the canonical task list.

Lifecycle verification checkpoint: **56 PHP tests / 389 assertions** pass across lifecycle, authoring, maintenance and calibration-report tests; **29 focused frontend checks** pass. Browser verification archived and restored demo task `CAL_INT 2026/002`, preserving its completed state and 3 October 2027 due date. A new pending demo task `CAL_INT 2026/003` was created through the canonical form and rescheduled from 3 to 17 October 2026; the modal/selection cleared only after success. The browser also exposed material records in the equipment selector: canonical choices, authoring validation/locked references and completion now require the explicit equipment classification. Regression tests reject forged material references while preserving lab boundaries. Equipment history serialization handles internal work without a supplier and no longer emits retired mutation links.

The broader design-system contract run produced **144 passes / 12 failures** across 156 combined checks. Those twelve failures concern inventory dossier/report/consumption, commercial authoring, sample detail, portal-request/customer directory and dispatch assertions outside the lifecycle changes; they remain unresolved and must be reconciled with the approved workflows before claiming broader frontend verification. No test files were removed. Two additional focused checks of the shared archive composable pass (duplicate submissions and network/HTTP/cancellation errors). Final production build passes in **1 minute 30 seconds**, with the existing large-chunk warnings; Pint and whitespace checks pass. The refreshed browser confirms named selection checkboxes, the 17 October reschedule and equipment-only choices. `output/playwright/phase5-maintenance-lifecycle.png` was captured and inspected; its viewport shows the pending task and completed history, while the inner scrolling layout clips the third row. The current remaining browser errors are the known local realtime connection refusal. Physical-device, full keyboard/motion and export rendering coverage remain unverified. Category/import/report paths, summary-card correctness and the completed-evidence policy remain open; this is not Phase 5 completion.

Maintenance import follow-up: CSV import now uses the same canonical authoring action, with a 500-row/2 MB UTF-8 limit and one all-or-nothing transaction. Equipment codes must resolve uniquely to active equipment in the current lab; categories, suppliers, calendar dates, recurrence and flags use shared validation. Issued identity, derived dates, laboratory ownership and completion state cannot be supplied by CSV. All imported tasks start pending. A retained immutable receipt binds the operation UUID to the lab, operator and file hash, making retries confirm the original result without duplicating tasks. Changed content requires a new operation; another lab or operator cannot replay the receipt. Tasks and receipt roll back together on write failure, and current operator authority is rechecked. The receipt migration was applied to the local development and isolated test PostgreSQL databases without rewriting existing records.

The unsafe queued import writer is retired with a fail-closed handler for already-serialized jobs. Its unscoped progress endpoints and four unused import/progress pages/components were removed. The new shared-theme import page gives real CSV requirements, a downloadable header, local equipment/reference values, persistent validation/network errors, pending feedback and an immediate duplicate-submit guard. Selecting a new file creates a new operation key; retries keep the original file/key. Better UI and Emil design guidance informed the clear requirements, accessible errors and honest pending state, not a new visual direction. Browser verification exposed a blank page title and incorrect Administration navigation placement; the importer now has a title, canonical task breadcrumbs and Inventory context.

Import checkpoint: **87 PHP tests / 537 assertions** pass across import, retired inventory jobs, lifecycle, authoring, maintenance and calibration-report coverage. After the final breadcrumb and invalid-row assertion changes, the import file passes **29 tests / 141 assertions**; these results overlap, not add. **16 maintenance frontend checks** pass, including retries after network/HTTP/cancellation failure, retained validation errors, fresh-file operation keys, compilation and navigation classification. Final build passes in **24.18 seconds**, with the existing large-chunk warnings; Pint and whitespace checks pass. In the browser, an invalid date produced a row-specific error while retaining the selected file and creating no task. A valid CSV created exactly one pending demo task, `CAL_INT 2026/004`, due 1 December 2026, and showed the confirmation toast. `output/playwright/phase5-maintenance-import.png` was captured and visually inspected. Realtime remains unverified because the local websocket is unavailable. The twelve broader frontend contract failures remain open; no full PHP suite was run at this focused checkpoint. This is progress within Phase 5, not phase completion.

Responsive verification follow-up: the 390 × 844 browser check exposed desktop navigation above the page because a direct-child selector skipped the sidebar's wrapper. An `!important` display utility also prevented the first corrected selector from hiding that wrapper. The sidebar now owns a semantic `display: contents` rule; only its desktop instance hides below 1024 px, while the mobile drawer remains available. The refreshed browser confirms a hidden desktop sidebar, an opening mobile drawer, and a 390 px document width with no horizontal overflow. `output/playwright/phase5-maintenance-import-mobile.png` was captured and inspected after repair. Current focused coverage is **17 maintenance frontend checks plus three shared-shell contract checks**, all passing. The final post-repair build passes in **28.10 seconds**, with the same existing chunk warnings; source whitespace checks pass. This narrow-screen browser check is not a physical-device or complete accessibility audit.

Maintenance reporting follow-up: list, dashboard, aggregate statistics and PDF/CSV/XLSX exports now share validated lab-scoped filters. Date-only deadlines use the server's calendar day, so today's tasks are not already overdue; zero-cost bounds work. Summary cards aggregate the full filtered dataset, independently of pagination, and the former fabricated monthly average is replaced by the real pending count due this month. Dashboard statistics explicitly describe the current state of tasks created during the selected month/three-month/twelve-month cohort; misleading historical completion trends were removed. Stale chart requests are cancelled, and failures no longer appear as zero-valued data. List resources narrow relation fields and provide date-only values and server-calculated days until due.

All retained report endpoints now return actual private, non-cacheable files through one action. Task exports reject more than 5,000 matching rows instead of truncating them, calendars are bounded to 366 days, spreadsheet strings are protected against formula interpretation, missing/archived references render safely, and PDFs identify the owning lab rather than the global lab setting. Unsupported category-summary/cost-analysis report types and nonfunctional column-selection controls were retired rather than pretending those options work. PDF, CSV and XLSX task/calendar exports remain supported; the dashboard's agenda shortcut explicitly downloads Excel. Download controls block duplicate submissions, show pending/error states and retain the modal after failure. Dashboard completion now uses the same existing-result requirement as the canonical list and never invents evidence.

Reporting checkpoint: **109 PHP tests / 754 assertions** pass across reporting, lifecycle, authoring, maintenance, calibration-report and import coverage. The final reporting-only rerun after PDF markup repairs passes **24 tests / 224 assertions**; these counts overlap. **25 focused frontend checks** pass, with a final eight-check reporting rerun after the last label change. Production build passes in **25.58 seconds**, with existing large-chunk warnings; Pint and source whitespace checks pass. Real-browser checks downloaded a task PDF and calendar XLSX, fetched the calendar PDF through the authorized endpoint, and rejected completion of demo task `CAL_INT 2026/004` because its result is missing, leaving it pending. Both single-page PDFs were rendered and visually inspected; renderer-specific summary spacing and subtitle contrast were repaired. Browser page titles are now present. `output/playwright/phase5-maintenance-reporting.png` was captured and inspected; the inner scrolling layout limits the screenshot to the visible portion, while dashboard chart/cost content was checked in the browser DOM. Full keyboard/slow-motion/physical-device coverage, multi-page report rendering, realtime delivery and the complete PHP suite were not verified in this slice. The twelve broader frontend contract failures remain open. This is a Phase 5 checkpoint, not phase completion.

Export failure/retry browser follow-up: a locally intercepted 422 response displayed its validation message while preserving the export panel and controls. The initial visibility assertion incorrectly targeted the zero-height dialog wrapper; the subsequent accessibility snapshot confirmed the visible panel, message and controls. After removing interception, retrying as CSV produced a real download and closed the panel only on success. No application data was changed by the simulated response.

Approved category policy: shared read-only presets plus lab-owned custom maintenance categories; codes become immutable after use, including archived tasks. The nullable lab-ownership migration was applied to the local development and isolated test databases. The development database contained only the existing `CAL_INT` preset, which remains shared; existing tasks and identifiers were not rewritten. New authoring assigns the active lab on the server. Owner changes and submitted owner/archive fields are rejected. Both legacy category writers and their GET archive/restore routes were retired in favor of one guarded controller and transactional save/archive actions; the old list bookmark redirects after authorization, and the retained lookup is lab-scoped. Task authoring/import options exclude peer categories. Archiving preserves existing task references and calibration behavior; new tasks cannot use archived categories. Category cards distinguish presets from private entries, show full filtered counts, expose only authorized actions and support reversible archive/restore. Used codes are visibly read-only, and errors/pending submissions retain form state.

Category verification checkpoint: **207 PHP tests / 1,254 assertions** pass across category ownership, authoring, import, lifecycle, reporting, maintenance, calibration reporting, application smoke and combobox coverage. The final category-only rerun after breadcrumb/pagination repairs passes **9 tests / 144 assertions**; counts overlap. **29 focused frontend checks** pass, with a final four-check category rerun after the last repairs. The final production build passes in **19.00 seconds**, with existing large-chunk warnings; Pint and source whitespace checks pass. Regression fixture corrections preserve archived category names and give the customer-request combobox test the lab membership now required by the approved privacy policy; application authorization was not weakened.

Real-browser category verification rejected an invalid code while retaining all entered values, created private demo category `DEMO_PJ6_MAINT`, saved its description, archived it, restored it and confirmed the original shared preset exposes no write controls. The browser revealed and verified repairs for missing pagination totals and untranslated breadcrumbs. Desktop and 390px screenshots were captured and inspected at `output/playwright/phase5-maintenance-categories.png` and `output/playwright/phase5-maintenance-categories-mobile.png`; the narrow viewport has no horizontal overflow. Used-code locking, peer access, revoked authority, retry safety and rollback are covered by automated tests, not claimed as additional browser checks. Physical-device/full keyboard and motion coverage, live realtime delivery and the complete PHP suite remain unverified here. The local websocket is still unavailable, and the twelve broader frontend contract failures remain open. This is a Phase 5 checkpoint, not phase completion.

Quality evidence: **12 tests / 132 assertions** pass for ratings/nonconformity, and **6 tests / 53 assertions** pass for the QMS laboratory boundary. Omitted corrective-action input now preserves actions; dashboard totals count the full authorized dataset while previews remain capped at 12.

Approved quality-history policy: corrective actions removed from the editor are archived rather than deleted; nonconformity removal is also archive/restore, preserving the dossier and private attachments. Additive soft-delete migrations were applied to development and isolated test databases without rewriting existing records. `SaveNonConformityRequest` owns bounded authoring validation, including an explicit allow-list of nested corrective-action fields and distinct IDs. `SaveNonConformity` locks the owning record, rechecks current operator authority inside the transaction, rejects foreign or archived action IDs and rolls back parent/action changes when a model vetoes a write. Submitted ownership, approval or effectiveness fields cannot be smuggled through nested action input. Omitted action input remains unchanged; an explicit multipart presence marker makes an empty submitted list archive the final action correctly.

`ArchiveNonConformity` provides separate delete/restore permission checks, lab-scoped locking, impersonation rejection and retry-safe transitions. Parent archive does not change child archive states, and restoration does not reactivate previously removed corrective work. Archived dossiers and attachments remain available through lab-private read routes; ordinary editing is disabled until restoration. The detail view labels archived corrective work, while the editor submits only active rows and editable fields. Shared archive confirmation/pending/error handling replaces unguarded deletion. An API Resource preserves local date/time values for editing; browser inspection found the previous UTC slicing could turn 21:11 into 20:11. Page titles and pagination metadata were also repaired without changing the approved visual direction.

Quality authoring checkpoint: **41 PHP tests / 532 assertions** pass across authoring, ratings/nonconformity, laboratory boundaries, QMS, quality-record boundaries and demo fixtures. **Seven frontend checks** pass for Vue compilation, action projection, multipart semantics, permission gates and archive feedback. Production build passes in **20.66 seconds**; Pint and source whitespace checks pass. Browser verification added a corrective action to the demo dossier, saved and reviewed it, removed it from the active plan, confirmed its preserved archived history, archived the dossier, opened it from the archive and restored it. Report time remained 03/10/2026 21:11. `output/playwright/phase5-quality-action-history.png` was captured and inspected. A stale dynamic-import failure during a build was resolved by loading the completed build; remaining current console errors are the known unavailable realtime websocket.

Quality evidence follow-up: attachment persistence now runs inside the guarded authoring transaction. Generated private UUID directories isolate new uploads; media ownership, size and SHA-256 are checked before saving succeeds. Rollback callbacks clean only those new directories, including on an outer transaction rollback; existing attachments remain untouched. Historical media retain their original path convention. Storage-cleanup failures are reported and can still require operational orphan cleanup if storage itself is unavailable. The editor retains selected files and entered values after validation, HTTP, network or cancellation failures, blocks overlapping edits while saving and tells users to confirm dossier state before retrying an uncertain network outcome.

Evidence checkpoint: **38 PHP tests / 372 assertions** pass across evidence integrity, authoring, lab boundaries, module coverage and PHP source compatibility; **eight frontend checks** pass. Two additional catalogue media-path regression cases pass with 14 assertions. Production build passes in **17.36 seconds**; Pint and source whitespace checks pass. Coverage includes a second upload failing after copy, successful retry, media-write veto, revoked authority, corrupted stored bytes and nested rollback. In the browser, a one-shot simulated 409 kept the selected file and displayed a retry message; the real retry saved one private attachment. The private download returned 200 and its SHA-256 matched the original 168-byte fixture. Browser verification exposed a pre-existing leading space before the PHP tag in `config/broadcasting.php`, which prefixed download output and truncated the final byte under Content-Length. That whitespace was removed and configuration-source output now has a regression guard. The initial Node request could not trust the local Herd certificate; download verification used the already-trusted browser instead. `output/playwright/phase5-quality-evidence.png` was captured and inspected. This browser failure simulation did not exercise backend rollback; automated tests cover that separately.

Quality reporting follow-up: one validated query now owns the lab-private cohort for list, summaries, charts and PDF/XLSX registers. Search is case-insensitive and treats percent/underscore literally; report-date bounds, status, severity, category and archive selection apply consistently before pagination. Summary counts cover all matches, and the attention count no longer counts the same dossier three times. The six-month trend shows actual report dates only; the former resolution series depended on timestamps authoring does not currently record and was removed rather than invented. The screen explicitly identifies the active/archive and filter scope. Exports use the accepted server filters, private/no-store responses and server-generated filenames, and reject more than 5,000 rows rather than truncating.

Archived dossiers export through authorized detail routes. PDF and Excel retain archived corrective actions with archive labels, zero-valued text and distinct unknown/false effectiveness. PDF includes global corrective work and action evidence. The Excel action sheet now maps each action once; its formatting no longer overwrites data or drops empty/zero values. Explicit string binding keeps formula-like input as text. Existing download feedback is reused for progress, duplicate-submit prevention and persistent failure/retry messages. Better UI and Emil guidance informed the explicit scope and honest feedback without a new visual direction; Inertia filters retain values and expose validation/network errors. Full keyboard, motion and physical-device checks were not performed.

Reporting checkpoint: **41 PHP tests / 436 assertions** pass across reporting, authoring, evidence, laboratory boundaries and module regression. The final reporting-only rerun passes **7 tests / 115 assertions**, adding coverage for false effectiveness, zero-valued narratives and landscape configuration; these counts overlap rather than add. **18 frontend checks** pass, including shared download failure/retry and quality filter/export contracts. Production build passes in **17.00 seconds**, with existing chunk warnings; Pint and source whitespace checks pass. Browser verification downloaded real dossier PDF/Excel files, simulated one export 422 then retried successfully, archived the demo dossier, searched the archive, downloaded its filtered register and archived dossier, then restored it. An initial browser search attempt raced archive navigation; the stable-page retry passed. The register PDF was widened to landscape after render inspection. The final register and two-page dossier PDFs were rendered and all pages visually inspected; the retained archived action appears in both formats. Evidence lives at `output/playwright/phase5-quality-reporting.png`, `phase5-quality-register.pdf`, `phase5-quality-details.pdf` and `phase5-quality-details.xlsx`. These are local fictional fixtures, not issued laboratory reports.

Approved quality lifecycle implemented: resolution requires a nonblank evidence narrative, followed by separately permissioned verification and explicit closure. The user confirmed that a separate verification permission is sufficient; the resolver may also verify if authorized. Four narrow permissions (`resolve_non_conformities`, `verify_non_conformities`, `close_non_conformities`, `reopen_non_conformities`) are seeded without assigning existing editor roles. The existing system-administrator gate remains unchanged. The additive lifecycle migration was applied to development and isolated-test PostgreSQL databases without fabricating historical evidence or timestamps.

`TransitionNonConformity` locks the owning lab/dossier, checks current authority before and after writing, validates the expected revision and binds retries to an operation UUID, actor, transition and evidence. Resolution, verification and closure cannot be forged through ordinary authoring. Server-stamped transition history and dossier/action snapshots are retained atomically on the guarded parent record; browser resources expose only history summaries, not bulky snapshots or replay identifiers. Reopening requires a reason, clears current-cycle timestamps/evidence and retains prior cycles. Existing resolved records without new-flow evidence must be reopened and documented; existing closed records remain locked. Closed editing accepts only observations, including rejection of empty forged core/action/file fields. Changes to resolved core data, actions or attachments invalidate verification; comments alone do not. Archive/restore preserve lifecycle state and history.

Lifecycle notices use distinct operation IDs rather than minute-based deduplication. Recipients must be active, verified, current members of the owning lab with `view_occurrences`; queued delivery rechecks access. Notifications run after transaction commit. A notification failure is reported without returning a false transition failure, releases its deduplication key, and an identical transition retry may reattempt delivery without repeating the transition. External mail/broadcast delivery and durable retry after a notification-enqueue outage were not verified; the existing queue infrastructure remains responsible for successfully queued delivery.

Lifecycle checkpoint: **61 PHP tests / 664 assertions** passed across lifecycle, authoring, evidence, reports, lab boundaries, module regression and operational notification contracts. **13 frontend checks**, production build (**16.76 seconds**), Pint and source whitespace checks passed. These counts overlap prior checkpoints. The browser completed resolve → verify → close, saved observations through a closed form exposing exactly one editable field, and reopened with a recorded reason while retaining previous evidence. A simulated HTTP 409 preserved the narrative and displayed recovery guidance before a successful retry. Screenshots `output/playwright/phase5-quality-lifecycle-closed.png` and `phase5-quality-lifecycle-reopened.png` were inspected. Better UI and Emil guidance informed explicit stage controls, persistent errors and restrained reuse of existing design tokens; no visual-direction change was introduced. The final history-summary resource guard was verified separately after this browser run.

Lifecycle reporting follow-up: dossier PDF and Excel exports now include current-cycle resolution, verification and closure evidence plus chronological transition history through one allow-listed presenter. Reopening does not erase earlier cycles from exports. Missing evidence is explicitly identified rather than inferred, zero-valued evidence is retained, spreadsheet narratives remain literal strings, and replay identifiers and private snapshots are omitted. The real browser downloaded both files. All three PDF pages were rendered and inspected; split history entries, an orphaned section heading and missing later-page footers were corrected. History now begins on its own page. The combined reporting/lifecycle checkpoint passed **24 PHP tests / 306 assertions**; the final reporting-only run after the last layout assertions passed **9 tests / 154 assertions**. Counts overlap. Pint passed. No frontend source changed in this export slice, so no additional Vite build was required. Rendered evidence: `output/pdf/phase5-nonconformity-lifecycle.pdf`; browser workbook: `output/playwright/phase5-nonconformity-lifecycle.xlsx`.

Quality remains open: count-based number suggestions and manual numbering need concurrency repair; automatic numbering format approval is pending. Assignment semantics need implementation/review. Full browser creation/assignment, responsive/keyboard/motion coverage and the full PHP suite were not verified in this slice. The twelve broader frontend contract failures also remain unresolved. Local realtime remains unavailable. This checkpoint does not declare the quality module or Phase 5 complete.

Demo/browser access: `app:demo-supporting-modules` creates fresh local-only staff, read-only reviewer and portal accounts, two isolated demo labs, fractional inventory, a purchase need, calibration tasks and a nonconformity. Existing accounts/passwords and role permissions are unchanged. Both demo labs use database-only notification overrides. **3 tests / 153 assertions** verify fixtures and real logins. Generated local credentials are kept outside the repository, not in this plan.

Portal implementation: `IssuePortalServiceInvitation` and `SubmitPortalServiceRequest` use database transactions and locked authority/recipient/invitation records. Submission does not use a time-based cache duplicate guard. Staff lists, lookups, exports, archive/restore and sample-intake references are lab-scoped; mixed-scope bulk mutations roll back. Portal history and mutations also require the original customer/site identity. Completion is retry-safe and cancellation rolls back if archiving fails. Archive, restore, portal completion and cancellation no longer mutate through GET. Browser verification covered issuance, actual portal login, failed validation retaining input/invitation, successful submission, consumed-invitation empty state, and the owning lab's private queue. Browser-discovered resource-wrapping and form-label defects were repaired. Evidence: `output/playwright/phase5-portal-staff-queue.png`, `phase5-portal-request.png`, and `phase5-portal-invitation-consumed.png`.

Portal checkpoints: **202 tests / 1,494 assertions** passed across invitation, portal service, intake creation/correction and concurrency coverage. A later targeted **40 tests / 395 assertions** passed additional ownership/lifecycle checks, export/smoke coverage and the intake smoke case; these checkpoints overlap and are not additive. Final portal-specific rerun: **26 tests / 225 assertions**. An intermediate rerun collided with Vite removing its manifest; it was rerun successfully after the build completed. Current procurement/maintenance/portal frontend checks: **13 passed**. Production build and Pint pass; pre-existing large-chunk warnings remain. Local realtime websocket service is unavailable, so live broadcast delivery is not verified. No full-suite run was requested at this checkpoint.

Composer inventory checkpoint: the HighSolutions sequence package is already absent; `ScopedSequenceAllocator` / `HasScopedSequence` provide internal numbering. Keep Laravel/Inertia/Ziggy foundations and established Spatie packages. PDF/QR/document packages are not treated as interchangeable: label/report rendering uses Spatie PDF's Chrome driver (`chrome-php/chrome`), mPDF remains in existing document paths and requires `mpdf/qrcode`, while Endroid QR has direct application callers. Keep spreadsheet/CSV/Word parsers, nested-set folder behavior, number-to-words, notification channel adapters, Redis client, user-agent parsing and OAuth adapters pending their retained workflow checks. `socialiteproviders/microsoft-azure` is a possible redundant dependency: configured routes currently allow `microsoft`, not `microsoft-azure`; removal requires a complete registration/authentication check and user approval. No dependency was added or removed in Phase 5, and no risky in-house replacement is proposed merely to reduce package count.

Portal-document privacy checkpoint: `PortalDocumentAccess` now applies the current customer/site pair to all six document libraries and PDF endpoints, independent of a staff session's active lab. Document dashboard counts and outstanding invoice amounts use the same customer boundary. Certificate search is logically grouped, preventing laboratory-code matches from escaping ownership and release predicates. Both certificate listing and download require validation, an unarchived paid invoice, and coherent customer/site/lab links through the collection, laboratory code and active sample entry. Downloads additionally reuse the shared-delivery result/sample/counter-analysis integrity check before constructing the report payload; the staff sharing permission is not applied to portal actors.

Credit-note, quote and receipt invoice links must match their owning lab/customer/site; receipt allocations are checked individually before list totals or PDFs can expose them. Existing composite foreign keys remain intact. Archived document roots are absent from both libraries and downloads, including crafted `filter=trashed` requests. Already-owned archived invoice references remain available within retained credit notes and receipts, without permitting standalone download of the archived invoice. Libraries use stable descending identity order. `PortalDocumentResource` exposes only the fields consumed by each portal library, excluding nested account/site records, staff action links, file paths, signatures, recipient lists and arbitrary metadata. No customer records, issued documents, credentials, schema or dependencies were changed by this slice.

Final verification: **79 PHP tests / 1,253 assertions** pass across portal boundaries, portal smoke/services and shared-delivery boundary/real-commit tests, in **74.82 seconds**. Valid PDF rendering for all six families, absent verification/payment, mismatched customer/site/source links, foreign result lineage, root archiving, retained archived invoice references and safe list payloads are covered. Early fixture errors exposed required month columns and existing composite/unique constraints; fixtures were corrected without weakening those constraints. The selected portal-library frontend contract passes **one check**; Pint, PHP syntax, scoped whitespace checks and read-only Laravel review pass. No frontend source changed, so no Vite build was required. Browser document workflows, full-suite execution and broader Phase 5 module requirements remain open; this is not portal or phase completion.

Portal browser checkpoint: `app:demo-portal-documents` creates fresh, environment-guarded, transactional local fixtures and a portal account without resetting existing credentials, changing global settings, sending notifications, allocating issued sequences or signing fictional financial documents. Five fixture tests cover real login, all six PDF renderers, hidden records, repeat setup, rollback and production refusal. The fixture exposed a contract-guide crash when optional country was absent; optional product/country/manufacturer/brand/lot cells now use null-safe, escaped output, including an HTML-injection regression.

Final focused regression passes **54 PHP tests / 913 assertions** in **20.22 seconds**. Four portal-shell frontend checks and the selected portal design-contract check pass; production build passes in **20.46 seconds**, with existing large-chunk warnings. Pint, scoped whitespace checks and read-only Laravel review pass. Better UI/Emil guidance informed a shared navigation repair, route-specific page titles, resource-aware account identity and named mobile controls. A browser-discovered 393 px header overflow at 320 px is fixed with responsive branding and spacing; the accessible brand text is retained. The final document width is 320 px, all 13 mobile links are available, Escape closes navigation and focus returns to its trigger. The initial dialog visibility timeout targeted its zero-height wrapper; the fixed-position panel itself was visible and operable.

The real browser visited all six document libraries, each containing one authorized fictional document, and obtained six 200 responses with PDF signatures. Certificate no-match search and clear recovery pass. Direct requests for the same-customer/foreign-site invoice and unreleased certificate return 404. Desktop/mobile/library-menu screenshots were inspected at `output/playwright/phase5-portal-documents-desktop.png`, `phase5-portal-documents-mobile.png` and `phase5-portal-mobile-navigation.png`. Browser fetch was used after the Node request context rejected the local Herd certificate; TLS verification was not disabled. All three pages of `output/pdf/phase5-portal-contract-guide.pdf` were rendered and inspected: the repaired optional-country cell renders, but pre-existing blank identity fields, excessive pagination, broken logo fallback and unsupported hard-coded status statements remain. This PDF is not approved for release. Full document transport recovery, full accessibility/device/theme coverage, the complete PHP suite and remaining Phase 5 modules remain open.

Contract-guide rendering follow-up: the shared staff/portal template now uses `customer.name` and the actual site name instead of nonexistent `customer.company`. Separately saved NIF/contact/email/collection-point fields remain document values; missing values are labeled rather than replaced with current directory data. B/L and internal reference are displayed separately. Observations are included with escaped text and retained line breaks; all active document fields are escaped. Unsupported automatic active-client, one-year-validity, complete-documentation and system-certification claims are removed. Existing legal citation, 15-day promise and director sign-off remain unchanged pending the user's separate policy decision. Staff/portal PDF titles and activity descriptions now correctly identify a contract guide; a missing historical author is tolerated.

PDF layout is simplified to a normal first-page letterhead, flowing identity/reference/product content, a separate identified terms/signature page and guide-reference/page-number footers. The redundant product page break, 105 mm top reservation and broken logo watermark are removed. Attempts to retain named repeated headers produced inconsistent first/later-page output under mPDF and were replaced with the simpler structure rather than accepted on source assertions alone. Both final pages of `output/pdf/phase5-portal-contract-guide.pdf` were rendered and inspected (`phase5-guide-flow-1.png` and `phase5-guide-flow-2.png`). The representative one-item fixture is now two readable pages, not three. Many-item/long-narrative and configured-logo variants remain to verify. No saved guide records, schema, dependencies or global settings were changed. Contract-guide lab ownership is not yet defined in the model and has been raised for an explicit decision; this checkpoint does not declare the guide workflow release-ready.

Contract-guide focused verification passes **17 PHP tests / 374 assertions** in **9.02 seconds**, across demo rendering, portal smoke and guide workflows. Pint, scoped whitespace checks and read-only Laravel review pass. No Vue source changed in this slice, so no Vite build was needed. The complete PHP suite was not run. Phase 5 remains active.

Procurement receiving recovery checkpoint: the receipt form now retains its pending guard through Inertia completion, preserves explicit zero prices, keeps drafts on validation/HTTP/network/cancellation failures, and avoids a redundant reload after successful redirect. Server eligibility and unexpected persistence failures return field errors rather than an unshared session flash; unexpected failures are reported and stock/ledger/line writes roll back. Browser verification used only the authorized fictional need 3/order 2 in demo lab 8: approval/conversion, transition to ordered, a locally intercepted HTTP 503 with retained quantity/zero price/reason, and one real partial receipt of 0.1250 against 0.5000. The resulting page shows 0.3750 remaining and 25% completion. No supplier message was sent. Recovery and successful-result screenshots are `output/playwright/phase5-receipt-http-recovery.png` and `output/playwright/phase5-receipt-partial-success.png`.

The browser also exposed an omitted warehouse relation in the order editor. A minimal, lab-scoped eager load restores the saved destination label without changing the destination. Regression coverage checks the label and exclusion of foreign-lab warehouse options; an initial invalid cross-lab test fixture was rejected by the existing composite foreign key and was corrected without weakening the constraint. Final focused verification passes **20 PHP tests / 183 assertions** across purchase-order integrity, procurement ownership and order display, plus **six frontend checks**. Pint, scoped whitespace checks and read-only Laravel review pass. The receipt frontend had already passed its production build before browser verification; the warehouse follow-up changes only PHP. Durable receipt idempotency, fresh locked authority/reference checks, nonconformity-operation permission and quarantine policy remain open. This is not procurement or Phase 5 completion; the complete PHP suite was not rerun.

- Triage existing quality/nonconformity, equipment/calibration, purchasing/inventory, commercial documents, portal, notification, and administration flows. Agree what is required for the first release; do not silently remove unfinished modules.
- Repair each retained module's schema and workflow before applying the shared UI. Prefer coherent task flows over reproducing every legacy screen or modal.
- Consolidate duplicated validation, serializers, filters, and state transitions as modules are touched. Preserve audit and financial history.
- Inventory Composer dependencies. Propose replacements only where internal ownership is cheaper and safer; dependency additions/removals require approval. Keep established Spatie functionality unless a concrete issue warrants review.

Receiving persistence follow-up: explicit model-event vetoes now fail the transaction rather than returning false success. Guards cover newly created stock and transaction types, ledger creation, stock increment, receipt-line/status writes, purchase-price updates, order status and optional receiving nonconformity creation. Seven fault-injection cases verify that a rejected write preserves the original order, line, item price and stock, with no retained ledger or nonconformity rows. A separate zero-price test confirms the item purchase price and ledger narrative retain zero without rewriting the agreed order-line price. The focused three-file regression passes **28 tests / 265 assertions**; Pint, scoped whitespace checks and read-only Laravel review pass. No frontend, schema or dependency change was made, and no additional demo receipt was submitted. Durable idempotency and current locked authority/reference validation remain required; permission and quarantine decisions remain pending. This checkpoint does not establish complete receiving safety or Phase 5 completion.

Receiving reference follow-up: a failing regression demonstrated that an archived warehouse could still receive stock. Receiving now reloads the order within its lab, locks all selected order lines, then locks unique live same-lab materials and warehouses in ID order and validates unit availability before attempting stock writes. Archived references and missing units return recoverable field errors. A mixed-line test confirms an unavailable second destination prevents even the first stock-create attempt. The existing display/receipt fixture was corrected to supply a unit rather than weakening the established catalogue requirement. Final four-file procurement/supplier regression passes **39 tests / 333 assertions** in **9.29 seconds**, with Pint, scoped whitespace and read-only Laravel review passing. No schema/frontend changes or further demo mutations were required. These tests do not establish independent-connection concurrency behavior; durable replay handling and current operator-authority checks remain open, alongside pending receiving policy decisions.

Receiving error-boundary follow-up: unexpected PHP errors now share the receipt rollback/report/recoverable-error path with ordinary exceptions, while validation errors retain their separate handling. Fault injection covers `RuntimeException` and `TypeError` and verifies the transaction level returns to its pre-request value with line, stock and ledger unchanged. The final purchase-order integrity file passes **27 tests / 224 assertions** in **4.44 seconds**; formatting, whitespace and narrow read-only review pass. Receiving still has no explicit operation gate in its route/controller; the previously raised permission decision remains unresolved and no new authority or grants were assumed. Phase 5 remains open.

Receiving replay checkpoint: new receipt submissions require a UUID. A hidden, non-mass-assignable JSON history on the locked owning order binds each UUID to the validated payload fingerprint and actor. Matching retries return without stock/ledger/nonconformity writes, including after full receipt; reuse with changed data is rejected. Replay evidence commits atomically with receipt effects, and a veto of the final history save rolls back the complete receipt. The browser retains its UUID while retrying an open modal; closing/reopening starts a new attempt, so the existing warning to inspect uncertain receipt state remains necessary. Existing historical receipts were not backfilled or reinterpreted.

The additive migration was applied to development and isolated-test PostgreSQL using their respective configured credentials. Focused post-format verification passes **43 PHP tests / 373 assertions**, **six frontend checks**, production build (**18.25 seconds**, existing chunk warnings), whitespace checks and read-only Laravel review. The browser submitted a second fictional 0.1250 receipt for demo order 2, then resent its identical UUID/payload; after reload the received total stayed 0.2500 and the remainder stayed 0.2500. Screenshot `output/playwright/phase5-receipt-replay.png` was inspected. No supplier messages or account grants changed. Independent-connection contention, migration rollback and broader browser failure variants remain unverified. Current operator-authority enforcement and permission/quarantine decisions remain open, so this does not close procurement or Phase 5.

Replay evidence follow-up: regression now proves a second actor cannot claim the original request UUID and retrying a receipt with nonconformity registration does not create another dossier or movement. The receipt-history migration can reverse an unused column while preserving existing order fields, but refuses removal once any history is stored, including on archived orders. Its guard and column removal share a transaction and PostgreSQL exclusive table lock. Round-trip and refusal tests run inside the isolated test transaction; development history was not rolled back. The final integrity file passes **33 tests / 272 assertions** after formatting; source whitespace and read-only Laravel review pass. Independent-connection receipt contention and the unresolved authority/policy gates remain open.

Receiving concurrency checkpoint: four native PostgreSQL cases use independent PHP processes and disposable test schemas. Each worker is observed waiting on the held order row in `pg_stat_activity` before release. Simultaneous identical partial and complete receipts retain one history entry and one stock movement; distinct competing receipts cannot exceed the ordered quantity; a destination archived while receiving waits is rejected without stock, ledger or replay evidence. These checks exercise the controller/database transaction, not HTTP middleware or every possible inventory race. No additional development receipt, account grant or supplier notification was created.

Final focused regression passes **65 PHP tests / 595 assertions** in **25.97 seconds**, covering native receipt contention, purchase-order integrity, procurement ownership, order display, supplier assessment and the unchanged nonconformity lifecycle. The user's separate-permission approval is retained: one authorized operator may resolve, verify and close; no two-person rule was added. Pint and narrow read-only Laravel review pass. No application/frontend source, schema or dependency changed in this checkpoint, so no Vite rebuild was required. Current operator-authority enforcement and receiving permission/quarantine decisions remain open; the complete PHP suite and remaining Phase 5 module gates are not claimed as verified.

Commercial quote checkpoint: the existing twelve-file financial regression passed **218 PHP cases / 2,727 assertions** in **147.72 seconds** before the picker changes. The final affected PHP regression passes **96 cases / 679 assertions** in **24.96 seconds**, across `WarehouseLookupTest`, `QuoteAuthoringTest`, `CommercialQuotePickerTest` and `BillingSourceLaboratoryOwnershipTest`. The first attempted affected command named a nonexistent customer-directory test and ran no cases; that command was corrected, not counted as verification. Final frontend verification passes **527 checks** in **4.05 seconds**, including actual Vue compilation and behavioral failure/retry/stale-response checks. Production build passes in **19.31 seconds** with existing large-chunk warnings; Pint, scoped whitespace checks and narrow read-only Laravel review pass. No schema, dependency, account-permission or production changes were made.

The customer-site lookup previously returned every warehouse column, including authentication/private contact fields, and malformed `customer_id=undefined` caused a PostgreSQL error. A validated request and explicit five-field API Resource now return only ID, customer ID, name, address and code. Missing customer selection returns an empty array without a database lookup; malformed IDs/search shapes receive 422. Search is customer-scoped and excludes archived sites/customers. Existing plain-array callers remain compatible. New quote site options use scoped Inertia v3 `useHttp` instances, cancel obsolete requests, discard stale completion, settle loading, retain visible errors and recover on retry. A missing address uses the actual site name/code rather than invented address data. Saved quote editing retains the selected site label.

Real browser verification used the existing authorized fictional staff/lab fixtures only. It reproduced a locally intercepted site-lookup 503, retained the selected customer, showed persistent feedback and recovered through search. A locally intercepted quote-save 503 retained site, quantity, agreed price and observations. Browser testing then found and repaired two retry defects: the native Inertia error overlay was not suppressed, and the confirmation flag stayed mounted after confirmation, preventing reopening. The corrected failure → confirmation retry → real save created fictional quote ID 2, `PP 2026/1`, with quantity 2.50, agreed unit price 10.00, 10% discount and total AOA 22.50. Canonical editing changed the agreed price to 12.00; reload and the saved detail confirm total AOA 27.00. No invoice was issued, supplier/customer message sent, key changed or existing password reset. This demonstrates local draft persistence, not fiscal-valid issuance.

Browser-discovered detail defects are also repaired: missing-address sites have a retained-identity fallback; billing state derives from the actual invoice link/conversion state instead of displaying boolean `false` or invented proposal approval states; permissioned editing is available without requiring nonexistent string statuses. Create/Edit/Index/Show have meaningful page titles. Enhanced pickers now associate real Headless UI labels and disable the entire interaction, including the arrow button, when unavailable. The existing theme, density and surfaces remain; this is workflow repair, not a new visual direction.

| Severity | Location | Before | After | Why |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| HIGH, repaired | `resources/js/Composables/useQuoteAuthoring.js:90`; `resources/js/Pages/Quotes/Create.vue:247` | Save failure produced a default overlay; later confirmation could not reopen | Local persistent feedback suppresses default error behavior; confirmation state resets before dispatch | Recoverable task state preserves the draft and enables a real retry |
| MEDIUM, repaired | `resources/js/Components/combobox-enhanced.vue:152` | Arrow remained interactive when input was disabled; input inherited arrow naming | Whole picker is disabled; field label is associated through Headless UI | Capability-matched controls and clear assistive labels |
| MEDIUM, repaired | `resources/js/Pages/Quotes/Show.vue:28` and `:110` | Literal boolean status, blank site and hidden authorized edit link | Actual billing state, retained site identity and permission-based editor link | Truthful state and identity feedback |

better-ui and emil-design-eng informed these visible, recoverable interaction states and accurate labels. Screenshots inspected: `output/playwright/phase5-quote-site-recovery.png`, `phase5-quote-save-recovery.png`, `phase5-quote-saved.png`, `phase5-quote-saved-mobile.png` and `phase5-quote-saved-dark.png`. The 390-pixel saved-detail viewport has no document-level horizontal overflow. Approve only the inspected quote creation/recovery/revision and label contracts. Complete mobile editor controls, full keyboard/focus recovery, hover/active/loading/empty coverage for every commercial control, measured theme contrast, physical devices, 10%-speed motion inspection and screenshot/spec matching are **Not verified**. The dark saved-detail table retains existing muted text/surface contrast that needs the broader commercial UI check. Issued financial-document observation edits, invoice/payment browser flows, trade-certificate browser checks and the complete PHP suite remain open. The unavailable local realtime websocket and intentionally intercepted 503 console entries are not claimed fixed. This checkpoint does not complete commercial release acceptance or Phase 5.

Exit: all seven representative workflows have retained happy-path, permission/failure and browser evidence; P5-C1–P5-C5 pass on the final source state. Hand back a concise Phase 5 completion report and explicit Phase 6 release holds, then stop. This is not production release approval, and Phase 6 does not begin automatically.

## Phase 6 — Release readiness

- Run the complete PHP suite, frontend tests, production build, and representative browser workflows. Resolve failures; review each skip explicitly.
- Verify tenant isolation, public sharing, authentication-related boundaries, upload/export access, and background processing across the retained modules.
- Measure representative query counts and response times with realistic data. Agree performance budgets from measured baselines rather than inventing targets.
- Rehearse PostgreSQL backup/restore, migrations, rollback/recovery, and application initialization on an isolated environment. Verify configuration and secret handling without disclosing credentials.
- Confirm administrator password setup, deployment target, operational ownership, and release scope with the user.

Exit: no unresolved critical data-integrity, authorization, or workflow defect; recovery rehearsal passes; the user approves deployment. This plan does not authorize production deployment or deletion of old databases/backups.

## Working cadence and remaining Phase 1 work

The frozen contract and unique B1–B4 register above are authoritative. Historical broader audit suggestions are deferred, not additional exit gates. Implement and verify those finite blockers, reconcile the named acceptance matrix, run the final exact-state complete checkpoint, publish normally to main only after gates 1–6 pass, verify remote SHA equality, and stop. Preserve completed evidence and existing changes.

Seek approval only for a consequential policy with no justified recommendation, personal approval, destructive actions beyond the approved contract, dependencies or new external communication. Routine reversible implementation, the complete checkpoint and normal main publication are already authorized. No Phase 2 work or preparation.
